# PONCHO: Siete Vidas — Game Design Document

Version: 0.10.0

This document is the anchor for every feature. Rules:

- Each rule is a section `### GDD-XXX-NN` with a `Status:` line.
- **Decided** = the user said so. **Proposed** = a recommendation that is not binding until the user confirms it. **Deprecated** = removed, kept so IDs are never reused.
- Proposals are listed in [Open Questions](#open-questions) so they can be confirmed or changed.
- Numbers use *game pixels*, unless stated otherwise: the screen is 480 x 270 game pixels, and each game pixel is drawn as 4 x 4 art pixels at the 1920 x 1080 art resolution (GDD-ART-01).
- Process for editing: `.claude/skills/harness-gdd-update/SKILL.md`.

## Changelog

- **0.10.0** (2026-11-04) GDD-WLD-10, GDD-WLD-11, GDD-WLD-12, GDD-WLD-13, GDD-WLD-14, GDD-ENM-03, GDD-BOS-03, GDD-LOOP-03, GDD-LIFE-03, GDD-LIFE-04, GDD-LIFE-05, GDD-MOV-07, GDD-MOV-08, GDD-CMB-01, GDD-CMB-02, GDD-CMB-03, GDD-PWR-03, GDD-WLD-01, GDD-WLD-03, GDD-WLD-04, GDD-WLD-05, GDD-WLD-07, GDD-ENM-01, GDD-BOS-01, GDD-PRG-01, GDD-PRG-02, GDD-PRG-03, GDD-UI-01, GDD-UI-02, GDD-UI-05, GDD-AUD-01, GDD-AUD-02, GDD-ART-06, GDD-TEC-04, GDD-MVP-02, GDD-TEC-07: World 2, Mercado, is designed, none of it built yet and all of it Proposed (user, 2026-11-04: "Design first, then build"; "Draft from GDD mood"). New: GDD-WLD-10 bouncy awnings (a high bounce of 520 px/s while jump is held or buffered, a low one of 340 otherwise, cut by releasing jump; Poncho never rests on one), GDD-WLD-11 papel picado strings (one-way cords that sag under him), GDD-WLD-12 to GDD-WLD-14 the levels 2-1 "Los toldos", 2-2 "Los cordeles" and 2-3 "Las azoteas", GDD-ENM-03 World 2's bestiary (the Paloma, the Chihuahua and the Avispas, the Tlacuache back in 2-3), GDD-BOS-03 El Tlacuachón in the arena 2-4, a luchador opossum only a stomp hits, reached by a high bounce off an awning. The glide is to be unlocked by 2-4's first clear, read from the save's progress with no new field. Every other section listed gets a Proposed, not-built note where World 2 changes it; the Decided sections GDD-LIFE-05 and GDD-ART-06 get Proposed notes only, their Decided text unchanged. GDD-WLD-05: "one signature hazard" a world becomes one or two signature elements, as GDD-WLD-02's table lists for World 2. GDD-LOOP-03: a Poncho power from World 2 on. Open Question 13 gets World 2's proposed 4 tiles; Open Questions 15, 18, 20 and 21 point to World 2; Open Questions 22 to 33 added. Dependency review for this minor version (GDD-TEC-07): `npm outdated` showed `oxlint` 1.90.0 and `vitest` 5.0.5 behind, upgraded to 1.91.0 and 5.1.0, gate and playtests green; `@types/node` stays on 24 to match the Node 24 that CI runs (ADR 0002). Review of PR #76: the Chihuahua never hops onto an awning (its hop peaks about 16 px, level with the lowest awning), and a world opens only once it is complete.
- **0.9.4** (2026-11-03) GDD-BOS-01, GDD-BOS-02, GDD-ENM-01, GDD-CMB-01, GDD-CMB-02, GDD-CMB-03, GDD-PWR-03, GDD-LIFE-02, GDD-LIFE-05, GDD-WLD-03, GDD-WLD-04, GDD-WLD-09, GDD-TEC-04, GDD-PRG-02, GDD-PRG-03, GDD-UI-01, GDD-UI-02, GDD-UI-05, GDD-AUD-02, GDD-ART-06, GDD-LIFE-03: World 1's boss, Don Zopilote, in his one-screen arena 1-4, the last of World 1's levels (user, 2026-11-02: "Don Zopilote as written", the numbers Proposed "for you to adjust after playing"; "Placeholders for now"; his rules "As planned"). GDD-BOS-01 and GDD-BOS-02 replace 0.9.0's "Decided for the build" and "Not built yet" with the built rules: a flat floor and two 3-tile pillars with a rock on each; he laps an ellipse in 4.0 s dropping four cactus fruit a lap, telegraphs 0.5 s, then dives (phases 1 and 3, 180 and 300 px/s, stuck 1.0 and 0.6 s) or sweeps the floor (phase 2). As the user decided: the claw and fireballs hit him only while he is stuck after a dive, a rock knocked by the claw in any phase, never a stomp; in phase 3 his fruit lies 3.0 s on the floor; a lost life keeps his hits and restarts his phase; running out of lives there goes back to 1-1 with seven, like anywhere in World 1. Three hits, hurt 1.0 s after each; 1.0 s after the third the level clear screen. "Next level" on 1-3 starts 1-4 with 1-3's lives, "Start" follows the World 1 run to it (user, 2026-11-02: "Follow the run"), and a clear of 1-4, World 1's last level, starts a new run at 1-1. A boss bar on the HUD; the validator's boss-level rule; 1-4 saved (no hilos, no saved run); its medal Gold for at most 3 lives lost in it, never Bronze; the stomp, land and meow sounds reused; 4 plants. Every measured number re-checked on the merged code, slopes and the lift fix included; the fruit's 0.7 to 0.9 s in view now pinned by a test. The neighbouring sections brought in line; Open Questions 19 (the pillar tops), 20 (the fruit's telegraph) and 21 (hilos in the arena) added, 18 names 1-4. Screenshots approved in session (user, 2026-11-03: "Approved, merge"; `docs/screenshots/boss/`).
- **0.9.3** (2026-11-03) GDD-WLD-09, GDD-WLD-08, GDD-WLD-05, GDD-WLD-03, GDD-WLD-04, GDD-MOV-09, GDD-ENM-02, GDD-LIFE-03, GDD-LIFE-05, GDD-PRG-02, GDD-UI-02, GDD-UI-05, GDD-TEC-04, GDD-ART-06: level 1-3, the last level of World 1 before the boss (user, 2026-11-02: "1-3: moving platforms, doors and keys, fragile walls and moving blocks. Everything is built already, so agents only lay out and test the levels."; "Add sand slopes too"; its two quirks: "Accept both"; user, 2026-11-03, its screenshots: "Approved"). GDD-WLD-09 built: six screens with two lifts, a shuttle over spikes, three doors and keys, three fragile walls and four crushers, each first in a safe spot, then with a threat, then in a test, with sand slopes, three Tlacuaches and a crumbling bridge; 50 hilos, three ofrendas, a veladora on a ledge over the shuttle, the exit on the last cliff (GDD-WLD-03: an exit stands on the ground, not only on the floor). A bot plays it through with all seven lives in under 2400 steps. The enemies' own walks keep clear of the crushers (Proposed); two quirks are known and accepted: a full jump from the shuttle's far wall clears the second fragile wall into the Alacrán's pen, and the Tlacuache let out of the third pen walks through the crushers. 1-3 joins World 1's run and lives (user, 2026-11-02: "Build it as decided", "Save the lives", "Lives at the clear", "Keep them", "Lives lost in the level", "Follow the run"): "Next level" on 1-2's results menu starts it with 1-2's lives (1-2's results menu no longer greys it), the save moves the run on to it, "Retry" starts it with the lives at its clear, its own results menu greys "Next level" until the boss arena is built, and clearing it ends the run (World 1's last level for now); out of lives in 1-3 its hilos are saved and the restart is at 1-1 with seven; its gold counts the lives lost in 1-3 only. `?level=1-3` opens it; its progress and runs are saved. GDD-WLD-08, GDD-WLD-05, GDD-WLD-04, GDD-MOV-09, GDD-ENM-02, GDD-LIFE-03, GDD-LIFE-05, GDD-PRG-02, GDD-UI-02, GDD-UI-05, GDD-TEC-04, GDD-ART-06: sentences brought in line. Open Questions 15 and 18 name 1-3. Screenshots approved in session (`docs/screenshots/level1-3/`). GDD-WLD-05, GDD-WLD-09: a falling Poncho lands on a rising moving platform that passes his feet within one step; before, he could drop through it (user, 2026-11-02: "Fix it"). He lands where his feet began the step at or above the platform's top, where it was or where it is now, whichever is lower, unless standing there would put his body box into anything solid above it (a fragile wall, a shut door or a piñata counts like a tile). Found while laying out 1-3's lifts (GDD-WLD-09, whose "is to catch him" now says it does).
- **0.9.2** (2026-11-02) GDD-WLD-08, GDD-LIFE-05, GDD-UI-02, GDD-PRG-02, GDD-PRG-03, GDD-WLD-07, GDD-WLD-06, GDD-WLD-04, GDD-WLD-05, GDD-LIFE-02, GDD-LIFE-03, GDD-LIFE-04, GDD-TEC-04, GDD-ART-06, GDD-ENM-02, GDD-MOV-09, GDD-UI-05: level 1-2, the lives carried through World 1, and the save keeping World 1's run, which "Start" follows (user, 2026-11-02: "I want to spawn a agent swarm so we do progress on the lvls generate all lvls left for world one."; "1-2: the Tlacuache, crumbling tiles and springs."; "Add sand slopes too"; "Three PRs in turn"; "Build it as decided"; "Save the lives"; "Lives at the clear"; "Keep them"; "Lives lost in the level"; "Follow the run", which replaced "First level not cleared"; the screenshots "Approved"). GDD-WLD-08 built: six screens with sand slopes throughout, three Tlacuaches in pens, thirteen crumbling tiles, three springs, a cactus buddy and two piñatas, each new thing first in a safe spot, then with a threat, then in a test; 50 hilos, three ofrendas, a veladora on a ledge beside the first spring, reached by its timed launch or by a jump from the wall it throws Poncho onto, an exit at column 174. "Next level" on 1-1's results menu now starts 1-2 (1-1's results menu no longer greys it), `?level=1-2` opens it, and its progress and runs are saved like 0-1's and 1-1's. A bot plays it through with all seven lives in under 1800 steps. GDD-LIFE-05 built for World 1: "Next level" and "Retry" start a World 1 level with the lives Poncho has at the clear, its ofrendas and veladora unused; the tutorial's lives carry nowhere; out of lives in 1-2, the press after the *Siete vidas gastadas* screen starts 1-1 at once with seven lives by the restart's flip. GDD-PRG-02: the save keeps World 1's run (`progress.worldLives`, the lives at each World 1 clear, 0 after the *Siete vidas gastadas* screen; `progress.worldAt`, the next level after each World 1 clear, 1-1 after World 1's last level and after the screen) and a saved run the lives it started with (`livesIn`); the hilos a run collected are saved for good when the screen comes up, in every real level (replacing the rule of 2026-10-31 that only a clear saved them); the version stays 1. GDD-UI-02: once the tutorial has started, "Start" plays the level World 1's run has reached with the lives World 1 keeps, 1-1 always with seven (after a game over too, and once the run has cleared World 1's last level, a new run); a save from before keeps the older rule (the first World 1 level not cleared); the title shows that level with those lives; an address, "Tutorial" and the restart give seven. GDD-PRG-03: gold for every hilo and at most 3 lives lost in the level, whatever Poncho came in with (`MEDAL.goldLivesLost` 3 replaces `MEDAL.goldLives` 4). GDD-LIFE-02, GDD-LIFE-03, GDD-LIFE-04, GDD-WLD-04, GDD-WLD-05, GDD-WLD-06, GDD-WLD-07, GDD-ENM-02, GDD-TEC-04, GDD-ART-06, GDD-MOV-09, GDD-UI-05: sentences brought in line. Open Questions 15 (1-2's veladora), 17 (what 1-2 brings back) and 18 (the details of World 1's run and lives). Screenshots approved in session (`docs/screenshots/level1-2/`).
- **0.9.1** (2026-11-02) GDD-MOV-09, GDD-MOV-03, GDD-MOV-06, GDD-LIFE-03, GDD-LIFE-05, GDD-WLD-03, GDD-WLD-05, GDD-ENM-02, GDD-PWR-02, GDD-PWR-03, GDD-ART-06, GDD-TEC-04, GDD-UI-02, GDD-UI-04, GDD-UI-05, GDD-PRG-02: sand slopes are built (user, 2026-11-02: "Ramps you run on"; the gentle slope is "1 up per 2 across", about 26.6°; the slope look "Approved" from screenshots; the speed across a slope rather than along it, no launch off a ramp's top and nothing inside a slope tile: "All three OK", their details Proposed). GDD-MOV-09's "Not built yet" gives way to the built rules: six slope characters in a level's rows, each solid below its surface line with a level tile under it; only the middle of Poncho's feet stands on a slope; he keeps the run speed across it, steps up and down by up to 4 px a step without leaving the ground, and jumps from it as from flat ground; a slope's high side is a wall; the Alacrán, the Tlacuache and the walking taco follow slopes, and fireballs bounce off them; a new one-screen test level, the slopes level (`?level=slopes`). GDD-ART-06: the slope tiles, the approved ground tiles cut along the slope, in a ground sheet of 28 frames; a tile under a slope is no top tile and grows no plant. GDD-TEC-04: the level validator checks that every slope has a level tile under it and holds nothing else. GDD-MOV-03, GDD-MOV-06, GDD-WLD-05, GDD-ENM-02, GDD-PWR-02, GDD-PWR-03: how each meets a slope. GDD-LIFE-03, GDD-LIFE-05, GDD-WLD-03, GDD-UI-02, GDD-UI-04, GDD-UI-05, GDD-PRG-02: the slopes level joins the sandbox as a test level.

- **0.5.7** (2026-10-25) GDD-WLD-05, GDD-PWR-05, GDD-ENM-02: the sandbox's dead end at the 2-tile wall is fixed (user, 2026-10-25: "Piñata onto platform end"). With the 32 px body, the Alacrán's platform and the piñata at column 9 made one ceiling 48 px above the floor, so on foot Poncho could not get onto the 2-tile wall from the left. The platform is now columns 4 to 7 and the piñata stands at its right end, column 8, directly above the door, which it closes off; it can be bumped from below once the door is open. Column 9 is open to the sky, so from the floor there a short hop lands him on the wall (checked by a test that plays the route from the spawn through the key and the door). The Alacrán's patrol goes back to x = 71 to 137, and the claw from the wall top still opens the piñata.
- **0.5.6** (2026-10-25) GDD-MOV-06, GDD-WLD-03, GDD-ART-01, GDD-CMB-02, GDD-WLD-05, GDD-WLD-04, GDD-ENM-02, GDD-PWR-05: Poncho's body box is built at 12 x 32, most of his height with the hat (user, 2026-10-25: first "in between", 12 x 20, as decided for when the sprite went into the game, which the review of PR #51 found still 12 x 14; then "most of hat", because his head came through the stones at 20 px), and the hurtbox follows it to 10 x 30 under the Proposed 1 px inset. He walks under a gap exactly 2 tiles high and not under one tile. The level limits (a 3-tile wall, a 5-tile gap) are unchanged. The claw box grows with the body to 20 x 32, so a swipe from the floor still opens a fragile wall he can walk through (user, 2026-10-25; GDD-CMB-02, GDD-WLD-05). The sandbox changes to fit him (user, 2026-10-25): the spring ledge moves up a tile, to row 7 (112 px above the floor), so his head clears its side on the jump to the pit hilo, which now lets go of right in the air to land on the spring (GDD-WLD-04), and its two hilos move up with it; both piñatas move up a tile so a standing jump bumps them (GDD-PWR-05): the one at column 9 now sits level with the Alacrán's platform, so the Alacrán's patrol runs on across its top, and the one at column 23 is in the path of the moving platform's far end, which stops Poncho against it. GDD-ENM-02: the Zopilote figures follow the hurtbox centre, now 16 px above his feet.

When the run frames play and which frame shows in the air are written out as proposals. The 12 x 20 body box comes in the next change.
- **0.5.3** (2026-10-25) GDD-ART-02, GDD-ART-01, GDD-ART-03, GDD-ART-05, GDD-MOV-06: the user's pixel drawing of Poncho (`sprite-pixel.jpg`) is the main sprite reference, with a tail added (user, 2026-10-25); the sprite keeps its pixels, each drawn as 2 x 2 art pixels, so Poncho's frame grows to 40 x 40 game pixels (160 x 160 art pixels, Proposed); its 13 colours are the sprite palette and its near-black is the outline colour. The body box grows to 12 x 20 when the sprite goes into the game (user, 2026-10-25: "in between", body and head but not the hat); until then it stays 12 x 14.
- **0.5.2** (2026-10-25) GDD-WLD-02, GDD-ART-06, GDD-ART-02: World 1 stays in the Chihuahuan desert, and its scenery swaps the reference scene's saguaros for cacti that grow there (user, 2026-10-25); the species, prickly pear, cholla, barrel cactus, palm yucca, ocotillo and lechuguilla, are Proposed. Poncho is a he (user, 2026-10-25); Open Question 5 answered.
- **0.5.1** (2026-10-25) GDD-CTL-02, GDD-UI-02, GDD-CTL-04, GDD-LIFE-05, GDD-PWR-03, GDD-MVP-01: gamepads are built: south jumps, west claws, east spits a fireball, start pauses, and the D-pad or the left stick moves (the stick with analog speed past its 0.2 dead zone). In the menus the D-pad or stick works like the arrow keys, south like Enter and start like Escape, and start on the title screen starts the level (user decisions, 2026-10-25). A gamepad press made while paused is held back like a key's. The pad is read once per frame, so a press shorter than a frame can be missed (user, 2026-10-25: accepted). Only pads with the standard mapping are read; the stick's menu threshold (0.5) and how a diagonal counts are written out as proposals.
- **0.5.0** (2026-10-25) GDD-ART-01, GDD-ART-02, GDD-ART-03, GDD-ART-04, GDD-MOV-06, GDD-WLD-02, GDD-WLD-03, GDD-TEC-03, GDD-TEC-07: the style guide. Decided by the user (2026-10-25): the art is drawn in code and approved from screenshots; it is drawn at 1920 x 1080, four times 480 x 270 on each side, so tiles are 64 x 64 and Poncho's frame 128 x 128 art pixels; the user's side-view sprite sheets (side, back, front and two walk cycles) become Poncho's sprite reference, winning over the illustration where they differ, and show the tail (long, white tip); each world has its own mood; World 1 blends the user's desert scene (blue and purple mountains, clouds, saguaros, prickly pears, agave, flowers, grass on the sand) with a warmer golden sky. Proposed: every rule keeps its numbers in game pixels (4 x 4 art pixels each), so no gameplay changes; the brim is about twice the body's width; a sprite palette sampled from the sprite sheets. New: GDD-ART-05 pixel rules (outline, light, shading, layers, hazard edges), GDD-ART-06 world moods, GDD-ART-07 UI look. Open Question 14 added (detail on phones). World 1 moves from the Chihuahuan to the Sonoran desert, where saguaros grow (Proposed, Open Question 3). Letterboxing now reads "integer factors where possible" again under its own Proposed bullet. Dependency review for this minor version (GDD-TEC-07): `npm outdated` showed vite 8.3.4 and oxlint-tsgolint 7.0.2004 behind, both upgraded (8.3.5, 7.0.2005), gate and playtests green; @types/node stays on 24 to match the Node 24 that CI runs (ADR 0002). Nothing is built yet: the game still draws placeholders at 480 x 270.
- **0.4.47** (2026-10-25) GDD-UI-02: the title screen's heading names the 2026-10-25 title menu decision, and the volume bars' height (10 px, centred) is written down (found in review).
- **0.4.46** (2026-10-25) GDD-UI-02, GDD-CTL-01, GDD-CTL-03, GDD-AUD-01, GDD-PRG-02, GDD-UI-03, GDD-MVP-02: a settings screen with Master and Effects volume (0 to 10, default 10), reached from a new "Settings" item in the pause menu (now Resume, Settings, Quit to title) and from a two-row title menu ("Start", "Settings") that replaces the title prompt; Space or Z picks there and Up or W chooses, so the jump keys no longer start the level by themselves, while claw, Fuego, Left, Right and a tap off the rows still do; the hidden touch buttons press nothing on the title screen. Sound effects play at Master times Effects. The settings are saved in the browser, the first slice of the save system. A language switch with English, Spanish and German comes next (user, 2026-10-25).
- **0.4.45** (2026-10-25) GDD-UI-02, GDD-CTL-03: a Left or Right key or touch button pressed while paused and held through the resume no longer runs until it is released and pressed again, as the rule already said, and a quick tap of either while paused moves nothing; the same holds for the one that starts the level from the title screen. A finger that went down while paused presses no touch button, not only no slide stick (a gap found in the review of 0.4.44).
- **0.4.44** (2026-10-23) GDD-UI-02, GDD-CTL-01, GDD-CTL-03, GDD-WLD-04: the pause screen becomes a two-item menu, "Resume" and "Quit to title": Up or W and Down or S choose, Enter, Space or Z picks, and a tap or click on an item picks it; Escape, P, the pause button and a tap anywhere else still resume. In slide-stick mode a finger that went down while paused, or was down when the level starts from the title screen, does not steer until lifted (found in review). Quitting brings back the title screen over a fresh run: seven lives, every hilo back, everything at its start (user decisions, 2026-10-23: a two-item menu; no confirmation; a fresh run). The menu's size, look and keys are written out as proposals.
- **0.4.43** (2026-10-23) GDD-UI-02, GDD-CTL-01, GDD-CTL-03: on the title screen a finger on the hidden mode switch no longer changes the touch mode, and a tap and a turn upright in the same frame no longer start the level upright; the Start row of the key table names the keys exactly (found in review).
- **0.4.42** (2026-10-23) GDD-UI-02, GDD-CTL-01, GDD-CTL-03: the title screen is built: the game opens on it, with the level standing still under a dark veil, "PONCHO" and "Siete Vidas", the placeholder Poncho at twice his size and a prompt; Enter, a tap or click anywhere, or a new press of any game button starts the level, and the button that started it counts as already held. An upright phone is asked to turn sideways first, so the level never starts upright (user decisions, 2026-10-23: the level behind a veil plus the placeholder Poncho; any button or a tap starts; "Press any button to start" or "Tap to start"; the pause screen stays resume-only for now).
- **0.4.41** (2026-10-23) GDD-AUD-02, GDD-LIFE-02, GDD-CMB-02, GDD-WLD-04: when the jump, land, claw, stomp, hilo, life lost and meow sounds play, as generated placeholders like the marimba. The hilo pluck climbs a major scale while hilos come within 0.75 s of each other. Poncho meows when he appears again after a lost life (user decisions, 2026-10-23: meow on respawn; all seven sounds in one change).
- **0.4.40** (2026-10-23) GDD-TEC-04, GDD-WLD-03, GDD-ENM-01, GDD-ENM-02, GDD-TEC-06: the level validator is built, ahead of the Tiled loader. A test runs it over every level in the game and checks 3 ofrendas, an exit, a length of 3 to 6 screens, no gap wider than the world's limit and no enemy starting within 3 tiles of a respawn. A gap is a run of columns whose bottom row is empty. The sandbox is a test level, which skips the exit and length checks (user decisions, 2026-10-23: validator only, Tiled later; the sandbox marked as a test level; a gap is a run of pit columns).
- **0.4.39** (2026-10-23) GDD-WLD-04, GDD-UI-01, GDD-LIFE-02, GDD-LIFE-04, GDD-LIFE-05, GDD-PWR-02, GDD-PWR-03: hilo pickups and the HUD hilo counter are built. A hilo is an 8 x 8 px skein in the middle of its tile, collected by touch with the body box, and once collected it stays collected through lost lives and the restart after the last life. The counter in the top right shows a skein and "collected/total" for the level. The sandbox has 20 (user decisions, 2026-10-23: pickups and counter first; about 20 in the sandbox, 50 stays the number for real levels; the counter reads collected/total; placeholder skeins in serape colours). Items that give hilos instead, the multi-piñata, the score and saving come later.

The sandbox has one in the pocket past the fragile wall (user decisions, 2026-10-23: a moving solid block is what crushes, a crush costs a life like a pit).
- **0.4.36** (2026-10-23) GDD-MOV-08, GDD-WLD-05, GDD-PRG-01, GDD-MVP-02: the wall cling and kick is built ahead of the MVP, behind the same ability flag as the glide (on in the sandbox, where the level's left and right edges are full-height walls): pressing toward a wall while falling slides at 60 px/s; jump beside a wall in the air kicks away at 130 px/s across and 380 px/s up, then sideways control is locked for 120 ms (user decisions, 2026-10-23: a kick whenever he touches a wall in the air, the cling wins over the glide, a 0.1 s grace after leaving the wall, claw marks as the placeholder look; and a spring launch wins over a wall kick, GDD-WLD-05).
- **0.4.35** (2026-10-22) GDD-MOV-07, GDD-PRG-01, GDD-MVP-02: the glide is built ahead of the MVP, behind an ability flag a level turns on (the sandbox does, until World 2 can unlock it): holding jump while falling caps the fall at 70 px/s, sideways movement unchanged, as long as jump is held; not within 150 ms of a stomp bounce; a placeholder spread poncho is drawn (user decisions, 2026-10-22: unlock flag on in the sandbox, jump held as he falls, a wide poncho bar). New proposal: a glide starts only once jump has been held longer than the 0.1 s jump buffer (GDD-MOV-03), so a press just before landing stays a buffered jump. The hold counts from a press the game saw, so a jump pressed while paused or held through a respawn does not glide (GDD-UI-02). GDD-UI-02, GDD-CTL-01: Escape and P now pause on every keyboard layout and every quick press counts once (they are read from the browser's own key events by the key's label).
- **0.4.34** (2026-10-22) GDD-UI-02, GDD-UI-01, GDD-CTL-01, GDD-CTL-03: the pause in a level is built: Escape or P, or a touch pause button in the top-right corner, pauses under a dimmed screen; the same key or button, or a tap anywhere, resumes; losing focus or a hidden tab pauses, and so does a phone turned upright, which first asks to be turned sideways; a press made while paused does nothing until pressed again (user decisions, 2026-10-22: resume only, button top-right, auto-pause on focus and portrait, resume with the pause key or a tap). ---

## 1. Vision

### GDD-VIS-01 Logline and premise

Status: Decided (title and premise, user, 2026-10-19). Wording is Proposed.

**PONCHO: Siete Vidas** is a 2D platformer starring **Poncho**, a cat in a Mexican poncho. A cat has nine lives in English folklore, but in the Spanish-speaking tradition a cat has **siete vidas**. The title is the central mechanic: seven lives, and how you spend them is the game (see GDD-LIFE-01).

Character trait (Decided, user, 2026-10-19): Poncho loves tacos and spicy food. This drives the power-ups (section 6) and Poncho's personality.

Story seed (Proposed): Poncho has lost lives one by one to a curse and must travel the seven worlds to get them back.

### GDD-VIS-02 Platforms and audience

Status: Decided (web and mobile, user, 2026-10-19). Audience is Proposed.

- Runs in desktop and mobile browsers. Touch controls are a first-class input (GDD-CTL-03).
- Audience (Proposed): players aged 8+ who like precise, forgiving, charming platformers. Difficulty is fair and readable, not punishing.
- Orientation (Proposed): landscape only on mobile.

### GDD-VIS-03 Design pillars

Status: Proposed

1. **Every life counts.** Seven lives is a visible, tangible resource that creates tension without cruelty.
2. **Cat agility.** Movement feels quick, light and forgiving. A cat always lands on its feet.
3. **Fair and readable.** Hazards are telegraphed. Deaths feel like the player's mistake and a retry is instant.
4. **Mexico with care.** Specific, respectful, affectionate references to real places, crafts and traditions. No stereotype shortcuts (GDD-VIS-04).

### GDD-VIS-04 Tone and cultural approach

Status: Proposed

- Tone: warm, playful, a little spooky. Día de Muertos imagery fits the seven-lives theme, and is treated as celebration of memory, not horror.
- Use specific references: serape weaving patterns, talavera tile, papel picado, alebrijes, ofrendas, regional landscapes (desert, sierra, jungle, cenotes, volcano).
- Avoid: sombrero-and-siesta caricature, cartel imagery, mocking accents, a single flattened "Mexico".
- Spanish is used for names and flavour; all gameplay text is also available in English (GDD-UI-03).
- If unsure about a reference, ask the user or leave it out.

### GDD-VIS-05 Origin and brand

Status: Decided (user, 2026-10-19). The scope limits are Proposed.

Poncho is the origin character of **Poncho on Base** ("PB"), a meme coin on the Base blockchain, where Poncho is billed as the cutest cat on Base. This game is a platformer starring that character. The "PB" marks in the reference image are that brand (GDD-ART-02).

Proposed scope: the game has no wallet, token, NFT or on-chain feature, and it never asks for an account. It plays fully offline. Any such integration needs its own GDD ID and a decision from the user first (Open Question 6).

---

## 2. Core loop

### GDD-LOOP-01 Moment-to-moment loop

Status: Proposed

Run, jump, dodge or defeat enemies, reach the next checkpoint. Each mistake costs a life but is recoverable. Mastery means finishing a world with lives to spare.

### GDD-LOOP-02 Level loop

Status: Proposed

Enter level, reach 3 checkpoints (ofrendas), reach the exit. Optional: collect all *hilos* (GDD-WLD-04) and find one secret. A level takes 2 to 4 minutes on first clear (the tutorial 0-1 is much shorter, which is fine for a tutorial, GDD-WLD-06).

### GDD-LOOP-03 World loop

Status: Proposed

A world is 3 levels and 1 boss. Lives are a resource across the whole world (GDD-LIFE-05). Clearing a world unlocks the next one and, from World 2 on, a Poncho power (GDD-PRG-01: World 1 gives none, World 2 the glide).

---

## 3. Siete Vidas (the lives system)

### GDD-LIFE-01 Seven lives, seven triangles

Status: Proposed (the number seven is Decided by the title)

- Poncho has **7 lives** at the start of every world.
- Lives are shown as **seven triangles**, the signature motif of Poncho's poncho (GDD-ART-02). Each triangle is one life. They are drawn on the HUD (GDD-UI-01); a lost life leaves an empty outline.
- The poncho in the reference has triangle and chevron patterns, not stripes, so the earlier "seven stripes" idea was dropped.
- The count never exceeds 7.

### GDD-LIFE-02 Losing a life

Status: Proposed

- A life is lost on: being hit while in the Base power-up state (GDD-PWR-01), touching an enemy or hazard without invulnerability, falling into a pit, or being crushed.
- On loss: the lost triangle on the HUD cracks and fades to an outline (0.4 s), Poncho flips and lands feet-first at the last checkpoint after 0.8 s (GDD-MOV-05), then has 2.0 s of invulnerability (blinking).
- Enemies and moving hazards reset to their initial state (a boss keeps the hits he has taken and starts his phase again, GDD-BOS-01), collected *hilos* since the checkpoint are kept (no loss of progress that is not a life). Built for hilos: a collected one stays collected and the count stays (GDD-WLD-04).
- Respawn is instant to restart: any input skips the 0.8 s delay after 0.3 s. Any input is any button held, jump, claw, Fuego, left or right (both directions at once too), or a push of the slide stick past its dead zone (GDD-CTL-03).
- A fall into a pit counts once the whole body is below the bottom row of the level (GDD-MOV-06). It costs one life even while invulnerable (GDD-CMB-03). For the first 0.45 s of the wait Poncho is hidden. A button held through the respawn does not make him jump. Because "any input" includes a held direction, a player who keeps running into a pit respawns after 0.3 s instead of 0.8 s.
- Respawn point: the latest ofrenda Poncho has reached (GDD-LIFE-03), or the level start if he touched none.
- **Respawn flip, built (Poncho's standing frame turns, GDD-ART-04):** in the last 0.35 s of the wait (21 steps) Poncho appears 48 px above the respawn point, drops with gravity-like acceleration while turning once, and is on the ground, upright and feet down, on the last step, so control returns without a jump in position (`FLIP` in `src/config/tuning.ts`). He lands at the respawn point, the latest ofrenda reached. After the last life there is no flip during the wait; the *Siete vidas gastadas* screen comes up instead, and the flip plays after it, at the start of the world's first level (GDD-LIFE-05). A skip (any input after 0.3 s) before the flip starts shows no flip, and a skip during it makes him appear at the respawn point at once. The turn shows on the sprite itself. He meows as he appears (GDD-AUD-02).
- **HUD crack, built:** the lost triangle keeps its outline, its red fill fades from full to nothing over 0.4 s (24 steps, `LIFE_LOSS` in `src/config/tuning.ts`) while a crack line appears across it quickly and fades with the fill. If several lives are lost at once, each of their triangles cracks. A triangle that is filled again (an ofrenda) stops cracking. The animation counts simulation steps, so it pauses with the game. 
- **Crushing (built, details Proposed; user, 2026-10-23: a crush costs a life like a pit):** Poncho is crushed when a moving block (GDD-WLD-05) pushes him, or carries him up, into a solid tile, so his body box would have to overlap it. A shut door, an unbroken fragile wall, a piñata, a spring and a crumbling tile that has not fallen count as solid. A crush costs exactly one life like a fall into a pit: in every power-up state (Taco and Fuego do not absorb it, and he comes back in Base, GDD-PWR-01) and even while invulnerable (GDD-CMB-03). The respawn wait, the flip and the respawn point are those of any lost life. Moving platforms never crush him.

### GDD-LIFE-03 Regaining lives

Status: Proposed (that only the latest ofrenda counts as the checkpoint is Decided, user, 2026-10-20)

- **Ofrenda checkpoint:** first activation of an ofrenda restores 1 life if fewer than 7. Each ofrenda restores only once in a run of its level: a level started anew (by "Next level", "Retry", the restart after the last life, or "Start" or "Tutorial" on the title) has every ofrenda unused, whatever lives it starts with (GDD-LIFE-05); "Continue" brings back the ones the saved run used (GDD-PRG-02).
- **Only the latest ofrenda counts (Decided, user, 2026-10-20):** an ofrenda becomes the respawn point only if it comes later in the level than the current one. Touching an earlier ofrenda never moves the respawn point back. One touched for the first time after a later one still restores its life, but is not the checkpoint. Ofrendas count in the order the level lists them, which is the order the player meets them.
- A restart after the last life (GDD-LIFE-05) puts every ofrenda back to unused and the respawn point at the start of the world's first level (1-1 in World 1, and in World 2, Proposed and not built yet, 2-1, GDD-WLD-12; the tutorial and a test level restart at their own start).
- **Veladora pickup:** a candle hidden in a level restores 1 life. At most 1 per level.
- **Veladora (built, details Proposed):** a 12 x 12 px pickup in the middle of its tile; a level has room for one. It is collected when Poncho's body box overlaps it (boxes that only share an edge do not touch), on any step he is in play, like a hilo (GDD-WLD-04): also the step he is hit, falls into a pit or is crushed, and then its life counts before the one lost that step. It restores 1 life if fewer than 7 and is used up either way. Once collected it is gone: losing a life does not bring it back, but like the ofrendas it is back after the restart that follows the last life (GDD-LIFE-05), after quitting to the title, and in a level started by "Next level" or "Retry" (GDD-UI-02). Placeholder look: a white candle with a yellow flame in a red glass. Levels 0-1, 1-1, 1-2 and 1-3 have one each (GDD-WLD-06 to GDD-WLD-09), the boss arena 1-4 (GDD-BOS-01) and the test levels (the sandbox, the slopes level) none. Proposed, not built yet: 2-1, 2-2 and 2-3 one each (GDD-WLD-12 to GDD-WLD-14), the boss arena 2-4 none (GDD-BOS-03). Not built yet: a sound. `VELADORA` in `src/config/tuning.ts`, `src/sim/veladora.ts`.
- No other source. Lives stay scarce.

### GDD-LIFE-04 La Última Vida (last life)

Status: Proposed

When exactly 1 life remains: the music shifts to a tense variation, the last triangle pulses, and a *Última Vida* bonus applies: hilos give double score (GDD-PRG-03). Risk and reward, never an added penalty.

- **Pulse, built (details Proposed):** while exactly one life is left, its triangle on the HUD (the leftmost) grows to 1.4 times its size and back, about its centre, smoothly (a cosine), every 0.8 s (48 steps), counted from the step the lives drop to one, or from the start of a level begun with one (GDD-LIFE-05: "Next level" or "Retry" after a World 1 clear with one life, or "Start" with one life kept for World 1, after quitting to the title too; checked by `e2e/world1.spec.ts`; Proposed, not built yet: the same after a World 2 clear or with one life kept for World 2's run, GDD-LIFE-05), so it always starts at its normal size; it keeps pulsing as long as one life is left, through the respawn wait too. It counts simulation steps, so it pauses with the game, like the crack (GDD-LIFE-02). With no lives (the Siete vidas gastadas screen, GDD-LIFE-05) or two or more it does not pulse. `LAST_LIFE` in `src/config/tuning.ts`, `src/ui/lastLife.ts`. Not built yet: the tense music (GDD-AUD-01) and the double hilo score (GDD-PRG-03), which wait for music and the score.

### GDD-LIFE-05 Out of lives

Status: Decided (lives are carried across a world, user, 2026-10-19; built for World 1, with its lives saved, "Retry" starting with the lives at the clear and the hilos saved when the screen comes up, user, 2026-11-02). Details are Proposed.

- Lives are tracked per world: 7 at world start, carried through its 3 levels and boss. **Built for World 1 (user, 2026-11-02: "Build it as decided"; "Lives at the clear"; "Save the lives"; "Follow the run"; details Proposed):** picking "Next level" on a level's results menu (GDD-UI-02) starts the next level with the lives Poncho has at the clear when both are levels of the same one of the seven worlds (1-1's go on into 1-2, 1-2's into 1-3 and 1-3's into the boss arena 1-4, GDD-WLD-08, GDD-WLD-09, GDD-BOS-02), and picking "Retry" starts the same level again with them ("The lives you finished the level with; its ofrendas and veladora can win some back before Next level."); a level started that way has every ofrenda and its veladora unused, whatever its lives (GDD-LIFE-03). A level of another world starts with seven. The save keeps World 1's run ("The save keeps the World 1 lives too: Start plays the first level not cleared with the lives you had, and after a game over the world restarts at 1-1 with 7, however you get there.", where "Follow the run" has since replaced "first level not cleared"; GDD-PRG-02, "World 1's run"): the lives at each World 1 clear and the level the run goes on to, and 0 lives and 1-1 from the step this screen comes up; "Start" plays the run's level with those lives, and 1-1, World 1's start, always with seven (GDD-UI-02, "Which level"). The tutorial 0-1 is outside the seven worlds (GDD-WLD-01): it starts with seven, and its lives carry neither into 1-1 nor into its own "Retry", which start with seven again (GDD-WLD-06), nor into the save. "Tutorial" on the title and an address give seven; "Continue" brings back the lives saved with the run (GDD-PRG-02). A test level (the sandbox, the slopes level) starts with seven and has no exit, so its lives go nowhere. `livesInto`, `startLevel`, `worldLivesAfter` and `worldAtAfter` in `src/levels/index.ts`, `startRun` in `src/sim/run.ts`. 1-3 gets them from 1-2 (GDD-WLD-09), and the boss arena 1-4, World 1's last level, gets them from 1-3 (GDD-BOS-02); a clear of 1-4 ends the run, so the save moves it back to 1-1, a new run that "Start" plays with seven.
- At 0 lives: *Siete vidas gastadas* screen, then restart from the first level of the world with 7 lives. Hilos collected stay (GDD-PRG-02). **Built (user, 2026-11-02: "Build it as decided"; the hilos: "Keep them": "Saved for good when the Siete vidas gastadas screen comes up, in every level; that's what GDD-LIFE-05 says."; details Proposed):** World 1's first level is 1-1 (GDD-WLD-07). Out of lives in 1-1, the restart is in 1-1 itself, the same scene going on. Out of lives in 1-2, 1-3 or the boss arena 1-4 (GDD-WLD-08, GDD-WLD-09, GDD-BOS-02), the press that ends the screen starts 1-1 at once, as the next level starts (GDD-UI-02: no title screen, the camera at its start, the button that pressed held there), with seven lives and its clock at 0:00, and the restart's flip plays at 1-1's start (below); the screen stays up until 1-1 has started. However the restart is reached it is 1-1 with seven: from the step the screen comes up the save keeps World 1's run at 1-1 with 0 lives (GDD-PRG-02, "World 1's run"), so quitting to the title, or closing the game, and picking "Start" plays 1-1 with seven too, until 1-1 is cleared again (GDD-UI-02, "Which level"). The tutorial 0-1 restarts at its own start, and so does a test level, which is in no world's order (`worldStart` in `src/levels/index.ts`, `restartRun` in `src/sim/run.ts`). The hilos the run collected stay, in every real level: they are saved for good on the step the screen comes up (GDD-PRG-02, "Progress"), so the ones collected in 1-2 or 1-3 are there, faded, the next time that level is played, and in a restart in the same level they also stay collected in the run (GDD-WLD-04).
- **Screen, built (details Proposed):** when the usual 0.8 s wait after the last life ends (or is skipped, GDD-LIFE-02), the screen comes up instead of a respawn, and Poncho stays hidden: there is no respawn flip in that wait. The level stays in view, dimmed, behind the title *Siete vidas gastadas* and seven empty triangles (the HUD's look for lost lives, larger, centred). Below the triangles: "Game over", and under it the prompt line "Press any button to Retry" (user, 2026-10-22). The screen stays up until a new press of any button: jump, claw, Fuego, left or right, on the keyboard, the touch buttons (which stay on top of it, usable) or a gamepad (GDD-CTL-02); in slide-stick mode a push of the stick past its dead zone is a press of left or right (GDD-CTL-03). Presses in its first 1.0 s (60 steps) are ignored, and a button held since before it, or pressed in that second and still held, does not count: only a button that was up on the step before counts, so pressing a second button while holding one does. The prompt line appears when presses start to count (user decision, 2026-10-22: press to continue). A tap shorter than one frame counts for every one of those buttons, left and right included (they are latched like jump and claw, L-006). At the press the world restarts: all seven lives, every ofrenda unused, the respawn point at the start of the world's first level (GDD-LIFE-03). Then the respawn flip plays (0.35 s, GDD-LIFE-02) and Poncho lands there, invulnerable for 2.0 s, with everything else reset as after any lost life. This flip cannot be skipped, since the button that ended the screen is usually still held, and a held jump does not make him jump when control returns. Placeholder art: a dark veil over the level, the title and "Game over" in pale text, the prompt in grey, outline triangles with 2 px lines. `GAME_OVER` in `src/config/tuning.ts`, `src/sim/run.ts`. In World 1 that level is 1-1, wherever the last life was lost (above). In the boss arena 1-4 too the press goes back to 1-1 with seven lives; Don Zopilote keeps none of his hits through it, so the fight starts afresh, with all three, when Poncho next reaches the arena (GDD-BOS-01). The tutorial and a test level restart at their own start. Not built yet: a sound, a transition.
- **World 2 (Proposed, not built yet; it applies the Decided rule "7 at world start, carried through its 3 levels and boss" to World 2, and adds nothing to the Decided text above):** 2-1 (GDD-WLD-12) starts with seven, whatever lives Poncho has at 1-4's clear: 1-4 and 2-1 are in different worlds, so `livesInto` already gives seven, and nothing carries between worlds (GDD-LIFE-01). Inside World 2, "Next level" carries the lives at the clear from 2-1 into 2-2, from 2-2 into 2-3 and from 2-3 into the arena 2-4 (GDD-WLD-13, GDD-WLD-14, GDD-BOS-03), and "Retry" starts the same level with them, as in World 1. At 0 lives in any World 2 level, 2-4 included, the *Siete vidas gastadas* screen comes up as in World 1, and the press that ends it starts 2-1 with seven lives and its clock at 0:00 (out of lives in 2-1 itself, the same scene goes on). From the step the screen comes up the save keeps World 2's run at 2-1 with 0 lives (GDD-PRG-02). Running out of lives in World 2 never sends Poncho back to World 1 and does not change World 1's saved run, medals or hilos; El Tlacuachón keeps none of his hits through it (GDD-BOS-03). The hilos the run collected are saved for good when the screen comes up, as in every real level. `worldStart` already gives 2-1 for every World 2 level. Once World 2 is built, the first bullet's last sentence ("a new run that 'Start' plays with seven") holds for World 1's saved run, but "Start" itself goes on to World 2 (GDD-UI-02, World 2 bullet; that needs the user's confirmation, Open Question 30).
- Assist mode (GDD-UI-03) can grant infinite lives, marked as such in the save.

---

## 4. Movement

All numbers are initial tuning targets, to be adjusted by playtest. Code mirrors them in `src/config/tuning.ts` (GDD-TEC-05). Values are chosen so that a full jump peaks at about 56 px (3.5 tiles) and covers about 66 to 68 px (4.1 to 4.3 tiles) horizontally at full run speed, level ground to level ground. The range is the landing resolution: one 60 Hz step at full speed is 2 px. The figures include the apex hang and the terminal velocity cap (GDD-MOV-04). They are measured by the simulation tests (GDD-TEC-05), not just calculated.

### GDD-MOV-01 Run

Status: Proposed

- Max run speed: 120 px/s (7.5 tiles/s).
- Acceleration on ground: 800 px/s². Deceleration (no input): 1000 px/s². Turn-around (opposite input): 1600 px/s².
- Air acceleration: 600 px/s². Air deceleration: 300 px/s².
- Speed is analog on gamepad and touch stick if supported (GDD-CTL-02, GDD-CTL-03), digital on keyboard.

### GDD-MOV-02 Jump

Status: Proposed

- Takeoff velocity: 410 px/s upwards. Max height holding jump: 56 px (3.5 tiles).
- Variable jump: releasing jump while rising multiplies upward velocity by 0.4 (minimum hop about 15 px, 0.9 tile: the takeoff step already rises about 6.6 px before a release can register, and cutting the remaining speed adds about 8 px).
- Jump is on button press (not release). Holding after landing does not rejump.

### GDD-MOV-03 Input forgiveness

Status: Proposed

- Coyote time: 100 ms after leaving a ledge, jump is still allowed.
- Jump buffer: 100 ms. A jump pressed up to 100 ms before landing executes on landing.
- Corner correction (built, `FORGIVENESS.cornerPx` in `src/config/tuning.ts`): while rising, if his head clips a block corner with only one side of his body inside the block, and by 4 px or less, he is moved sideways by exactly that overlap, past the corner, and keeps his upward speed. A clip of more than 4 px, or a block over both sides of his body (a real ceiling), is a head bump as before. It never applies while falling.
- Stomp bounce buffer: jump pressed within 100 ms of a stomp gives the high bounce (GDD-CMB-01).
- On sand slopes (GDD-MOV-09) coyote time and the jump buffer work as on flat ground, and corner correction is unchanged.

### GDD-MOV-04 Gravity and falling

Status: Proposed

- Gravity rising: 1500 px/s². Gravity falling: 2250 px/s² (1.5 times rising). Terminal velocity: 360 px/s.
- Apex hang: whenever the vertical speed magnitude is below 40 px/s, whichever gravity applies (rising or falling) is multiplied by 0.5, for a floatier peak. Order per step: apply gravity, clamp to terminal velocity, then move using the average of the old and new vertical velocity (and likewise horizontally). That is exact for constant acceleration, so the figures in this section do not depend on the step rate.
- Fixed timestep 60 Hz (GDD-TEC-02), so all values are deterministic.

### GDD-MOV-05 Cat reflexes

Status: Proposed

- No fall damage from height. Falling only hurts through pits or hazards.
- Landing squash animation 80 ms, does not block input.
- Respawn animation always ends with a feet-first landing (GDD-LIFE-02). Built as the flip described in GDD-LIFE-02.
- Air control is generous (GDD-MOV-01); on a turn mid-air the sprite flips instantly.

### GDD-MOV-06 Size, hitbox and tile

Status: Proposed. Based on the reference images (GDD-ART-02); validate against the real sprite sheet.

- Tile size: 16 px.
- Sprite frame: 40 x 40 game pixels, drawn as 160 x 160 art pixels (GDD-ART-01), Proposed: Poncho is the user's drawing at 2 art pixels per pixel, about 36 game pixels tall with the hat, so a 32 x 32 frame is too small. The frame is only for drawing; the boxes below decide what he touches. Poncho is a chibi cat about 2.5 heads tall with a very wide sombrero (the brim is about twice the width of the body, as in the sprite references, GDD-ART-02).
- Body hitbox (ground and walls): **12 px wide, 32 px tall (Decided, user, 2026-10-25: "most of hat", so his head and hat do not come through the stones; only the top of the crown and the ear tip are outside it)**, centred at the feet. It walks under a gap exactly 2 tiles high, as tall as he is, and not under one 1 tile high (checked by tests in `src/sim/collision.test.ts`). It was 12 x 14 until the sprite went into the game, then briefly 12 x 20; growing it left the level limits of GDD-WLD-03 as they were (re-measured, 2026-10-25); the claw box grew with it (GDD-CMB-02), and the sandbox's spring ledge and both piñatas moved up a tile (GDD-WLD-05, GDD-PWR-05). On a sand slope only the middle of his feet stands on it, and the rest of the box passes into the slope on the uphill side (GDD-MOV-09).
- Hurtbox (enemies and hazards): 10 x 30 px, inside the body hitbox, so near-misses feel fair. It is centred in the body box: 1 px in from each side, 1 px above the feet and 1 px below the top (Proposed).
- Level edges: the left and right edges of a level are solid walls, so Poncho cannot walk out. The sky above is open, so he can jump above the top row. Below the bottom row is open space, a pit (GDD-WLD-05).
- The sombrero brim is visual only. The crown top is the head-bump point for piñatas (GDD-PWR-05).

### GDD-MOV-07 Poncho glide

Status: Proposed. Post-MVP (GDD-MVP-02), unlocked by GDD-PRG-01.

Hold jump while falling: the poncho spreads like a parachute. Fall speed capped at 70 px/s, horizontal speed unchanged. Lasts as long as the button is held. Cannot be started within 150 ms of a stomp bounce.

- **Built (unlock flag, start and look decided by the user 2026-10-22, details Proposed):** an ability flag on the level turns the glide on (`abilities` in `src/sim/run.ts`); the sandbox level has it on so it can be played until clearing World 2 unlocks it (GDD-PRG-01). Holding jump (any jump key or the touch Jump button) while falling glides: a jump held from takeoff glides as soon as he starts to fall, and a press while already falling (off a ledge, or after letting go) glides too. While gliding the fall speed is capped at 70 px/s (a slower fall speeds up to it as usual), and running and steering in the air are as in any fall. Letting go ends it and he falls as usual; pressing again glides again. Landing, on tiles or a moving platform, ends it.
- **Start after the jump buffer (Proposed):** a glide starts only once jump has been held for more than 0.1 s (6 steps, the jump buffer of GDD-MOV-03), so a press just before landing is still a buffered jump and does not glide him past the window. A jump held from takeoff is long past that at the top. The hold counts from a press the game saw: a jump still held after a respawn, or pressed while paused and held through the resume (GDD-UI-02), does not glide until it is pressed again; a glide held through a pause carries on.
- **Stomp lock:** a stomp bounce (GDD-CMB-01) starts a 150 ms lock (9 steps) in which a glide cannot start; a spring launch has none (it rises far longer than that). `GLIDE` in `src/config/tuning.ts`, `src/sim/movement.ts`.
- **Look (placeholder):** a bar 28 x 4 px in red and cream serape stripes 4 px wide, just above the body, drawn while gliding. Not built yet: the glide sprite and sound.
- **With World 2 (Proposed, not built yet):** besides an ability flag on a test level, the unlock that 2-4's first clear saves turns the glide on in real levels (GDD-PRG-01, Open Question 29). An awning bounce (GDD-WLD-10) starts no stomp lock, like a spring launch; a high bounce held through its peak glides by itself, since jump has been held for more than 6 steps, and landing on an awning while gliding is a landing: the glide ends and a high bounce follows, since jump is held. World 2's levels are designed without it.

### GDD-MOV-08 Wall cling and kick

Status: Proposed. Post-MVP (GDD-MVP-02), unlocked by GDD-PRG-01.

Cat claws: pressing toward a wall while falling slows the slide to 60 px/s. Jump from the wall kicks away at 130 px/s horizontal and 380 px/s upward. 120 ms of locked horizontal control after the kick.

- **Built (kick timing, glide priority, grace and look decided by the user 2026-10-23, details Proposed):** the same ability flag as the glide turns it on (`abilities.wallCling`); the sandbox has it on, and the level's left and right edges are full-height walls to try it on, until clearing World 3 unlocks it (GDD-PRG-01). A wall counts when it is flush beside his body box (running into one snaps him flush, GDD-MOV-06).
- **Cling:** in the air, falling, pressing toward a wall he touches (the left or right button, key or stick that way), the fall is capped at 60 px/s (a slower fall speeds up to it). Not while rising, not pressing away or nothing, not on the ground. Landing, on tiles or a moving platform, or a stomp bounce ends it. With the glide on too, the cling wins: against the wall pressing toward it he slides even with jump held, and glides once off it.
- **Kick:** a jump press (one the game saw: a jump held through a respawn or a pause does not count, GDD-UI-02) while he touches a wall in the air, rising or falling, pressing toward it or not, kicks him away from it at 130 px/s across and 380 px/s up, facing away. It also works within 0.1 s (the coyote time, GDD-MOV-03) of leaving the wall, once per touch. A press that is a normal jump (on the ground, or in coyote time after walking off a ledge) stays a normal jump. While he rises from a spring launch there is no kick: that launch is fixed once it starts (GDD-WLD-05; user decision, 2026-10-23); falling again, he can kick. The sandbox spring is right against the 2-tile wall, where this matters. For 120 ms (7 steps) after the kick, sideways input does nothing and he keeps the kick's speed. Releasing jump while rising cuts the kick like a jump (GDD-MOV-02). `WALL` in `src/config/tuning.ts`, `src/sim/movement.ts`.
- **With World 2 (Proposed, not built yet):** while he rises from an awning bounce (GDD-WLD-10) a wall kick works, unlike a spring launch, since the bounce is steerable; an awning's edge and a papel picado string (GDD-WLD-11) are no wall to cling to or kick off.
- **Look (placeholder):** three short pale claw marks, 2 px wide, slanting down the half of the body next to the wall, while sliding. The level's edges are not drawn as walls, so in the sandbox he seems to cling to the edge of the picture. Not built yet: the cling sprite and sound.

### GDD-MOV-09 Sand slopes

Status: Proposed (user, 2026-11-02: "Add sand slopes too" to World 1's levels; "Ramps you run on": "Poncho runs up and down at his usual speed, his feet stay on the slope going down, and he jumps from it as from flat ground. Enemies walk on them too."; "Two angles": "45° (1 tile up per tile across) and a gentle 22.5° (1 tile up per 2 across)", the gentle one corrected by the user the same day to "1 up per 2 across", about 26.6°; the art: "Extend the desert tiles", "the same approved sand and rock, cut along the slope by the same script, shown to you in screenshots before it lands", and the look approved from those screenshots: "Approved"; three choices put to the user, the speed across a slope rather than along it, no launch off a ramp's top and nothing inside a slope tile: "All three OK". Everything else, the details of those three choices, the numbers, the level characters and the slopes level included, is Proposed.)

World 1's signature element (GDD-WLD-02): ramps of sand in the ground that Poncho, the walking enemies and the walking taco follow, at 45° (a tile up per tile across) and a gentle slope of a tile up per two tiles across (about 26.6°; user, 2026-11-02: "1 up per 2 across", correcting the "22.5°" first put to them, which no whole number of tiles gives).

- **Slope tiles in a level's rows (GDD-TEC-04):** besides `#` (a level tile, solid) and `.` (empty), six characters are slope tiles. A slope tile is solid below its surface line and empty above it. The surface runs straight across the tile between these heights, in px up from the tile's bottom edge (a tile is 16 px):

  | Char | Slope | Left edge | Right edge |
  | --- | --- | --- | --- |
  | `/` | 45°, rising | 0 | 16 |
  | `\` | 45°, falling | 16 | 0 |
  | `r` | gentle, rising, lower half | 0 | 8 |
  | `R` | gentle, rising, upper half | 8 | 16 |
  | `F` | gentle, falling, upper half | 16 | 8 |
  | `f` | gentle, falling, lower half | 8 | 0 |

  A 45° rise of one tile is `/` with a `#` after it on the same row; a gentle one is `rR`; a dune is `rR##Ff`. The tile directly under a slope tile is a level tile (`#`), so a slope never hangs in the air or sits in the bottom row. A slope tile holds nothing else (user, 2026-11-02: "All three OK"; details Proposed): no spike, spring, crumbling tile, door, fragile wall or piñata. The level validator checks both, the level tile under it and nothing in it (GDD-TEC-04). A slope tile has no slope or level tile directly above it, unless the level means a wall there (a rule for writing a level's rows; the validator does not check it). In the slopes level (below) a slope's high end meets level ground or the next slope at its height, and its low end meets level ground at its height or the edge of a pit. In 1-2 (GDD-WLD-08) one ramp's high end is a gap's edge (column 25), two ramps' high ends meet crumbling tiles at their height (columns 51 and 104), and one ramp's low end meets the side of a 1-tile block (column 167, beside the block at 166), a dip 16 px deep. In 1-3 (GDD-WLD-09) the high ends of the two 45° ramps (columns 157 and 163) meet its crumbling bridge at its height, and once the bridge has crumbled they meet no ground; its other slopes, a 45° hill and a gentle dune, meet level ground or the next slope at their height at both ends. A slope's high end that meets no ground is a ledge from above and a wall from below (below).
- **Standing:** on a slope only the middle of his feet stands on it: the middle of the bottom edge of the 12 x 32 body box, his position (GDD-MOV-06). The rest of the box ignores slope tiles, so on the uphill side it passes into the slope, 6 px at the box's edge on a 45° slope and 3 px on a gentle one. He stands still on a slope and never slides down it. Landing on a slope puts the middle of his feet on its surface (measured by `src/sim/slopes.test.ts`).
- **Level tiles beside a slope:** while the middle of his feet is in a slope tile, level tiles meet his box only above the slope's surface under his feet, lowered by as much as the slope rises under half the box's width (6 px on a 45° slope, 3 px on a gentle one). So at a slope's top his uphill corner passes into the tile that carries the ground on, while a wall or a ceiling still stops him, and a wall one tile high at a slope's foot stops his box flush against it (measured by `src/sim/slopes.test.ts`). The same holds for the boxes of the walking enemies, the taco and the fireballs, by their own widths.
- **Running at his usual speed (speed across, user, 2026-11-02: "All three OK"; details Proposed):** on a slope, uphill and downhill, his speed across (along x) is the run of GDD-MOV-01 exactly as on flat ground: up to 120 px/s, with the same acceleration, deceleration and turn-around. A slope neither slows him nor speeds him up across; along the slope itself he covers more ground (1.4 times as much on a 45° slope). Crossing the slopes level's 45° hill (two tiles up, two flat, two down, 96 px across) at full speed takes 48 steps, as long as 96 px of flat ground (measured by `src/levels/slopes.test.ts`).
- **Feet kept on the ground (4 px, Proposed):** on the ground he steps up and down by up to 4 px in one step without leaving it. The level tile under a slope counts as ground whose top is that slope's surface, so feet that cross from one slope tile into the tile under the next slope go straight onto it. Running down a slope his feet are put back on it every step, and he runs from flat ground onto a slope and off it at both ends, over a peak and through a valley, on the ground every step (measured by `src/sim/slopes.test.ts` and the slopes level's route). A rise of more than 4 px in one step is a wall: a slope's high side, 8 or 16 px tall, stops the middle of his feet. A drop of more than 4 px is a fall, as off a ledge or into a pit. At the run speed a 45° slope rises or drops 2 px a step (3 px at the Chile Fantasma's 180 px/s, GDD-PWR-04), so 4 px covers every speed he runs at. In the air he is never pulled down onto a slope, and a step up lifts him only when the middle of his feet has moved into a level tile (the tile at a slope's top, so a jump taken there keeps his speed), never while it is in a slope tile, so a jump from a slope rises as from flat ground.
- **Walls and ceilings:** a slope that runs up into a wall stops him with his box against the wall and his feet on the slope; one that runs down into a wall stops him flush against it on the slope; one that runs up under a ceiling stops him where his head meets the ceiling, never pushing him back (measured by `src/sim/slopes.test.ts`).
- **Jumping (GDD-MOV-02):** a jump from a slope is a jump from flat ground: the same 410 px/s takeoff and the same peak above the point he takes off from as on flat ground, standing or running, uphill or downhill (about 56 px for a full jump), and a short hop is as high as on flat ground too (measured by `src/sim/slopes.test.ts`). The one exception is a takeoff whose first step carries the middle of his feet out of a slope tile into a level tile (the tile at the slope's top, or the tile under the next slope up): that step lifts him onto the ground's top there, by what the slope rises in that step, at most 2 px at the run speed, so the jump peaks that much higher (measured at a ramp's top by `src/sim/slopes.test.ts`). Running off a ramp's high end where no ground follows, he falls as off a ledge: no launch (user, 2026-11-02: "All three OK"; details Proposed).
- **Input forgiveness (GDD-MOV-03):** coyote time and the jump buffer work on slopes as on flat ground: running off a slope's end into the air leaves the same 0.1 s to jump as running off a ledge, and a jump pressed up to 0.1 s before landing on a slope takes off on landing. Corner correction is unchanged on a jump from a slope (measured by `src/sim/slopes.test.ts`).
- **Wall cling (GDD-MOV-08):** a slope is no wall to cling to or kick off; only level tiles are (only the sandbox turns the cling on, and it has no slopes).
- **Crumbling tiles (GDD-WLD-05):** at the top of a slope, a crumbling tile starts its warning only once he stands on its top, not while his feet are still on the slope beside it (measured by `src/sim/slopes.test.ts`).
- **Moving platforms and moving blocks (GDD-WLD-05):** they ignore slopes, and a level keeps their paths clear of slopes. The level validator does not check that; a level that has both checks it in its own tests (the slopes level and 1-2 have no moving platform or block; 1-3 checks its three platforms and four blocks, GDD-WLD-09).
- **Enemies (GDD-ENM-02):** the Alacrán and the Tlacuache walk, and the Tlacuache charges, up and down slopes with the middle of their feet on the surface, by the same 4 px step. A slope's high side turns them like a wall. They turn at a ledge where the ground under the front edge of their box ends: no level tile and no slope in the row of their feet or the row below. Their box, and the Tlacuache's sight (his feet within 16 px of its own), go by where their feet are now, not where they started; a lost life puts them back at their start. The Zopilote ignores tiles; the cactus buddy does not move.
- **Items (GDD-PWR-02, GDD-PWR-03):** a walking taco follows slopes up and down the same way, turns at a slope's high side, and still falls off ledges. A fireball bounces off a slope straight up at the speed it bounces off flat ground (about 245 px/s, a 20 px arc), bounces on up a slope and over the tile at its top, and vanishes at a slope's high side as at a wall. Only a slope lifts a fireball: one that meets the side of a level tile near its top still vanishes (measured by `src/sim/slopes.test.ts`).
- **Gaps (GDD-WLD-03, GDD-TEC-04):** a gap is measured as before, by the columns whose bottom row is empty. The level tile under a slope fills its column, so a slope beside a gap does not change its width: the slopes level's gap, between a falling and a rising slope, is 3 tiles (measured by `src/levels/slopes.test.ts`).
- **Test place, the slopes level (Proposed):** a one-screen World 1 test level, 30 x 17 tiles, opened with `?level=slopes` (GDD-UI-02). Like the sandbox it has three ofrendas and no exit and is never saved, and it has no hilos; the sandbox itself has no room left for slopes, and levels 1-2 and 1-3 (GDD-WLD-08, GDD-WLD-09) bring them into play. The floor's top is row 14 (y = 224). Left to right: the spawn at column 3; a 45° hill two tiles high, `/` at columns 4 and 5 up to a top at columns 6 and 7 (y = 192), `\` at 8 and 9; the first ofrenda at column 11; a gentle dune one tile high, `rR` at 12 and 13 up to a top at 14 to 16 (y = 208) with the second ofrenda at column 15, `Ff` at 17 and 18; the third ofrenda at column 19; a 45° bump, `/\` at 20 and 21; a gap at columns 22 to 24; a second bump, `/\` at 25 and 26; flat ground to the right edge. An Alacrán starts on top of the hill (column 7) and walks from the level's left edge to the gap's edge (x = 7 to 345), over the hill, the dune and the first bump; a Tlacuache starts against the right edge (column 29) and walks the second bump (x = 408 to 472) (measured by `src/levels/slopes.test.ts`). With the enemies taken out, holding right from the spawn he runs over every slope on the ground at full speed, and a jump from the falling side of the first bump crosses the gap: taken at x = 341 it lands at x = 409 on the second bump (measured by `src/levels/slopes.test.ts`). `src/levels/slopes.ts`.
- **Look (GDD-ART-06):** the approved ground tiles cut along the slope (GDD-ART-06, Slope tiles), approved from screenshots (user, 2026-11-02: "Approved"; `docs/screenshots/sand-slopes/`).
- `SLOPE` in `src/config/tuning.ts`, `src/sim/slope.ts`, `src/sim/tilemap.ts`.
