A crusher crushes Poncho standing under it as it comes down (97 steps after they start for the ones at columns 64 and 120, 49 steps after they leave their top for the corridor's) and never one standing beside it, on the floor or, at column 120, on the Alacrán pen's block; between the corridor's two crushers, at the key, he is safe (measured by `src/levels/level1-3.test.ts`). No platform's or crusher's path crosses a sand slope (GDD-MOV-09; measured by `src/levels/level1-3.test.ts`).
- **Enemies (GDD-ENM-02):** one cactus buddy (column 22), three Tlacuaches (on the dune from 72, behind the third fragile wall from 145, on the last cliff from 177), one Zopilote (cruising at y = 112 from 108) and one Alacrán (from 117, behind the second fragile wall). The Zopilote spots Poncho waiting on the step for the shuttle, where his hurtbox centre is 64 px below its cruise line (measured by `src/levels/level1-3.test.ts`). While Poncho is away each walker keeps to its stretch: the dune's Tlacuache from x = 1096 to 1272, over the dune and the floor round it, the Alacrán from 1863 to 1897, in its pen, the Tlacuache behind the third fragile wall from 2312 to 2344, and the one on the last cliff from 2712 to 2872 (measured by `src/levels/level1-3.test.ts`).
- **The enemies' own walks keep clear of the moving blocks** (the main session's choice, 2026-11-02, Proposed: an Alacrán walking through a solid stone block looks wrong; narrowed to the enemies' walks as placed by the user's "Accept both", 2026-11-02): enemies pass through moving blocks (GDD-WLD-05), so 1-3 places them where none walks or flies through a crusher's column. Each of its six enemies, while Poncho is away (every door shut and every fragile wall whole), keeps clear of all four crushers' columns, and so does the Alacrán once its fragile wall is broken (it then walks out as far as the far wall and back to its pen's block, from x = 1831 to 1897; measured by `src/levels/level1-3.test.ts`). The Tlacuache let out of the third pen is the exception (below).
- **Known and accepted (user, 2026-11-02: "Accept both"; kept as known quirks, their details Proposed):**
  - *A second way into the Alacrán's pen, past a fragile-wall lesson.* The second fragile wall stands one floor column past the shuttle's far wall, whose top is two tiles below the fragile wall's, so from the far wall's right end a full jump clears the fragile wall and lands in the pen (at x = 1884.6, 39 steps after the jump), the wall still whole: a second way in beside the claw from the floor. The Alacrán stays penned that way, since the wall stays (measured by `src/levels/level1-3.test.ts`).
  - *The Tlacuache let out of the third pen.* If Poncho opens the door at column 140, breaks the third fragile wall (column 143) and moves on leaving its Tlacuache alive, the Tlacuache walks out left through the open door, through the corridor and its two crushers and into the column of the crusher at 120, as far as the block of the Alacrán's pen (x = 1928): the one enemy whose walk then crosses moving blocks. It takes a player who skips it, and Poncho is past it by then (measured by `src/levels/level1-3.test.ts`).
- **Piñata (GDD-PWR-05):** in Base a jump from under it opens it, the taco drops to the floor, and holding right once it has dropped catches it. In Taco or Fuego a chile comes out instead and stays on its top, where a claw from the right end of the 2-tile wall opens it and a hop from there takes the chile, as in 1-1 (measured in Base and in Taco by `src/levels/level1-3.test.ts`).
- **Hilos:** 50 (GDD-WLD-04), in trails along the path: on the floor at the start, over the hill, either side of the first key, over the cactus buddy and the first gap, either side of the first fragile wall, on the 2-tile wall, past the piñata, up the first lift and on the first cliff, under the first crusher, over the pen's block and on the dune, past the second door and over the second gap, before the second ofrenda, on the step, along the shuttle's path, one on the veladora ledge, on the far wall and before the second fragile wall, in the Alacrán's pen and past its crusher, before the corridor, along it and either side of its key, past the third door, in the third pen and over its wall, up the ramp and over the crumbling bridge, up the last lift and on the last cliff. Each is in an empty tile within a jump of the ground (a level tile or a sand slope) in its column or within two columns of it, and none is touched by Poncho standing at the start or at an ofrenda (measured by `src/levels/level1-3.test.ts`).
- **Hilos in play:** holding right from the start, with no jump, collects the first three hilos ahead of him, one on the floor and two on the hill, by the 60th step (1 s) (measured by `src/levels/level1-3.test.ts`).
- **Played through:** from a fresh start with seven lives, holding right, jumping at each wall, gap, spike, cactus buddy and closed piñata, clawing each fragile wall and each Alacrán, Tlacuache or Zopilote just ahead, waiting for each lift and the shuttle and riding them, and waiting before each crusher until it rises clear, reaches the exit losing no life on the way, in under 2400 steps (40 s), having used all three keys and collected 45 of the 50 hilos; after waiting 1, 2, 3 or 4 s at the start it still reaches the exit losing none (measured by `src/levels/level1-3.test.ts`). A player's first clear takes longer; whether it reaches the 2 to 4 minutes of GDD-LOOP-02 is for playtesting.
- **Plants (GDD-ART-06):** 16, of six kinds (no palm yucca), none on a sand slope or on the tile under one, and none in a crusher's column (the plant rule does not look at moving blocks, so the layout keeps them apart; checked by `src/ui/plants.test.ts`).
- Drawn with World 1's backdrop, ground and slope tiles (GDD-ART-06, GDD-MOV-09); its other things keep their placeholder art until theirs is drawn. The look approved from screenshots (user, 2026-11-03: "Approved"; `docs/screenshots/level1-3/`).

### GDD-WLD-10 Bouncy awnings (toldos)

Status: Proposed (user, 2026-11-04: "Design first, then build"). World 2's first signature element (GDD-WLD-02). Every rule and number below is Proposed; nothing is built yet. Numbers that follow from the simulation are targets, to be measured once it exists.

The cloth awnings over the market stalls work like trampolines: Poncho lands on one and is thrown up again. He never stands on one (Open Question 22).

- **Shape:** an awning lies in one tile row and covers 2 to 4 whole tiles. Only its top surface, the top edge of that row, acts, and it is flat: World 2 has no sloped awnings. The tiles of its row under it are empty (the level validator checks it, GDD-TEC-04). An awning is no level tile: it never fills a pit and never changes a gap's width (GDD-WLD-03).
- **One-way, like a moving platform (GDD-WLD-05):** Poncho passes through an awning from below and from the sides, so he can jump up through it; its underside never bumps his head and its sides never stop him. He lands on it by the platform rule: his feet end a step at or below its top after starting that step at or above it, his body box overlaps its columns, and standing there would not put his body box into anything solid above it.
- **The bounce:** in the step his feet reach its top, by falling onto it or by walking onto it from a roof at the same height, he is thrown straight up and keeps his speed across. Two strengths, chosen as a stomp's are (GDD-CMB-01):
  - **High bounce:** 520 px/s, if jump is held or was pressed in the 0.1 s buffer (6 steps, GDD-MOV-03). Target: it peaks about 90 px (5.6 tiles) above the awning's top.
  - **Low bounce:** 340 px/s otherwise. Target: it peaks about 39 px (2.4 tiles) above it, lower than a jump on purpose.
  - Releasing jump while rising cuts the bounce like a jump (GDD-MOV-02), so unlike a spring launch a bounce can be steered and shortened. A jump press while rising does not replace it, there is no extra jump in the air, and there is no coyote time off an awning, since he was never on the ground.
  - One step gives one bounce; a stomp in the same step gives the stomp's bounce instead (GDD-CMB-01).
- **He never rests on it:** left alone he keeps bouncing low, holding jump he keeps bouncing high, and to get off he steers off a side. Targets: a low bounce about once every 0.45 s and a high one about once every 0.7 s; at full run a high bounce back down to the same height carries him about 84 px (5.2 tiles) across, a low one about 54 px (3.4 tiles).
- **Against a spring (GDD-WLD-05):** a spring is a solid tile set flush into the floor, launches at 480 or 600 px/s for a timed press, and is fixed once launched; an awning is one-way cloth in mid-air, bounces at 340 or 520 px/s while jump is held or buffered, and its bounce is cut by releasing jump. Holding is easier for children than the spring's timed press, and the awning suits chains.
- **Claw, items and enemies:** the claw and fireballs pass through an awning (GDD-CMB-02, GDD-PWR-03); awnings never tear, break or crumble, and nothing about one resets when a life is lost. A walking taco passes through it. Walkers (the Tlacuache, GDD-ENM-02) never step onto an awning: its end is a ledge end to them, and they turn there. Flyers ignore awnings. A level keeps a piñata's drop and its taco's walk off awnings, and puts no enemy's start on one (the level tests check it). Hilos, keys and the veladora may float above awnings as rewards for a high bounce.
- **Enemies (GDD-ENM-03; Open Question 25):** an awning bounces only Poncho. No enemy steps or hops onto one: an awning is no landing spot for any enemy, so an awning beside a walker's path is a ledge end to it. (The Chihuahua's hop peaks about 16 px up, level with the lowest awning, 2-3's at row 13, and below every other one; the rule holds whatever an awning's height.)
- **Glide and wall cling:** a bounce starts no glide lock (GDD-MOV-07), and a wall kick works while rising from one; an awning's edge is no wall (GDD-MOV-08).
- **Placeholder look (GDD-UI-03):** a cloth slab 8 px tall at the top of its row, in vertical stripes 4 px wide of teal and cream, with a scalloped front edge (half-circles 4 px wide) hanging 3 px below and its underside in shadow terracotta, and no poles, since many awnings hang in mid-air or over spikes. On each bounce its middle dips 2 px for 3 steps and springs back, so it shows by movement, not only colour. Teal is also the moving platform's colour, so the stripes and the scallops tell them apart (Open Question 32). Every awning has the same colours, so the signal can be learned. The final look is in GDD-ART-06 (World 2).
- **Sound (GDD-AUD-02):** until its own sound exists, the spring's sound played an octave lower.
- **Teaching (GDD-WLD-03):**
  1. **Safe spot (2-1, GDD-WLD-12):** an awning 2 tiles above the market street in front of a building 5 tiles tall, 48 px above the awning's top: a jump from the street cannot climb it, nor can a low bounce, and a hop onto the awning holding jump bounces him onto its roof (targets). A miss drops him back to the street with no life lost. Hilos trace the high bounce's arc.
  2. **With a threat (2-1):** awnings over spikes, so steering off one costs a life. Letting go of jump, a low bounce that stays under a threat a high one rises into, is taught in 2-3 under the Avispas (GDD-WLD-14).
  3. **Test (2-1):** a staircase of three awnings, each 48 px higher than the last, over spikes, taken in one held chain.
- `AWNING = { bounce: 340, boostBounce: 520, dipPx: 2, dipSteps: 3 }` in `src/config/tuning.ts` and `src/sim/awning.ts` when built.

### GDD-WLD-11 Papel picado strings (cordeles)

Status: Proposed (user, 2026-11-04: "Design first, then build"). World 2's second signature element (GDD-WLD-02). The role chosen and every rule and number below are Proposed; nothing is built yet. Numbers that follow from the simulation are targets.

A stout cord strung with papel picado flags runs across the street from roof to roof. Poncho walks it like a cat on a washing line, and it sags under him (Open Question 23).

- **Why this role:** it is a new verb that fits a cat, balancing on a line, with no new button (the controls have no "down" or "grab", GDD-CTL-01), and it works across the level while the awnings work up and down. Grab-and-swing would need a grab input and overlaps World 4's vines; bridges the claw cuts would be the papel picado fragile walls' verb on the same material (GDD-WLD-05); a plain one-way platform is the moving platform already. The sag gives strings their own identity.
- **Shape and anchors:** a string lies along the top edge of one tile row, r, and spans 2 to 8 whole columns, c0 to c1. Each end is anchored: the tiles at (c0 − 1, r) and (c1 + 1, r) are level tiles, so at rest the cord is flush with the roofs either side and Poncho walks from roof to cord to roof without a seam. The tiles of row r under the span are empty and hold nothing (no spike, spring, piñata, crumbling tile, door or fragile wall; the validator checks it, GDD-TEC-04). A string is always level in World 2, and it is no level tile, so it never fills a pit; it may span a gap within the world's limit (GDD-WLD-03).
- **One-way:** a one-way floor like a moving platform (GDD-WLD-05). He jumps up through it from below and lands on it by the platform rule, measured against the cord's height under his feet at that moment. Its sides and underside never block him, and nothing on it can crush him.
- **The sag:** while his feet are on the cord it bends into a V with its lowest point under the middle of his feet. With t his place along the span (0 at one end, 1 at the other), the dip there is maxDip × 4t(1 − t), where maxDip is 2 px per tile of span, at most 12 px: a 4-tile string dips 8 px, one of 6 tiles or more 12 px. The dip moves towards that by at most 2 px a step, and he is carried down and up with it as a platform carries him. When he leaves, it straightens at 2 px a step, with a visual wobble of ±1 px over 0.5 s. It depends only on where he is and on the last steps, so it is the same on every run, and it never goes below row r's top 12 px.
- **What the sag does:** a jump from a sagging cord starts from the dipped point, and there is no launch when he leaves it (only awnings throw). Target: from the middle of a 6-tile string a full jump peaks 12 px lower than from its ends, so a roof 48 px above the cord is climbed from near its ends but not from the middle (about 44 px of rise against 48). That teaches "jump from near the ends" with no hazard.
- **Strings never snap.** Coyote time (GDD-MOV-03) applies where a cord's end meets the air, which never comes up in World 2, since every string is anchored. Strings one above another, 48 px apart, make a ladder he jumps up through, landing on each in turn (target: a full jump from near a cord's end reaches the next cord, a tight margin, GDD-WLD-13).
- **Claw, items and enemies:** the claw and fireballs pass through a string, which is never cut (GDD-CMB-02, GDD-PWR-03). Walking tacos and every enemy ignore strings: no walker treats a cord as ground, and no enemy perches on one (Palomas perch on roofs and post tops beside them, GDD-ENM-03). A level keeps piñata drops and enemy starts off strings. Hilos may hang from a cord, one per tile at most, 8 px under its top, collected by touch as usual and drawn in front of its flags. A lost life puts every cord back straight.
- **Against the papel picado fragile walls (GDD-WLD-05):** a fragile wall is a solid 16 x 16 pink block, solid on every side, that the claw breaks; a string is a thin line with flags in four colours, passed through from below, that the claw passes through. The rule to keep: the claw breaks tiles, never lines. The fragile walls keep their look in World 2 (Open Question 23).
- **Placeholder look (GDD-UI-03):** a dark cord 1 px thick, with flags 8 x 10 px, one every 8 px, in pink, gold, teal and blue in turn (the Mercado palette, GDD-ART-06), each with a cut-out pattern of holes 1 px wide that show the backdrop and a zig-zag lower edge. Under load the cord is drawn as two straight lines, from each anchor to his feet, with the flags hanging from them. Each anchor is a small hook on the roof tile's edge. The flags are scenery: touching them does nothing. These are the only flags with cut-outs, twice the size of the backdrop's (GDD-ART-06).
- **Sound (GDD-AUD-02):** none yet; later a soft creak while sagging.
- **Teaching (GDD-WLD-03), in 2-2 (GDD-WLD-13):**
  1. **Safe spot:** a 4-tile string between two roofs over the open street, so a fall costs only time.
  2. **With a threat:** a 6-tile string over a pen with a Chihuahua that cannot reach it, then a string on a Paloma's level, so Poncho jumps its flight from the sagging cord.
  3. **Test:** a ladder of three strings up a shaft, then a 4-tile string over a 4-tile pit under Avispas.
- `PAPEL = { dipPerTile: 2, maxDip: 12, dipRate: 2 }` in `src/config/tuning.ts` and `src/sim/papel.ts` when built.

### GDD-WLD-12 Level 2-1 "Los toldos"

Status: Proposed (user, 2026-11-04: "Design first, then build"). The layout, the name and every number are Proposed; nothing is built yet. Figures that follow from the simulation are targets, to be measured by `src/levels/level2-1.test.ts`.

The grid is World 1's: 16 px tiles, 17 rows (0 to 16), and a market-street floor whose top is row 14 (y = 224) wherever there is a street. Roofs are solid columns from their roof row down to row 16. A pit is what the validator means: columns whose bottom row is empty, here alleys with no street. The same holds for 2-2 and 2-3 (GDD-WLD-13, GDD-WLD-14), with four rules for all three (Proposed):

- The world's gap limit is 4 tiles (GDD-WLD-03): 2-1 keeps to 3 as a gentle step down from 1-3, 2-2 brings in the 4-tile pit and 2-3 has four.
- Missing a beat costs time, not a life, unless the beat is a test: every stretch of street a fall reaches has a way back up within the same screen, an awning, a stall step or a cord to jump up through (Open Question 33).
- Height bands: a climb that needs the high bounce puts its target 48 to 64 px above the awning's top (out of the low bounce's reach, cleared by the high one with room); a climb the low bounce may make is at most 16 px; a cord reached from an awning is at most 64 px above its top.
- The levels are designed and validated without the glide and the wall cling (GDD-PRG-01), and their level tests also pass with every ability on (`npm run test:abilities`).

- **Place in the game:** World 2's first level (GDD-WLD-01). Once World 2 is complete, "Next level" on 1-4's results menu starts it, always with seven lives (GDD-LIFE-05, World 2 bullet); "Retry" starts it again with the lives at its exit, and "Next level" starts 2-2 with them; running out of lives in any World 2 level restarts here with seven. `?level=2-1` opens it with seven. It has one tip, the awning tip, over the first awning (GDD-UI-05). The glide and the wall cling are off on the first run (GDD-PRG-01). `src/levels/level2-1.ts`.
- **Size:** 150 x 17 tiles (5 screens, 2400 x 272 px), one screen shorter than 1-3, as a gentler first level for the world (Open Question 33). Poncho starts on the street at column 3. The exit stands at column 144 on the last roof (row 8, its foot at y = 128): with the camera at the right edge it is on screen from 384 to 400 px, left of the phone's Jump button. The street runs the whole width but for one alley pit of 3 tiles (columns 113 to 115).
- **Teaching order (GDD-WLD-03):** the awnings first, then the Paloma, then the Chihuahua, each in a safe spot, then with a threat, then in a test.
  - **Screen one (columns 0 to 29), the awning's safe spot:** a 1-tile block (8) and a stall counter 2 tiles tall (12 to 14) warm up; then awning A1 (18 to 20, row 12, top y = 192) in front of a building 5 tiles tall (21 to 30, roof row 9, y = 144, 48 px above A1). A jump from the street cannot climb it (80 px), nor can a low bounce; a hop onto A1 holding jump bounces him onto its roof (targets). A miss drops him back to the street. Five hilos trace the high bounce's arc, and the awning tip shows here. A building 3 tiles tall (31 to 34, row 11) steps him back down to the street.
  - **Screen two (30 to 59), the Paloma in a safe spot:** the first ofrenda (37, on the street); the first piñata (43, row 10); awning A2 (45 to 47, row 12) in front of a low roof (49 to 63, row 11, y = 176). The first Paloma perches at column 52 on that roof: from the street Poncho is 48 px below its level, so it never spots him (GDD-ENM-03), and a high bounce off A2 steered right comes down onto it, a stomp from above (target). A jump from the street onto the roof also works, but lands him on its level 3 tiles away, and it flies at him: the awning is the safe way. A second Paloma perches at column 61 on the same roof, spots him on its level at 80 px and flies at him, which teaches the standing jump over it.
  - **Screen three (60 to 88), awnings with a threat:** past the low roof, ten spikes on the street (64 to 73, row 13) under two awnings, A3 (64 to 66) and A4 (69 to 71), both row 12. Poncho drops onto A3 from the roof's end, 16 px down; bounces carry him to A4 (from A3's middle to A4's start is about 56 px, against a low bounce's reach of about 54 px across with only an overlap needed: target, tight), and from A4 one bounce takes him onto a low roof 16 px up (74 to 80, row 11). Bouncing, his feet never go below y = 192, so the spikes hurt only if he steers off. A building 2 tiles higher follows (81 to 88, roof row 9), with the second ofrenda at column 82, the second piñata above it and a 1-tile parapet at column 88.
  - **Screen four (89 to 117), the awning test, then the Paloma again:** a staircase of three awnings over spikes, A5 (90 to 92, row 12, top y = 192), A6 (94 to 96, row 9, top y = 144) and A7 (98 to 100, row 6, top y = 96), then a roof at row 3 (102 to 107, top y = 48): every step is 48 px, beyond the low bounce, so the chain is taken holding jump (targets). Then a drop to a roof at row 8 (108 to 112) with the third Paloma at column 110: Poncho comes down onto it from well above its 16 px band, so a stomp from above works again (target, rechecked with the staircase's rows). Then the alley pit of 3 tiles (113 to 115), jumped onto a low building (116 and 117, row 11).
  - **Screen five (118 to 149), the Chihuahua:** a street pen (118 to 123) between that low building and a 2-tile stall block (124 and 125) holds the first Chihuahua (120, 112 px from the third ofrenda, beyond its 96 px sight). Standing on the low building, 48 px above its feet, Poncho is spotted; it yaps and hops at him but cannot hop onto the wall, so it stays and yaps (GDD-ENM-03), and he watches its hop before he drops in to stomp or jump it. The third ofrenda (127, on the street); the second Chihuahua (134, on the street, beyond its 96 px sight from the ofrenda); the last awning, A8 (137 to 139, row 12), in front of the exit building (141 to 149, roof row 8, 64 px above A8). The second Chihuahua chases Poncho along the street toward A8; it runs on under A8, which it never hops onto (GDD-WLD-10) and whose top its hop stays under, to the exit building's wall at 141, and yaps there while he bounces up onto the exit roof, so the awning is a way out of a chase (target; its start is moved until the test sees it happen).
- **Secret (GDD-WLD-03):** the veladora floats left of A7 in plain view (Open Question 15), high enough that only A7's high bounce, steered left, reaches it, and neither the bounce steered right onto the roof nor a low bounce does (its row is set when the staircase is measured; target). Missing the way back down to A7 means the spikes, so it is a risk.
- **Ofrendas** at columns 37, 82 and 127 (24.7%, 54.7% and 84.7%). Every enemy starts at least 3 tiles from the start and from each ofrenda (GDD-ENM-01, the validator), and no Paloma or Chihuahua spots Poncho standing at a respawn (target).
- **Enemies (GDD-ENM-03):** three Palomas (52, 61, 110) and two Chihuahuas (120, 134). No Avispas and no Tlacuache.
- **Piñatas (GDD-PWR-05):** the first (column 43) gives a taco in Base that walks right on the street, under A2 (items pass through awnings), and turns at the low roof's wall (49); in Taco or Fuego its chile stays on top, where a high bounce off A2's left end steered left lands on it. The second (over the parapet roof) gives a taco that turns at the parapet and comes back, and a chile reached by a full jump from the parapet's top. All targets.
- **Hilos:** 50 (GDD-WLD-04), in trails along the path: on the street at the start (columns 4 to 6, collected by holding right with no jump by the 30th step), over the block and the stall, on A1's high-bounce arc, on the first roof, on the way to the first piñata, on the arc from A2, between the Palomas, over A3 and A4, on the low roof and the ofrenda roof, on the staircase's arcs, one beside the veladora, on the row-3 roof and the drop to the third Paloma, over the alley pit, over the pen, on A8's arc and on the exit roof.
- **Played through (target):** from a fresh start, holding right, holding jump at each awning, jumping each block, Paloma flight and Chihuahua, and stomping the Palomas from above, a bot reaches the exit losing no life in under 1800 steps (30 s).
- Drawn with World 2's backdrop, ground and scenery (GDD-ART-06, World 2).

### GDD-WLD-13 Level 2-2 "Los cordeles"

Status: Proposed (user, 2026-11-04: "Design first, then build"). The layout, the name (Open Question 31) and every number are Proposed; nothing is built yet. Figures that follow from the simulation are targets, to be measured by `src/levels/level2-2.test.ts`. The grid and the four rules of GDD-WLD-12 hold.

- **Place in the game:** World 2's second level. "Next level" from 2-1 starts it with the lives at 2-1's exit; "Retry" restarts it with the lives at its own exit, and "Next level" starts 2-3 with them. Out of lives, it restarts at 2-1 with seven. `?level=2-2` opens it with seven. It has one tip, the papel picado string tip, over the first string (GDD-UI-05). `src/levels/level2-2.ts`.
- **Size:** 180 x 17 tiles (6 screens). Poncho starts on a roof at column 3 (row 10, y = 160); the exit stands at column 174 on the last roof (row 10). Two pits of 4 tiles, the world's first (columns 49 to 52 and 161 to 164).
- **Teaching order:** the strings (GDD-WLD-11) and the Avispas are new, and the Chihuahua's chase becomes a threat under a string.
  - **Screen one (0 to 29), the string's safe spot:** string S1, 4 tiles (11 to 14, row 10), anchored on the start roof (0 to 10) and the next roof (15 to 22), sagging 8 px in its middle. Under it the open street lies 64 px down, with a stall 2 tiles tall (12 and 13), from whose top a full jump goes up through the cord onto it: "through from below" as the way back after a fall (target). The string tip shows here. The first piñata (20, row 6), then a 2-tile step up (23 to 29, row 8).
  - **Screen two (30 to 52), a string with a threat, then the first 4-tile pit:** string S2, 6 tiles (30 to 35, row 8), dipping 12 px, over a street pen walled by the roofs either side, with the first Chihuahua (34) and a small awning (30 and 31, row 12). On the cord Poncho is 84 px or more above the Chihuahua, out of its sight. Fallen in, he is chased, and a high bounce off the pen's awning takes him back up through the cord, which the Chihuahua cannot follow, so the awning is the pen's way out (target). The first ofrenda (45, on a roof from 36 to 48), then the first 4-tile pit (49 to 52), crossed by a running jump at the same height.
  - **Screens two and three (53 to 99), the Avispas, then a string with a threat:** the Avispas' home is at column 61 over their sweets stall on the roof (53 to 66, row 8). Poncho lands across the pit 112 px from it, out of its 64 px sight, and sees it gather (0.5 s) as he walks on; a fall back into the pit is not a likely way to meet it. He claws it or jumps it as it drifts (36 px/s). A 1-tile step down (67, row 9) anchors string S3 (68 to 73, row 9, 6 tiles), and a Paloma perches at column 77 on the far roof (74 to 82): on the cord he is on its level, the sag within its 16 px band, so it spots him at 80 px and flies along the cord's line, and he jumps it from the sagging cord (target: even from 12 px down a jump clears its 10 px body). Under S3 a street well with an awning (69 to 71, row 12) bounces him back up. Then a drop of 5 tiles to the street, the second piñata (85, row 10), the second Chihuahua (92) and the second ofrenda (99, on the street).
  - **Screen four (100 to 115), the string test, a ladder:** an arcade pillar hangs at column 102 (rows 5 to 11), the street passing under it (the *portales*). Beside it a shaft (103 to 107) holds three strings at rows 11, 8 and 5, each anchored between the pillar and the building on the right (108 to 115, roof row 5). A full jump from the street reaches the first cord; from near each cord's end a full jump reaches the next, 48 px up (target, tight: the dip is under 4 px only within about 9 px of the anchor, a band about 3 px wide for his 12 px body, with about 4 px of landing margin). The top cord is flush with the roof. A fall costs time only. A Paloma perches at column 113 on that roof and meets him as he steps off the top cord.
  - **Screen five (116 to 149), jump from the end (a teaching beat, not a test):** string S7, 8 tiles (123 to 130, row 8), dipping 12 px, with the next roof (131 to 142, row 5) 48 px above its far end: only a jump from near that end climbs it (target). Since the roof is above the far end, a player jumps from there anyway, so this beat shows the sag's rule rather than testing it. Under it a street well with an awning (124 to 126, row 12), whose high bounce lands him back on the cord and cannot reach the roof. Two drops of 3 and 2 tiles lead to the third ofrenda (153, on a roof from 150 to 160, row 10).
  - **Screen six (150 to 179), the last test:** string S8, 4 tiles (161 to 164, row 10), over the second 4-tile pit, where a fall costs a life, with Avispas hovering over its middle (home at column 163, at his head's height on the cord), which spot him at the roof's edge (159); their home is 160 px from the ofrenda, out of their sight there. He claws them from the roof's edge, or crosses fast, since the swarm drifts at 36 px/s and a running jump clears the pit without the cord. Then the exit roof (165 to 179).
- **Secret:** the veladora lies on a 2-tile ledge (97 and 98, row 4) left of the ladder's top, in plain view: a running jump left from the top cord, past the pillar's top, reaches it, and from the middle cord it is out of reach (targets). A miss drops him to the street: time, not a life.
- **Ofrendas** at columns 45, 99 and 153 (25%, 55% and 85%).
- **Enemies:** two Chihuahuas (34, in the pen; 92), two Avispas (61, 163) and two Palomas (77, 113), every Paloma on a roof beside a string, never on a cord (GDD-ENM-03).
- **Piñatas:** the first (20) gives a taco that turns at the step's wall (23), and a chile reached by a hop from the step's top; the second (85) a chile reached by dropping onto it from the roof's end (82). A chile here gives fireballs for the Avispas. All targets.
- **Hilos:** 50, in trails: on the start roof, over S1 and on its stall, by the first piñata, on the step, over S2 and one hanging under its middle (touched where the sag carries his body down to it, or by a jump up through the cord from the street), on the pen awning's arc, on the ofrenda roof and over the pit, on the Avispas' roof, over S3, on the Paloma's roof, on the street by the piñata and the Chihuahua, up the ladder (two per cord), one on the veladora ledge, on the top roof, over S7 and one hanging under its middle, on the way down, over S8 and by the exit. Holding right from the start collects the first three by the 30th step.
- **Played through (target):** with 2-1's input rules, plus jumping each Paloma's flight from the cord and clawing each swarm just ahead, a bot reaches the exit losing no life in under 2400 steps (40 s).

Status: Proposed (user, 2026-11-04: "Design first, then build"). The layout, the name and every number are Proposed; nothing is built yet. Figures that follow from the simulation are targets, to be measured by `src/levels/level2-3.test.ts`. The grid and the four rules of GDD-WLD-12 hold.

Four pits of 4 tiles: 49 to 52, 67 to 70, 159 to 162 and 168 to 171 (the last under a string).
- **Teaching order:** this level combines the world's elements: awning to string, string to awning, the Avispas over an awning, the Tlacuache back on the roofs, and strings over pits.
  - **Screen one (0 to 34), warm-up:** awning A1 (8 to 10, row 12) up to a roof 5 tiles tall (12 to 20, row 9); string S1 (21 to 26, row 9) to a roof (27 to 34) with a Paloma at column 29 that spots him on the cord; under S1 a street awning (22 to 24) back up.
  - **Screen two (35 to 66), letting go of jump with a threat, then the Tlacuache:** running off the roof's end drops Poncho onto awning A3 (35 to 38, row 12, top y = 192). The Avispas' home is over it, at column 37 with its centre at y ≈ 66: not holding jump, the low bounce keeps his hurtbox centre more than 64 px from the swarm's centre, out of its sight and its reach, orbit included, while the high bounce rises into its box (targets). From A3 a low bounce carries him onto a low roof 16 px up (41 to 48, row 11); a running jump from the first roof cannot skip A3 (target). The hilos here lie in the low bounce's band, under the swarm's reach (rechecked with its height). The first ofrenda (45), a 4-tile pit (49 to 52), then a roof at row 11 (53 to 66) where two chimney posts 3 tiles tall (55 and 62, rows 8 to 10) anchor string S2 (56 to 61, row 8). Between them, on the roof, the Tlacuache returns (start at column 59), charging along under the cord and turning at the posts. A Paloma perches on the right post's top (62), spots him on the cord and flies along it, so he jumps it from the sag with the Tlacuache below. Fallen in, he jumps back up through the cord or over a post. The first piñata (54, row 7).
  - **Screen three (67 to 101), the Chihuahua with a threat:** a 4-tile pit (67 to 70) to a roof (71 to 80, row 11), then the street with a Chihuahua (85). A low awning (89 to 91, row 13, top y = 208) lies on the street 64 px below the next roof (93 to 101, row 9): a high bounce reaches the roof, and the Chihuahua, which never hops onto the awning, is left yapping on the street (target). The second ofrenda (98) on that roof.
  - **Screen four (102 to 128), awning to string, and the secret:** Poncho drops onto awning A6 (103 to 105, row 11, top y = 176), whose high bounce carries him over a hanging arcade pillar (106, rows 7 to 11) onto string S4 (107 to 112, row 7); the low bounce falls short (targets). Avispas hover under S4 (home at column 110): lingering on the cord's sagging middle brings his centre within their 64 px and they rise after him, while crossing without stopping keeps ahead of them (target). A roof (113 to 122, row 7) leads to string S5 (123 to 128, row 7), anchored on a pole (129, rows 7 to 16). Under S4 the street passes under the pillar, with a 1-tile stall step (102) back onto A6; under S5 a well with an awning (124 to 126, row 11) and a step (127).
  - **Screen five (129 to 158), string to awning, and the Tlacuache test:** walking off the pole's top drops Poncho 80 px onto awning A7 (130 to 132, row 12) over six spikes (130 to 135); its high bounce lands him on a roof 48 px up (136 to 148, row 9). There a Tlacuache (start at column 144) is to charge him as he lands (target: whether it does depends on its walk, so its start is moved until the test sees it); he claws it, or jumps it and goes on. It turns at the roof's left end and at a chimney (149, rows 6 to 8). Over the chimney the third ofrenda (153) and the second piñata (151, row 5), its chile reached by a hop from the chimney's top.
  - **Screen six (159 to 179), the last test:** a 4-tile pit (159 to 162) to a short roof (163 to 167, row 9), with Avispas over it at head height (home at column 165); he claws them as he lands or burns them with the chile's fireballs (target: a fireball's 20 px bounce reaches the swarm's lower edge). Then string S6 (168 to 171, row 9) over the last 4-tile pit, and the exit.
- **Secret, a room (Open Question 33):** in the base of the building under S4's right end, a papel picado fragile wall 2 tiles tall (113, rows 12 and 13) closes a 2 x 2 room (114 and 115) holding the veladora (115, row 13). A claw from the street breaks it (target: one swipe breaks both tiles). Poncho reaches it by coming down from the cord into the street past the Avispas' home, and goes back up by the stall step and A6. It is in plain view from the cord, as a pink block in the terracotta wall.
- **Ofrendas** at columns 45, 98 and 153 (25%, 54.4% and 85%).
- **Enemies:** two Palomas (29, 62), one Chihuahua (85), three Avispas (37, 110, 165) and two Tlacuaches (59, 144), each Tlacuache walking only between its posts or ledges, as in 1-2 (target).
- **Hilos:** 50, in trails along the path: the start and A1's arc, the first roof, over S1, the Paloma's roof, A3's low-bounce band, the ofrenda roof, over the pits, up a post and along S2, the street by the Chihuahua, the low awning's arc, the second ofrenda's roof, A6's arc onto S4 and S4's ends, one in the secret room, along S5, A7's arc, the Tlacuache's roof and the chimney, the third ofrenda and the second piñata, over S6 and by the exit.
- **Played through (target):** a bot reaches the exit losing no life in under 2700 steps (45 s).
- **Difficulty ramp from 1-3 (Proposed):** 2-1 is easier than 1-3, since the world is new: 5 screens, one 3-tile pit, the street catching most falls, and lives lost only on spikes, Palomas and Chihuahuas. 2-2 brings in the 4-tile pit and the string over a pit as its last test. 2-3 has four 4-tile pits, spikes under the awnings and every enemy of the world; its target time (45 s) is above 1-3's 40 s.

---

## 9. Enemies and bosses

### GDD-ENM-01 Enemy design rules

Status: Proposed

- One idea per enemy, readable at a glance, with a 0.5 s telegraph before any attack. (Built for the Zopilote's dive, the Tlacuache's charge and Don Zopilote's dive and sweep, GDD-BOS-02. His cactus fruit has none of its own: it falls in view for 43 to 56 steps, 0.7 to 0.9 s, before it reaches a Poncho standing on the floor below it, Open Question 20.)
- Every enemy can be beaten at least one way: stomp, claw or avoidance.
- Enemies never spawn on screen within 3 tiles of Poncho's respawn. (Checked for every enemy in every level, the sandbox included, by the level validator, GDD-TEC-04.) Don Zopilote starts at least 3 tiles from the spawn of his arena, its only respawn, and starts there again whenever Poncho respawns (checked by the level validator's boss-level rule, GDD-TEC-04; GDD-BOS-01).
- Enemy contact is resolved by GDD-CMB-03 and never costs more than 1 life. (Built for the Alacrán: it is a hit like a spike, one life in Base.) Avoidance is a built way to beat an enemy: the cactus buddy cannot be defeated at all (GDD-ENM-02).
- **Never scenery (Proposed, not built yet; for World 2's enemies, GDD-ENM-03):** no World 2 enemy shares its silhouette or main colours with the world's scenery. No stall or backdrop shows painted or carved pigeons, dogs, wasps, opossums or lucha masks; the papel picado colours (pink, gold, teal and blue, GDD-ART-06) are never an enemy's main colour, judged by hue, not by name; every World 2 enemy moves or animates while it is in play (the Tlacuache playing dead and El Tlacuachón's pose and defeat excepted), so a still shape is scenery; and scenery keeps 2 columns clear of every enemy's start. El Tlacuachón's ripples have no telegraph of their own: his landing, after a 0.5 s telegraph, is their warning (GDD-BOS-03, Open Question 27).

### GDD-ENM-02 World 1 bestiary

Status: Proposed

| Enemy | Behaviour | Defeated by |
| --- | --- | --- |
| Alacrán (scorpion) | Patrols a platform, turns at edges | Stomp or claw (also a fireball, GDD-PWR-03) |
| Zopilote (vulture) | Flies a sine path, swoops after a 0.5 s telegraph | Claw (also a fireball, GDD-PWR-03; not stompable) |
| Cactus buddy | Static, spiky, harmless from below | Avoid only (not stompable; the claw passes through it and a fireball is used up by it) |
| Tlacuache (opossum) | Fast charger, plays dead after hit | Claw (even while it plays dead), stomp after wake (also a fireball while awake, GDD-PWR-03) |

- **Alacrán (built, details Proposed):** a 14 x 10 px scorpion standing on the ground that walks at 30 px/s (`ENEMY` in `src/config/tuning.ts`). It starts at the place the level gives it, heading right. It turns round when its box meets a wall, flush against it, and when the ground under its leading edge ends, so it never walks off a ledge and never falls. It walks up and down sand slopes with the middle of its feet on them, a slope's high side turns it like a wall, and the ground under its leading edge is a level tile or a slope in the row of its feet or the row below (GDD-MOV-09). A stomp (GDD-CMB-01), a claw swipe whose box overlaps it (GDD-CMB-02) or a fireball that overlaps it (GDD-PWR-03) defeats it; it is then gone. Every fireball that overlaps a live enemy in a step is used up, whatever defeats the enemy that step. Touching it any other way is a hit (GDD-CMB-03). It uses Poncho's hurtbox against its own box, like spikes: boxes that only share an edge do not touch. A defeated one stays defeated until a life is lost, when every enemy is alive again and back at its start, heading right (GDD-LIFE-02). It is a placeholder drawing of a dark red scorpion with pale claws and tail. The sandbox has one, on the platform at row 10, columns 4 to 7, which Poncho reaches from the top of the 2-tile wall; the piñata at column 8 is the platform's right end, at the same height, so the Alacrán walks across its top too and turns at its far edge (x = 71 to 137); the platform is 64 px above the first ofrenda, clear of its respawn (GDD-ENM-01). Level 0-1 has three, level 1-1 six and level 1-3 one, penned behind a fragile wall, its walk clear of the moving block just past its pen (GDD-WLD-06, GDD-WLD-07, GDD-WLD-09), and the slopes level one, which walks over its hill, its dune and its first bump (GDD-MOV-09). Not built yet: sounds, a defeat animation. `src/sim/enemy.ts`.
- **Zopilote (built, details Proposed):** a 16 x 10 px vulture that flies (`ZOPILOTE` in `src/config/tuning.ts`). The level gives the middle of its start and its cruise line, and it ignores tiles. Cruising, it flies at 40 px/s up to 48 px either side of its start, turning round there (it starts heading right), while bobbing up and down 12 px on a sine with a period of 2.5 s (150 steps). When Poncho's hurtbox is within 96 px to either side of it and between 0 and 80 px below it, it spots him: it freezes and flashes for exactly 0.5 s (30 steps, the telegraph of GDD-ENM-01; the body flashes red and the wings are raised). It then dives in a straight line at 150 px/s to the spot Poncho's hurtbox centre was at when the telegraph ended (it aims once and does not follow him on), and from there climbs straight back up to its cruise line at 60 px/s, then cruises again from where it is, and may spot him again at once. It cannot be stomped (GDD-CMB-01): landing on it is a hit (GDD-CMB-03), and so is touching it any other way. A claw swipe (GDD-CMB-02) or a fireball (GDD-PWR-03, which is used up) defeats it. It is defeated for good until a life is lost, when it is alive again on its cruise line at its start, heading right (GDD-LIFE-02). It is a placeholder drawing of a dark brown vulture with a pink head, flapping wings and three pale thorns on its back. The sandbox has one, cruising at y = 64 from x = 216 (168 to 264), above the middle of the level: from the floor Poncho's hurtbox centre is 144 px below its cruise line and it never spots him; on the Alacrán's platform he is 80 px below it, the edge of its reach, so it spots him there whenever it is at or below its cruise line: half of every bob, and on its first step (user, 2026-10-25: kept as the taller body makes it, a harder platform) (a Poncho who lingers there is dived on); and the spring ledge and the moving platform, 32 px and 48 px below it, are well within reach. The Tlacuache's ledge, from x = 400, is out of its sideways reach, so it never spots Poncho there. It is far from every respawn (GDD-ENM-01). Level 1-1 has two, each cruising at y = 136, 72 px above the hurtbox centre of a Poncho on the floor, so it spots him there, and neither reaches a respawn point (GDD-WLD-07). Level 1-3 has one, cruising at y = 112 over the shuttle's spikes, 64 px above the hurtbox centre of a Poncho waiting on the step before the shuttle, so it spots him there, and it never spots him at the start or at an ofrenda (GDD-WLD-09). Not built yet: sounds, a defeat animation, a swoop that leaves the screen. `src/sim/enemy.ts`.
- **Cactus buddy (built, details Proposed):** a static 12 x 16 px cactus standing on the ground at the place the level gives it (`CACTUS` in `src/config/tuning.ts`). It never moves, and it is "avoid only" (user decision, 2026-10-22): a stomp does not defeat it (it cannot be stomped, GDD-CMB-01, and landing on it is a hit), a claw swipe passes through it, and a fireball that overlaps it is used up without harming it, so it can cover Poncho from fireballs. Touching it is a hit (GDD-CMB-03), except that it is harmless from below: a contact is no hit while Poncho is moving up and his hurtbox top was at or below its underside on the step before, once his head is above that line he is inside it and the usual hit applies. That can only happen for a cactus with no solid tile directly beneath it: a cactus standing on the ground or a ledge has the top of that tile as its underside, which Poncho's head cannot get below while he overlaps it (tile collision, GDD-MOV-06). So no cactus buddy that stands on something can be passed from below, and no level has one with nothing beneath it yet; the rule is only covered by unit tests on a cactus in mid-air. It uses Poncho's hurtbox against its own box, like the other enemies. It is a placeholder drawing of a green cactus with two arms and three pale thorns on top. It is alive again, simply where it was, after a lost life (GDD-LIFE-02). The sandbox has one on the right half of the top of the 2-tile wall (columns 10 and 11, top y = 192), at x = 184, leaving the left 18 px of the wall top free to stand on and to jump from to the Alacrán's platform; it is more than 3 tiles from every respawn (GDD-ENM-01; checked by the level validator, GDD-TEC-04). No spot in the sandbox shows the from-below case (see above), so that is covered by unit tests. Level 1-1 has three, and levels 1-2 and 1-3 one each, each on the floor (GDD-WLD-07 to GDD-WLD-09). Not built yet: sounds. `src/sim/enemy.ts`.
- **Tlacuache (built, details Proposed):** a 16 x 10 px opossum standing on the ground (`TLACUACHE` in `src/config/tuning.ts`). It starts at the place the level gives it, heading right, and walks at 24 px/s, turning at walls (flush against them) and at ledge ends, and walking and charging up and down sand slopes, like the Alacrán (GDD-MOV-09). It spots Poncho when his feet are within 16 px of its own, up or down (its feet where they are now, on a slope too), and his hurtbox centre is in front of it (the way it faces) by more than 0 and up to 80 px (user decision, 2026-10-22: sight, then dash). It then freezes and flashes red for exactly 0.5 s (30 steps, the telegraph of GDD-ENM-01) and charges at 150 px/s the way it faces, without turning to follow him, until a wall or the end of its ground stops it (as in its walk, it never runs off a ledge); there it turns round and walks again, and may spot him again at once. Touching it is a hit (GDD-CMB-03), while it walks, telegraphs or charges. Its first stomp (GDD-CMB-01) does not defeat it: Poncho bounces as from any stomp and it plays dead for 2.0 s (120 steps), lying still on its back (user decision, 2026-10-22: stomp twice). Playing dead it is out of play except for the claw: touching it is no hit, landing on it is no stomp and a fireball passes over it without being used up, but a claw swipe that overlaps it defeats it (user decision, 2026-10-22). Then it wakes facing the way it faced, walking, and touching it is a hit again (so a Poncho standing in it when it wakes is hit; its feet twitch in its last 0.5 s as a warning). After it has played dead once, a stomp defeats it. The claw (GDD-CMB-02) defeats it at any time, and a fireball (GDD-PWR-03) at any time it is awake, whether or not it has been stomped; either wins over a first stomp in the same step. It is a placeholder drawing of a grey opossum with a pale face, pink nose and ears and a long bare pink tail. A lost life brings it back at its start, walking, not yet stomped (GDD-LIFE-02). The sandbox has one in the middle of a ledge 5 tiles long in the top right corner (row 6, columns 25 to 29, x 400 to 480, top y = 96), which it walks end to end; Poncho reaches the ledge with a jump from the moving platform at its right end, 32 px lower. The ledge is out of the Zopilote's sideways reach and 128 px above the third ofrenda, far from every respawn (GDD-ENM-01; checked by the level validator, GDD-TEC-04). The slopes level has one, which walks its second bump (GDD-MOV-09). Level 1-2 has three, each in a pen: one on the floor, which does not spot Poncho on the pen's walls, one on a 45° hill and one on a gentle dune (GDD-WLD-08). Level 1-3 has three: on a sand dune penned by a block and a door, behind a fragile wall, and on the last cliff's top, where it walks past the exit; the one behind the fragile wall, let out and left alive, walks through moving blocks (GDD-WLD-09). Not built yet: sounds, a defeat animation, a charge pose. `src/sim/enemy.ts`.

### GDD-ENM-03 World 2 bestiary

Status: Proposed (user, 2026-11-04: "Design first, then build"). The roster, every rule and every number are Proposed; nothing is built yet (Open Question 24). Speeds and reaches that follow from the simulation are targets, measured once each enemy is built.

World 2 adds three enemies, and one World 1 enemy comes back. Each new one meets a signature element: the Paloma perches beside the papel picado strings (GDD-WLD-11), the Chihuahua chases Poncho along the streets the awnings lift him out of (GDD-WLD-10), and the Avispas guard a sweets stall. Each follows GDD-ENM-01: one idea, a 0.5 s telegraph before any attack, at least one way to beat it, and a contact costs at most 1 life (GDD-CMB-03). Sizes are game px, width x height.

| Enemy | Behaviour | Defeated by | First in |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| Paloma (pigeon) | Perches; spots Poncho on its own level, puffs up, flies straight at him and back | Stomp, claw or fireball | 2-1 (GDD-WLD-12) |
| Chihuahua (a vendor's runaway dog) | Hops; spots Poncho, yaps, hops after him | Stomp, claw or fireball | 2-1, second half |
| Avispas (a wasp swarm) | Hovers over its sweets stall; spots Poncho, gathers, drifts after him on a leash | Claw or fireball (not stompable) | 2-2 (GDD-WLD-13) |
| Tlacuache (from World 1, GDD-ENM-02) | Unchanged | As in GDD-ENM-02 | 2-3 (GDD-WLD-14) |

The Alacrán, the Zopilote and the cactus buddy belong to the desert and do not appear in World 2. The Tlacuache returns because opossums live in Mexican towns and raid fruit, so it fits a market roof with no new rule. World 2 has no heavy enemy (so the claw's stun stays unbuilt, GDD-CMB-02), no alebrije enemy (market stalls sell alebrijes as folk art, so one would look like scenery) and no new minor hazard (Open Question 24).

- **Awnings and strings:** no enemy steps or hops onto an awning; a walker turns at its end like a ledge end, and the Chihuahua treats it as no landing spot (GDD-WLD-10). A string never blocks an enemy, and no enemy perches on a cord: Palomas perch on roof tiles and post tops beside strings (Open Question 23).
- **Never scenery:** GDD-ENM-01's World 2 rule.
- **Defeats stay kind (GDD-VIS-04; Open Question 24):** a defeated World 2 enemy is not hurt on screen: it pops off with a small puff (placeholder), and later art shows it scampering or flying home unhurt, the Chihuahua running home most of all, since it is a pet dog children stomp. World 1's enemies keep "it is then gone". A defeated enemy stays defeated until a life is lost; then every enemy is alive again at its start, heading right (GDD-LIFE-02).
- **Respawn clearance (GDD-ENM-01):** no enemy's start lies within 3 tiles of a respawn; the Paloma's start is its perch and the Avispas' their home point (the validator, GDD-TEC-04).

**Paloma (pigeon):**
- **Size and start:** a 12 x 10 px box. The level gives the middle of its perch, on a roof tile or a post top, and the y of its feet. It starts perched, facing right.
- **Perched:** it stays put, bobbing its head and turning to face Poncho's side every 1.0 s, so it always moves.
- **Spotting and telegraph:** it spots Poncho when his feet are within 16 px of its own, up or down (he is on its level), and his hurtbox centre is up to 80 px to either side of it. It then faces him, puffs up and flashes red for exactly 0.5 s (30 steps). A Poncho above or below its level is never spotted, so he can drop onto it from an awning's bounce, or jump up at it, and stomp it on its perch.
- **Flight:** after the telegraph it flies straight across at its perch height, toward the side Poncho was on, at 100 px/s for 160 px (10 tiles), ignoring tiles; it aims once and does not follow him. Then it turns and flies back to its perch at 60 px/s, lands, and may spot him again at once. Target: a standing jump on its level clears its 10 px body.
- **Beaten:** stompable perched and in flight (GDD-CMB-01), World 2's soft flier; a claw swipe or a fireball that overlaps it defeats it (GDD-CMB-02, GDD-PWR-03), a fireball reaching it only within about 20 px of the ground it rolls on, which includes a Paloma flying at Poncho on his own level (target).
- **Placeholder look:** a slate-grey pigeon with a green and purple sheen on its neck, an orange eye and pink feet; puffed up and flashing red for the telegraph.
- **Levels:** 2-1 meets the first on a low roof, reached from above by a high bounce off an awning, and a second on Poncho's level, to teach jumping over it; 2-2 and 2-3 put them on roofs and posts beside the strings.
- `PALOMA = { width: 12, height: 10, sightX: 80, sightY: 16, telegraphSteps: 30, flySpeed: 100, flyRange: 160, returnSpeed: 60 }` in `src/config/tuning.ts` when built.

**Chihuahua (a vendor's runaway dog):**
- **Size and start:** a 12 x 10 px box standing on the ground, as small as the Paloma. The level gives its middle and the y of its feet; it starts facing right. (The breed is named after the state of World 1's desert.)
- **Idle:** it hops in place, about 4 px up every 0.8 s, so it always moves.
- **Spotting and telegraph:** it spots Poncho when his hurtbox centre is within 96 px to either side and his feet within 48 px of its own, up or down. It then turns to him, crouches and yaps, flashing for exactly 0.5 s (30 steps).
- **Chase hops:** it hops toward the side he was on at the start of each hop, so unlike the Tlacuache it turns to follow him between hops. Each hop goes 32 px (2 tiles) forward and about 16 px up (target: about 220 px/s up and 110 px/s across under Poncho's gravity), with 0.4 s on the ground between hops: about 46 px/s on average, so Poncho's 120 px/s run always outruns it. It stops chasing once he has been out of its sight for 2.0 s, and idles where it stopped.
- **Ledges:** it never hops where the landing point has no ground; a hop that would land over a pit, off a ledge or into a wall is not taken, and it stays and yaps instead. An awning is no landing spot (GDD-WLD-10; Open Question 25). So it never falls into a pit.
- **Beaten:** a stomp, a claw swipe or a fireball defeats it (GDD-CMB-01, GDD-CMB-02, GDD-PWR-03); touching it any other way is a hit (GDD-CMB-03), in the air too.
- **Placeholder look:** a tan Chihuahua with big pink-lined ears, a dark nose and a red collar with a small gold tag (red is no papel picado colour).
- **Levels:** 2-1's second half meets the first in a closed street pen with no awning, to teach its hop, and a second that chases him to an awning he bounces away from (GDD-WLD-12); 2-2 puts it in a pen under a string, and 2-2 and 2-3 mix it with Palomas.
- `CHIHUAHUA = { width: 12, height: 10, sightX: 96, sightY: 48, telegraphSteps: 30, hopX: 32, hopUp: 220, restSteps: 24, giveUpSteps: 120 }` in `src/config/tuning.ts` when built (`hopUp` in px/s, a target).

**Avispas (a wasp swarm):**
- **Size and home:** a 16 x 16 px box, a swarm of five dots, that ignores tiles. The level gives its home point, the middle of the swarm, over its sweets stall (dulces, pan dulce or aguas frescas). The stall is part of the Avispas' own placeholder drawing, not a scenery piece, so it goes with the enemy entry and nothing touches it.
- **Idle:** it circles its home point 6 px out once every 1.5 s, its dots jittering.
- **Spotting and telegraph:** it spots Poncho when his hurtbox centre is within 64 px of its centre, in any direction; it then draws its dots into a tight ball and flashes red for exactly 0.5 s (30 steps), with a buzz.
- **Drift:** it drifts straight toward Poncho's hurtbox centre at 36 px/s, aiming again every step, through tiles, strings and awnings, on a leash of 96 px from its home. When he has been more than 112 px from its home for 1.0 s it drifts back home at 36 px/s and circles again. Running away always works.
- **Beaten:** not stompable (GDD-CMB-01): landing on it is a hit like touching it anywhere else, with no bounce, and it carries three pale thorns, the mark of every enemy that cannot be stomped. A claw swipe that overlaps it scatters it, which defeats it (GDD-CMB-02); a fireball that overlaps it defeats it and is used up (GDD-PWR-03).
- **Placeholder look:** five dots striped in black and a pale lemon yellow, its hue clearly apart from the papel picado gold (chosen outside the world's palette when drawn), with pale wings; the ball flashes red in the telegraph.
- **Respect (GDD-VIS-04):** wasps drawn to sweets, as at any sweets stall anywhere, not a fly swarm over food, which could read as a "dirty market" caricature (Open Question 24).
- **Levels:** 2-2 meets the first over a stall across a gap of open roof, so Poncho sees it gather and follow before it can reach him; 2-3 puts one over an awning, where bouncing up into it is the danger, and others over a cord and a roof.
- `AVISPAS = { size: 16, orbitRadius: 6, orbitSteps: 90, sight: 64, telegraphSteps: 30, speed: 36, leash: 96, giveUp: 112, giveUpSteps: 60 }` in `src/config/tuning.ts` when built.

**Tlacuache (returns from World 1):** all of GDD-ENM-02's rules and numbers, unchanged (walk 24 px/s, sight 80 px, telegraph 0.5 s, charge 150 px/s, plays dead 2.0 s after its first stomp, the claw at any time), and its placeholder look, now a fruit raider on the market roofs. By the walker rule it never steps onto an awning, so a Poncho bouncing on one is safe from its charge only while in the air. 2-3 brings it back as the test step, charging along a roof under a string a Paloma guards (GDD-WLD-14).

### GDD-BOS-01 Boss structure

Status: Proposed (user, 2026-11-02: World 1's boss built "as written", the numbers Proposed for the user to adjust after playing; what a lost life does to the fight "As planned")

Each world ends with a boss in a single-screen arena. Three phases, each teaching or testing the world's signature element. Bosses do not drop extra lives; the fight must be doable with the lives the player has left. Checkpoint at the arena start.

- **A boss level (built for World 1, details Proposed):** the arena is a level of its own, the last of its world, one screen wide (30 x 17 tiles): `1-4` for World 1 (GDD-BOS-02). It has no ofrendas, no exit, no hilos and no veladora: no extra lives and no secret. Poncho starts, and respawns after every lost life, at the arena start the level gives (GDD-LIFE-02). The boss takes 3 hits; 1.0 s (60 steps) after the hit that defeats him the level clear screen comes up as at an exit (GDD-UI-02): the progress is saved (GDD-PRG-02), and the results menu greys "Next level" while the world has no level after it.
- **A life lost mid-fight (user, 2026-11-02: "As planned"; details Proposed):** the boss keeps the hits he has taken and starts the phase he is in again from its start (the next phase, if a hit's flashing had not ended), with all his fruit gone and every rock back on its spot (checked by `src/sim/bossfight.test.ts`). Running out of lives in the arena restarts the world from its first level with seven lives, as anywhere in the world (GDD-LIFE-05; 1-1 in World 1): the fight starts afresh, with all three of his hits, when Poncho next reaches the arena (checked by `e2e/world1.spec.ts`).
- **The validator's boss-level rule (GDD-TEC-04):** a level with a boss has no ofrendas and no exit and is exactly one screen wide, and the boss's starting point is at least 3 tiles from the spawn (GDD-ENM-01). It keeps the other checks every level gets: no gap wider than the world's limit, no enemy starting within 3 tiles of a respawn, and the sand slope checks (GDD-MOV-09).
- **The boss bar:** the HUD shows the boss's name and the hits he has left (GDD-UI-01).
- `src/sim/boss.ts`, `src/levels/level1-4.ts`, `src/levels/validate.ts`.
- **World 2 (Proposed, not built yet):** World 2's boss level is `2-4`, El Tlacuachón's arena (GDD-BOS-03), held to the same rule. A lost life there clears every thing he made (his ripples), as Don Zopilote's fruit is cleared; running out of lives there restarts World 2 at 2-1 with seven (GDD-LIFE-05). The results menu greys "Next level" while the game has no level after it: once World 2 is complete, 1-4 leads on to 2-1, and 2-4 names none until World 3 exists. `src/sim/tlacuachon.ts`, `src/levels/level2-4.ts` when built.

### GDD-BOS-02 World 1 boss

Status: Proposed (user, 2026-11-02: "Don Zopilote as written"; "Placeholders for now", shapes "like the other enemies have"; the speeds, timings and arena filled in as Proposed "for you to adjust after playing"; his rules "As planned", marked below; the placeholder look approved from screenshots, user, 2026-11-03: "Approved, merge"). Placeholder art.

A giant vulture, Don Zopilote, circles the arena and drops cactus fruit. Phase 1: dodge dives. Phase 2: knock rocks onto it with claw swipes. Phase 3: faster dives plus ground hazards. Defeat in 3 hits.

- **The arena, level 1-4 (built, details Proposed):** 30 x 17 tiles, one screen (480 x 272 px), World 1's last level: "Next level" on 1-3's results menu starts it (GDD-UI-02, GDD-WLD-09), and `?level=1-4` opens it. Drawn with World 1's backdrop and ground tiles (GDD-ART-06). The floor is flat (user, 2026-11-02: "As planned"): its top is row 14 (y = 224) across the whole width, with no pit and no sand slope, so World 1's boss does not test the sand slopes, as GDD-BOS-01 asks of each boss. A pillar 4 tiles wide and 3 tall stands at each side, the arena's walls (columns 0 to 3 and 26 to 29, rows 11 to 13, top y = 176): a full jump from the floor beside either one lands on its top and a short hop does not (measured by `src/levels/level1-4.test.ts`). A rock rests on the inner edge of each pillar top, its middle at x = 56 and x = 424. Poncho starts on the floor at column 14 (x = 232). No ofrendas, exit, hilos, veladora or other enemies (GDD-BOS-01). `src/levels/level1-4.ts`.
- **Don Zopilote:** a box of 48 x 30 px, three times the Zopilote's (GDD-ENM-02), that ignores tiles. Placeholder look: the Zopilote's drawing at three times its size, with a gold crown of three points on his pink head; his wings flap, raised for 8 steps and lowered for 8, while he laps, drops, sweeps or climbs, are raised through his telegraph, and are held lowered while he dives, is stuck, hurt or defeated. The three pale thorns on his back mark that he cannot be stomped (GDD-CMB-01): landing on him is a touch like any other (checked by `src/sim/bossfight.test.ts`). He follows a fixed pattern with nothing left to chance; only where Poncho is changes it. `BOSS` in `src/config/tuning.ts`, `src/sim/boss.ts`, `src/ui/bossPose.ts`.
- **Circling:** he laps an ellipse 152 px to either side of a middle the level gives and 16 px above and below it (x = 240, y = 72 in 1-4), once in 4.0 s (240 steps), starting and ending at its right or left end (x = 392 or 88 in 1-4): from the right end he goes leftwards along its lower half first, from the left end rightwards along its upper half first. The fight starts with him at the right end. He drops a cactus fruit on the 40th, 100th, 160th and 220th step of each lap, its top at his box's bottom: in 1-4, from the right end they fall at x = 316, 108, 164 and 372, from the left end at 164, 372, 316 and 108 (measured by `src/sim/boss.test.ts`). He faces the way he moves across; when he does not move across he keeps the way he faced.
- **Telegraph (GDD-ENM-01):** at the end of each lap he freezes for 0.5 s (30 steps) before his attack, facing Poncho, wings raised and body flashing red, 4 steps on and 4 off. In phases 1 and 3 the attack is a dive, in phase 2 a sweep.
- **Dive (phases 1 and 3):** as the telegraph ends he takes the middle of Poncho's hurtbox, across only, and dives in a straight line to the floor below it (his box's bottom on the floor's top), that x kept between the ends of his lap (88 to 392 in 1-4), so he never clips a pillar. He dives at 180 px/s in phase 1 and at 300 px/s in phase 3. Then he is stuck in the sand, dazed (three pale dots over his head): 1.0 s (60 steps) in phase 1, 0.6 s (36 steps) in phase 3. Then he climbs in a straight line at 120 px/s to the nearer end of his lap (the right one when he is in the middle) and laps from there. In 1-4 a phase 1 dive aimed at the start (x = 232) sticks him in the sand 71 steps after it starts (measured by `src/sim/boss.test.ts`).
- **Sweep (phase 2):** as the telegraph ends he drops straight down at 180 px/s until his box's bottom is on the floor, sweeps along it at 150 px/s to below the other end of his lap, and climbs straight up at 120 px/s to that end, then laps from there; so the sweeps go right to left and left to right in turn. In 1-4 his drop or his sweep reaches Poncho standing anywhere on the floor between the pillars, and neither reaches him on a pillar top away from its inner edge (see "Touching him") (measured by `src/levels/level1-4.test.ts`): the pillars are where he dodges it.
- **Cactus fruit:** a red prickly pear, an 8 x 8 px square, that falls straight down at 120 px/s from where he dropped it. In phases 1 and 2 it bursts and is gone when it touches a tile. In phase 3 it lies on that tile's top for 3.0 s (180 steps), then it is gone: these lying fruit are phase 3's ground hazards (user, 2026-11-02: "As planned"). Touching one, falling or lying, is a hit (GDD-CMB-03), pushed away from the fruit. The claw and fireballs pass through fruit (checked by `src/sim/bossfight.test.ts`). A fruit has no telegraph of its own: it falls in view for 43 to 56 steps (0.7 to 0.9 s) before it reaches a Poncho standing on the floor below where it was dropped (the first of a lap from the right end, dropped on its 40th step at x = 316, reaches him on the 83rd), longer than the 0.5 s of GDD-ENM-01 (measured by `src/sim/bossfight.test.ts`; Open Question 20). In 1-4 no fruit falls on a pillar top (measured by `src/levels/level1-4.test.ts`). `FRUIT`.
- **Touching him** is a hit (GDD-CMB-03), pushed away from his middle, while he laps, telegraphs, dives, drops or sweeps. Stuck in the sand, climbing back, hurt or defeated he does no harm. A step where several things touch Poncho is one hit, pushed away from the first of spike, enemy, Don Zopilote, fruit (GDD-CMB-03; checked by `src/sim/bossfight.test.ts`). In 1-4, on a pillar top nothing of his reaches a Poncho standing there in any phase, neither he nor his fruit, while Poncho's middle is at least 5 px in from the pillar's inner edge (x = 59 or less on the left pillar, 421 or more on the right); nearer that edge (he can stand with his middle up to just under 6 px past it), the dive or the drop beside that pillar, his box's side flush with the pillar's wall, reaches him (measured by `src/levels/level1-4.test.ts`; Open Question 19).
- **Rocks:** a rock is a 12 x 12 px square resting on its spot. A claw swipe (GDD-CMB-02) whose box overlaps a resting rock knocks it the way the swipe faces, at 90 px/s across and 150 px/s up, from the next step; it then falls at 1500 px/s², and it breaks when it touches a tile, falls out of the level or touches Don Zopilote. A broken rock is back on its spot 2.0 s (120 steps) later. A rock never hurts Poncho (checked by `src/sim/bossfight.test.ts`). In 1-4 a rock knocked inwards off the left pillar breaks on the floor on its 23rd step, about 35 px out from where it rested, and one knocked back over its pillar breaks on the pillar top (measured by `src/sim/boss.test.ts` and `src/sim/bossfight.test.ts`). `ROCK`.
- **Hitting him (user, 2026-11-02: "As planned": a claw swipe or a fireball only while he is stuck in the sand after a dive, a rock knocked onto him with a claw swipe, never a stomp; details Proposed):** a flying rock that touches him is a hit, in any phase, unless he is hurt or defeated. A claw swipe or a fireball (GDD-PWR-03) that touches him is a hit only while he is stuck in the sand, so only in phases 1 and 3; a fireball that touches him is used up either way, unless he is defeated, and passes through him then. Stomps never hit him. A hit on him is settled before his touch, so his own touch on the step that hits him does not hurt Poncho. A step that costs Poncho a life does not hit him (as such a step never reaches an exit, GDD-WLD-03).
- **Phase 2 in 1-4 (measured by `src/levels/level1-4.test.ts`):** standing on a pillar top facing the middle (x = 40 or 440), a claw press knocks its rock onto him when it comes in a window of steps: on the pillar he drops beside, from the 12th to the 35th step after his drop starts (24 steps); on the pillar he climbs beside, from 36 steps before his climb starts to 28 steps after it (65 steps).
- **After a hit:** he is hurt for 1.0 s (60 steps), still, his body flashing white, 4 steps on and 4 off, and can neither be hit nor hurt anyone; then he climbs to the nearer end of his lap and starts the next phase. The third hit defeats him: his fruit is gone at once, he falls at 120 px/s, flashing white, until his box's bottom is on the floor, and 1.0 s (60 steps) after that hit the level clear screen comes up (GDD-BOS-01, GDD-UI-02).
- **Played through:** a bot that waits at the start, runs 64 px past where a dive aims, claws him while he is stuck, climbs the left pillar in phase 2 and claws its rock when he drops beside it, beats him with all seven lives in under 1500 steps (25 s) (measured by `src/levels/level1-4.test.ts`). A player's first try takes longer, and with the lives carried through World 1 (GDD-LIFE-05) a player may reach him with fewer than seven.
- **Sounds (GDD-AUD-02):** a hit on him plays the stomp, his landing in the sand after a dive the land, and his defeat Poncho's meow; nothing else is new.
- Not built yet: his real art, a sound of his own, a defeat animation beyond the fall.

### GDD-BOS-03 World 2 boss

Status: Proposed (user, 2026-11-04: "Design first, then build"). The boss, every rule and every number are Proposed; nothing is built yet (Open Question 26). Placeholder art. Figures that follow from the simulation are targets, to be measured by `src/levels/level2-4.test.ts`.

El Tlacuachón, a giant opossum in a luchador's mask, the champion of the fruit raiders on the market roofs (GDD-ENM-03), fights in the arena 2-4. He leaps at Poncho across the street, and each landing sends ripples along the floor; then he poses, open to a stomp. He is 4 tiles tall, so a jump from the floor cannot reach his top: only a bounce off an awning (GDD-WLD-10) gets Poncho above him, so the awnings are both the safe spot and the way to hit back. Phase 1: one leap, then a long pose. Phase 2: two leaps in a row, then a shorter pose. Phase 3: three leaps with tall ripples that reach the low awnings, then a short pose. Defeat in 3 hits. Don Zopilote swoops and drops things and a stomp never hits him; El Tlacuachón stays on the ground and leaps, and only a stomp hits him.

- **Why this boss, and respect (GDD-VIS-04):** lucha libre is a much-loved family show in Mexico, and lucha masks are sold in every market; the tlacuache is already the world's fruit raider, so the boss closes that story. He is a showman, not a villain. His mask never comes off, not even at his defeat: in lucha libre the mask is sacred to the wrestler. Nothing mocks the sport or the people who love it. His defeat is the opossum's own trick: he plays dead. "Tlacuachón", a fond form meaning "big opossum", stays the same in every language, as "Don Zopilote" does. Poncho's moves, the arena and the masks keep clear of wrestling-game conventions beyond El Tlacuachón's own pose and leap, so the fight is not read as a copy of existing luchador games. No stall or backdrop shows lucha masks (GDD-ENM-01, World 2).
- **The arena, level 2-4:** 30 x 17 tiles, one screen (480 x 272 px), World 2's last level: "Next level" on 2-3's results menu starts it, and `?level=2-4` opens it. Drawn with World 2's backdrop and ground (GDD-ART-06). The floor's top is row 14 (y = 224) across the whole width, with no pit and no slope. A wall 1 tile wide and 14 tall stands at each side (columns 0 and 29, rows 0 to 13), house fronts in the ground tiles, so the street runs from x = 16 to x = 464. Three bouncy awnings: A at columns 5 to 7, row 13 (top y = 208, 16 px above the floor); B at columns 13 to 16, row 12 (top y = 192, 32 px up), the higher one, over the middle stall; C at columns 22 to 24, row 13 (top y = 208). Nothing solid lies above any awning. Poncho starts on the floor at column 9 (x = 152), facing right; El Tlacuachón at column 27 (x = 440), facing left. No ofrendas, exit, hilos, veladora, strings or other enemies (GDD-BOS-01; no strings, Open Question 26). `src/levels/level2-4.ts`.

  ```
  col      0         1         2
           012345678901234567890123456789
  row 0    #............................#   (rows 1 to 11 the same)
  row 12   #............====............#   awning B, top y = 192
  row 13   #....===.S............===..T.#   awnings A and C, top y = 208; S Poncho (x = 152), T El Tlacuachón (x = 440)
  row 14   ##############################   floor, top y = 224 (rows 15 and 16 the same)
  ```

- **Free floor:** the stretches of street with no awning above, each with room for his box and at least 8 px to spare from the awning beside it: x = 16 to 80, 128 to 208, 272 to 352 and 400 to 464 (the level test checks it).
- **El Tlacuachón:** a box of 40 x 64 px standing on the floor. He never stands on an awning or a string (GDD-ENM-03's walker rule). He is not solid: Poncho passes through his box, and only touching it counts. Placeholder look: the Tlacuache's drawing (GDD-ENM-02) standing upright on his hind legs, grey fur, a pale face, pink nose and ears and a long bare pink tail, in a red lucha mask with white trim round the eyes and a short red cape (red is no papel picado colour). He has no three thorns, since he can be stomped. His pattern is fixed, with nothing left to chance; only where Poncho is changes it. `TLACUACHON` in `src/config/tuning.ts`, `src/sim/tlacuachon.ts`, `src/ui/bossPose.ts` when built.
- **Why only a bounce reaches him (targets):** his top is 64 px above the floor, and a stomp counts only when Poncho's feet, on the step before, were at most 4 px below it (GDD-CMB-01), so his feet must climb above 60 px. A full jump from the floor peaks about 56 px up and a low bounce off A or C about 55 px (16 + 39): neither can stomp him. A high bounce off A or C peaks about 106 px up (16 + 90) and a low bounce off B about 71 px (32 + 39): both can. If the measured heights differ, his height or the awnings' rows change; the rule kept is that a jump from the floor and a low bounce off A or C never reach his top, and a high bounce off A or C does.
- **Idle:** at the fight's start and after every respawn he bounces on his toes for 1.0 s (60 steps) before his first telegraph; touching him then is a hit.
- **Telegraph (GDD-ENM-01):** before every leap he crouches facing Poncho and raises a fist for 0.5 s (30 steps), his body flashing red, 4 steps on and 4 off.
- **Leap ("la plancha"):** as the telegraph ends he takes the middle of Poncho's hurtbox, across only, as his landing point, kept so his box stays between the walls. A landing point that would put his box under an awning, or within 8 px of one, moves out to the side of that awning he leaps from, 8 px clear of it: he never lands under or beside an awning Poncho bounces on, but he may fly over awnings on his way. A landing point where he stands makes a leap straight up and down. He flies in an arc, straight across at a steady speed, his box's bottom peaking 80 px above the floor halfway; the leap takes 0.8 s (48 steps) in phase 1, 0.7 s (42) in phase 2 and 0.6 s (36) in phase 3, and he ignores tiles in the air. He lands with his box's bottom on the floor's top. To be measured (targets): a phase 1 leap aimed at a Poncho who stays at the start lands on the start; a Poncho who hops onto A during the 1.5 s of idle and telegraph is safe there, and the leap lands at x = 156 (his box from 136 to 176), right of A; a high bounce off C while he flies over it is a fair hit.
- **Ripples:** on the step he lands, one ripple leaves each side of his box and runs outward along the floor's top at 150 px/s, 16 px wide and 8 px tall in phases 1 and 2, 24 px tall in phase 3, gone after 160 px or at a wall. Placeholder look: a curl of pale market dust with two limes bouncing in it. Touching one is a hit (GDD-CMB-03), pushed away from its middle; the claw and fireballs pass through ripples. To be measured (targets): a jump from the floor clears a ripple of either height; a Poncho bouncing on A or C is clear of an 8 px ripple, whose top (y = 216) is below the awning's top; a 24 px ripple (top y = 200) hits him as his feet touch A or C, so those awnings become risky in phase 3, and it never reaches him on B (top y = 192).
- **Phases:**
  - **Phase 1 (teaching: bounce high):** one telegraph and leap, then a pose of 1.5 s (90 steps). A, beside the start, is safe from the landing and the ripples, low or high; only holding jump carries Poncho onto the posing boss, and a low bounce does no harm.
  - **Phase 2 (test: read two leaps):** two telegraphs and leaps back to back, each aimed afresh, then a pose of 1.0 s (60 steps); a Poncho who moves between awnings after the first landing must time the second.
  - **Phase 3 (test: pick the high awning):** three telegraphs and leaps back to back, with the 24 px ripples, then a pose of 0.75 s (45 steps). A and C are risky; B is safe, every landing aimed at a Poncho on B ends beside it on the side the leap came from, and a low bounce off B already reaches his top.
- **The pose:** he stands still facing the screen with his arms raised, a pale sparkle over his mask marking the opening (placeholder; shape and stillness, not only colour, GDD-UI-03). Touching him while he poses is no hit.
- **Touching him:** while he idles, telegraphs, leaps or lands, a hit (GDD-CMB-03), pushed away from his middle; while he poses, is hurt or is defeated, nothing. A step where several things touch Poncho is one hit, pushed away from the first of spike, enemy, boss, ripple.
