- **Hitting him (Open Question 26):** a stomp (GDD-CMB-01) is a hit in every state but hurt and defeated, bouncing Poncho at 260 px/s, or 410 px/s with jump held, like any stomp. A claw swipe passes through him and never hits him; a fireball that touches him is used up and does not hit him, since he swats it away, and passes through him once he is defeated. A hit on him is settled before his touch, so his own touch on the step he is hit does not hurt Poncho, and a step that costs Poncho a life does not hit him.
- **After a hit:** he is hurt for 1.0 s (60 steps), still, his body flashing white, 4 steps on and 4 off, neither hit nor hurting; then the next phase starts with its first telegraph where he stands. The third hit defeats him: his ripples are gone at once, and he plays dead, flat on his back, tongue out, still, mask on, the Tlacuache's trick (GDD-ENM-02), hurting no one. 1.0 s (60 steps) after that hit the level clear screen comes up (GDD-BOS-01, GDD-UI-02). Later art: he springs up, bows, and scampers off over the roofs with an orange.
- **A lost life mid-fight (GDD-BOS-01):** he keeps his hits and starts his phase again from its start (the next phase if a hit's flashing had not ended), back at his start facing left, his ripples gone, the 1.0 s idle before his first telegraph. Out of lives in 2-4, the *Siete vidas gastadas* screen sends Poncho back to 2-1 with seven (GDD-LIFE-05, World 2), and the fight starts afresh, with all three hits, when he next reaches it.
- **The clear and the glide:** saved like any boss level's clear (GDD-PRG-02). The medal follows 1-4's rule: Gold for at most 3 lives lost in the arena, else Silver, never Bronze (GDD-PRG-03). The first clear unlocks the glide, with its line on the clear screen (GDD-PRG-01, GDD-UI-02). "Next level" is greyed, since 2-4 is the game's last level until World 3 exists. The fight is designed without the glide; a replay with it is easier, which is accepted.
- **Played through (target):** a bot that hops onto A at the start holding jump, steers onto him from A's nearer end when he poses beside A, and in phase 3 moves to B and stomps him from there, beats him with all seven lives in under 1800 steps (30 s). Also to be measured: every figure under "Why only a bounce reaches him", that each free stretch of floor fits his box with 8 px to spare, that a high bounce from each end of A and C, holding towards him, lands on a pose 8 px past that end, and the ripple rules.
- **Sounds (GDD-AUD-02):** a hit on him plays the stomp sound, his landing the land sound an octave lower, and his defeat nothing more than that hit's stomp (not Poncho's meow, which is Poncho's own cue). Not built yet: his real art, a sound of his own, a defeat animation beyond playing dead.
- **Tuning:** `TLACUACHON = { width: 40, height: 64, idleSteps: 60, telegraphSteps: 30, leaps: [1, 2, 3], leapSteps: [48, 42, 36], leapRise: 80, awningClear: 8, poseSteps: [90, 60, 45], hurtSteps: 60, defeatSteps: 60, hits: 3, flashSteps: 4 }` and `RIPPLE = { width: 16, heights: [8, 8, 24], speed: 150, range: 160 }` in `src/config/tuning.ts`, and `boss: { kind: 'tlacuachon', x: 440, floor: 224 }` in 2-4 (1-4 gets `kind: 'zopilote'`), when built.

---

## 10. Progression and meta

### GDD-PRG-01 Unlocks

Status: Proposed

- Clearing worlds unlocks permanent **abilities** in order (these are not the temporary power-ups of section 6): Glide (GDD-MOV-07, after World 2), Wall cling (GDD-MOV-08, after World 3). Further powers are open (GDD-MVP-02). Until there are worlds, a level turns an ability on itself: the sandbox has the glide and the wall cling on.
- **The glide unlock (Proposed, not built yet; Open Question 29):** the glide is unlocked on the step the boss arena 2-4's level clear screen first comes up, the step 2-4's `cleared` becomes true in the save (GDD-PRG-02, "Progress"; GDD-BOS-03), so World 2 is always first played without it. Once unlocked it is on in every real level, World 1 and World 2 replays included, and never in the tutorial 0-1; the levels of Worlds 1 and 2 stay designed and validated without it. It is saved nowhere new: it is read from the progress, unlocked when `levels["2-4"].cleared` is true, so no field and no migration are needed and the version stays 1. In code, one function such as `unlockedAbilities(save)` in `src/levels/index.ts`, merged by the scene with the level's own abilities into `startRun`, so `src/sim/run.ts` stays free of the save. An address keeps the save's unlocks. A level's own `abilities` flag stays for test levels only. The wall cling is unlocked the same way by World 3's boss level, when it exists.
- Hilos buy cosmetics for the poncho (patterns and colours). Cosmetics never change gameplay.

### GDD-PRG-02 Save system

Status: Proposed

- Autosave to browser `localStorage` after each level and each checkpoint. Versioned schema with migration. Built: at the level clear (see "Progress" and "World 1's run"), at each checkpoint (see "Saved run") and when the *Siete vidas gastadas* screen comes up (see "Progress" and "World 1's run").
- Saves: world progress, hilos total, unlocked powers, cosmetics, settings, assist flags.
- Save never blocks the main thread and survives storage being unavailable (play without saving).
- **Built (details Proposed):** the settings and whether the tutorial has been started, as `{ "version": 1, "settings": { "master": 0-10, "effects": 0-10, "language": "en", "es" or "de", "buttonOpacity": "low", "medium" or "high", "layout": "standard" or "mirrored" }, "progress": { "tutorialPlayed": true or false, "worldLives": { "1": 0 to 7 }, "worldAt": { "1": "1-2" } } }` (`worldLives` and `worldAt`: see "World 1's run") under the key `poncho-siete-vidas` in local storage, written at each change of either and read when the game opens (GDD-UI-02). A save without `progress`, as the settings-only saves before it, or with anything but `true` there, reads as not played, and one without `language` takes the browser's (GDD-UI-02), so the version stays 1. Saving one part keeps the other. Where the browser refuses storage, the tutorial counts as played for the rest of the visit only. `src/sim/save.ts`.
- **Progress (built; user, 2026-10-31: each hilo once, the best medal, the best time and "cleared" per level, saved at the level clear; its hilos also when the *Siete vidas gastadas* screen comes up, user, 2026-11-02: "Keep them"; details Proposed):** the save gets `"levels": { "0-1": { "hilos": "0110…", "medal": "bronze", "silver" or "gold", "bestSteps": 8040, "cleared": true }, … }`, one entry per real level (0-1, 1-1, 1-2, 1-3 and the boss arena 1-4, whose `hilos` text is empty since it has no hilos; the test levels, the sandbox and the slopes level, are never saved). `hilos` is one character per hilo of the level, in the level's order, `1` for collected. `bestSteps` is the fastest clear time in simulation steps (GDD-UI-02, "Clear time"). It is written on the step Poncho reaches the exit (GDD-WLD-03), or in a boss level on the step its level clear screen comes up (GDD-BOS-01), and its hilos also on the step the *Siete vidas gastadas* screen comes up (below); a run that quits or retries keeps nothing of its own. At the clear every hilo collected in the run is added to the saved ones (never removed), the medal is kept if it is better than the saved one (gold over silver over bronze), the time if it is shorter, and `cleared` becomes true; nothing else of the level's entry changes. On the step the *Siete vidas gastadas* screen comes up (GDD-LIFE-05), in every real level (user, 2026-11-02: "Keep them": "Saved for good when the Siete vidas gastadas screen comes up, in every level; that's what GDD-LIFE-05 says."; this replaces the rule of 2026-10-31 that only a clear saved a run's hilos), the hilos collected in the run are added to the saved ones in the same way, and nothing else of the level changes: the medal, the time and `cleared` stay as saved, and a level with nothing saved gets an entry with no medal, no time and `cleared` false. So the hilos stay when the restart plays another level (GDD-LIFE-05, Decided: "Hilos collected stay"). `recordHilos` in `src/sim/save.ts`. A save without `levels`, as the saves before it, or with an entry that is not readable, reads as nothing saved for that level: a `hilos` text whose length is not the level's hilo count, or with another character than `0` and `1`, reads as no hilo; a medal other than the three names as none; a `bestSteps` that is not a whole number above 0 as none; and `cleared` only as true when it is exactly true. So the version stays 1. `cleared` decides what "Start" plays only for a save that keeps no World 1 run (GDD-UI-02, "Which level"); the world map will read it too. Where the browser refuses storage, the progress counts for the rest of the visit only, like the tutorial flag. `src/sim/save.ts`.
- **World 1's run (built; user, 2026-11-02: "Save the lives": "The save keeps the World 1 lives too: Start plays the first level not cleared with the lives you had, and after a game over the world restarts at 1-1 with 7, however you get there."; "Follow the run": "The save remembers where your World 1 run is: Start plays the next level in order of the current run (1-1 after a game over, then 1-2, ...). Clears still count for medals.", which replaced "first level not cleared" for "Start"; details Proposed):** `progress` gets `"worldLives": { "1": 4 }` and `"worldAt": { "1": "1-2" }`, each per world by its number: `worldLives` the lives the save keeps for the world's run (GDD-LIFE-05), a whole number from 0 to 7, and `worldAt` the level its run has reached, the id of one of the world's real levels. Only World 1 has levels yet, so only its run is kept. Both change on the same two steps, and on no other. On the step Poncho reaches the exit of a World 1 level (GDD-WLD-03), or the level clear screen of the boss arena 1-4 comes up (GDD-BOS-01), `worldLives` becomes the lives he has there, the lives "Next level" carries (GDD-UI-02), and `worldAt` the next level of the world in the game's order (1-2 after 1-1, 1-3 after 1-2, 1-4 after 1-3); after the world's last level, the boss arena 1-4 (GDD-BOS-02), it is the world's first level, 1-1: the run is over and a new one starts (Proposed). On the step the *Siete vidas gastadas* screen comes up in a World 1 level, `worldLives` becomes 0 and `worldAt` 1-1: the world restarts at 1-1 with seven however that is reached (GDD-LIFE-05). A life lost or won, a checkpoint, quitting, "Retry" and closing the game leave both as they were, so quitting a level and picking "Start" plays it again from its start with the lives Poncho had at the last clear (Open Question 18). The tutorial, outside the seven worlds, and the test levels write neither. "Start" reads them (GDD-UI-02, "Which level"). A clear still counts for the medal, the best time and the hilos, and `cleared` is still saved (see "Progress"), but it no longer decides what "Start" plays while the run is kept. A save without `worldLives`, as the saves before it, or with an entry that is not a whole number from 0 to 7, keeps no lives for that world, which reads as seven; a save without `worldAt`, or with an entry that names no real level of that world (a test level, the tutorial, another world's level, anything that is no level id), keeps no run for it, and "Start" goes by the levels cleared (GDD-UI-02, "Which level"); the version stays 1. Where the browser refuses storage both last for the rest of the visit, like the progress. `worldLivesAfter` and `worldAtAfter` in `src/levels/index.ts`, `src/sim/save.ts`.
- **Saved run (built; user, 2026-10-31: resumed by a "Continue" row; it keeps the lives, the run's hilos, the clock and the used ofrendas; dropped at the level clear and by the Siete vidas gastadas screen, not by quitting or by starting another level; details Proposed):** on the step the checkpoint moves to a later ofrenda (GDD-LIFE-02, GDD-LIFE-03) in a real level (0-1, 1-1, 1-2 or 1-3, never a test level; the boss arena 1-4 has no ofrenda, so no run is ever saved there, GDD-BOS-01), the save gets `"run": { "level": "0-1", "checkpoint": 1, "lives": 5, "livesIn": 7, "used": ["ofrenda:…", …], "hilos": "0110…", "clock": 4210 }`: the level, the position of that ofrenda in the level's order, the lives after its touch, the lives the run started with (GDD-PRG-03 counts the lives lost from them), every ofrenda and veladora used so far in the run (each restores a life once, so a resumed run gives none again), the hilos collected in the run (one character per hilo, as in "Progress", the saved ones included) and the clock (GDD-UI-02, "Clear time"). There is one saved run at most: a checkpoint reached in any run, of any level, replaces it. It is dropped on the step Poncho reaches the exit or a boss level's clear screen comes up (where "Progress" is written) and on the step the *Siete vidas gastadas* screen comes up (GDD-LIFE-05), in a real level only: a test level never drops it; quitting to the title, "Retry", starting another level and closing the game keep it. Picking "Continue" (GDD-UI-02) resumes it: the level starts (at once, as the next level does, when it is not the one behind the title), with the lives, the used ofrendas and veladora, the clock and the run's hilos as saved (added to the hilos saved for good, which stay faded, GDD-WLD-04), and Poncho comes in at that ofrenda by the respawn flip of a restart (GDD-LIFE-05: it cannot be skipped, the meow plays), in Base with no keys, and everything else at its start, as after a lost life. In 0-1 the tutorial tips show again for the stretch Poncho is in, as on a fresh start (user, 2026-11-01: "show it again"; GDD-UI-05). A saved run that does not read (an unknown level, a checkpoint that is not one of its ofrendas, lives that are not a whole number from 1 to 7, a `hilos` text of the wrong length, a clock that is not a whole number of 0 or more) reads as none; `used` keeps only texts. A `livesIn` that is not a whole number from 1 to 7, as in a run saved before it (which started with seven), reads as 7. Where the browser refuses storage it lasts for the rest of the visit, like the rest of the progress. `src/sim/save.ts`.
- **World 2 (Proposed, not built yet; the version stays 1):** "Progress" adds 2-1, 2-2, 2-3 and the arena 2-4 to the saved real levels, 2-4's `hilos` text empty like 1-4's, and the glide's unlock is read from it (GDD-PRG-01), which is how "Saves: unlocked powers" is met. "World 1's run" becomes each world's run: `worldLives` and `worldAt` keep one entry per world that has levels, such as `"worldLives": { "1": 4, "2": 6 }` and `"worldAt": { "1": "1-1", "2": "2-3" }` (`SAVED_WORLDS` already derives this from `LEVELS`). Each world's entries change only on that world's two steps: at a clear in the world, `worldLives` becomes the lives at the clear and `worldAt` the next level of the same world, and after the world's boss level `worldAt` becomes the world's first level, a new run, while `worldLives` keeps the lives at that clear, which "Start" does not use, since the world's first level always gets seven (as `worldLivesAfter` does now); when the *Siete vidas gastadas* screen comes up in the world, 0 and the world's first level. A clear of 1-4 resets World 1's run to 1-1 and writes nothing for World 2, so 2-1 starts with seven. A save whose `worldAt["2"]` names no real World 2 level keeps no World 2 run. "Saved run" adds 2-1, 2-2 and 2-3; the arena 2-4 has no ofrenda, so no run is saved there. The one saved run may be in any world, and "Continue" resumes it in its world with its lives.

### GDD-PRG-03 Score and medals

Status: Proposed. Medals are kept (user, 2026-10-30); the score is optional and can be cut.

Score from hilos, enemies and time. Level medals: Bronze (clear), Silver (all hilos), Gold (all hilos and at most 3 lives lost in the level; user, 2026-11-02: "Lives lost in the level": "Gold if you lose at most 3 lives in that level, whatever you came in with."). Última Vida doubles hilo score (GDD-LIFE-04).

- **Medals (built; kept, user, 2026-10-30, details Proposed):** the medal is shown on the level clear screen (GDD-UI-02). "All hilos" counts every hilo of the level collected, those taken before a restart after the last life included, since they are kept (GDD-WLD-04, GDD-LIFE-05); a level without hilos has them all. "Lives lost in the level" is the lives the run started with (those carried into the level, GDD-LIFE-05, or seven) less the triangles still whole as Poncho reaches the exit, or in a boss level as its level clear screen comes up (GDD-BOS-01), so a life restored by an ofrenda or the veladora counts against one lost (GDD-LIFE-03; Open Question 18), and a level started with seven gives gold for 4 or more lives at the exit, as before. After the restart that follows the last life the count starts again from its seven, as the clock does (GDD-UI-02, "Clear time"), and a run resumed by "Continue" counts from the lives it started with (GDD-PRG-02, "Saved run"). The boss arena 1-4 has no hilos (GDD-BOS-01), so it has them all: its medal is Gold for at most 3 lives lost in it, else Silver, never Bronze (entered with seven, Gold with 4 lives or more left at its clear; entered with five, with 2 or more; measured by `src/levels/level1-4.test.ts`). Proposed, not built yet: the boss arena 2-4 has the same rule (GDD-BOS-03). 3 is `MEDAL.goldLivesLost` in `src/config/tuning.ts` (measured by `src/sim/run.test.ts` and `src/sim/results.test.ts`). Since progress is saved (GDD-PRG-02), every hilo saved from an earlier run counts too, so Silver and Gold can be built up over runs; the best medal is kept per level. `src/sim/results.ts`.
- **Not built yet:** the score, and Última Vida's double hilo score (Open Question 10).

---

## 11. UI and accessibility

### GDD-UI-01 HUD

Status: Proposed

- Top-left: seven triangles (GDD-LIFE-01), filled = life, outline = lost. Lives fill from the left, so a lost life empties the rightmost filled triangle. Filled and outline differ by shape, not colour alone (GDD-UI-03). Next to them, a small icon of the current power-up state (GDD-PWR-01). After it, one small key per key carried (GDD-WLD-05).
- Top-right: hilo counter. On touch the pause button takes the corner (GDD-UI-02), so the counter goes left of it.
- **Hilo counter (built, details Proposed; user, 2026-10-23: collected/total):** a small skein (the hilo pickup's look, in yellow) and then the hilos collected and the level's total, as "12/20", in 8 px pale monospace text. Its right edge is 8 px from the screen's right edge, as the lives are 8 px from the left, and its top is level with the lives. On a touch device it ends 6 px left of the pause button instead, wherever that button has grown or moved to (GDD-CTL-03, safe-area insets). In the boss arena 1-4, which has no hilos, it reads "0/0" (Open Question 21), and so it will in the arena 2-4 (Proposed, not built yet, GDD-BOS-03). `src/ui/hiloCounter.ts`.
- **Boss bar (built in a boss level, details Proposed):** at the top middle of the screen, the boss's name ("Don Zopilote", the same in every language, GDD-UI-02) in 8 px pale text (`#fff3dc`), its middle 10 px from the top, and under it three pips 6 px square and 4 px apart, their top 18 px from the top, centred together on the screen's middle (x = 227 to 253): one filled (red `#b94842` with a 1 px pale edge) for each hit the boss has left, from the left, and a grey outline (`#9ba3c4`, 1 px) for each hit taken, so they differ by shape, not colour alone (GDD-UI-03). It is there for the whole level, the three outlines of a defeated boss included, and drawn with the rest of the HUD: above the pause, level clear and *Siete vidas gastadas* screens, under the title screen's veil. Nothing else is drawn there: the lives are top left, the hilo counter and the pause button top right. A level without a boss has none. In 2-4 (Proposed, not built yet) it names "El Tlacuachón", the same in every language (GDD-BOS-03). `BOSS_BAR` in `src/config/tuning.ts`, `src/ui/bossPose.ts`.
- Minimal, semi-transparent, hidden during cutscenes. On touch, the touch controls keep clear of the safe-area insets (built, GDD-CTL-03). The HUD should keep clear of them too (user, 2026-11-02: "Later, own PR"); until then the lives, the hilo counter and the boss bar keep their places, the counter moving only with the pause button, so on a screen of about 16:9 with a cutout on the left the first triangles lie under it. On a landscape iPhone nothing of the HUD is covered: the side notches lie in the letterbox bars and there is no top inset.

### GDD-UI-02 Menus and flow

Status: Proposed

Boot, title, world map, level, pause, results, settings. Fully operable by touch, keyboard and gamepad. Pause happens automatically if the tab loses focus or the device rotates to portrait.

- **Pause in a level (built; the button top-right, auto-pause on focus and portrait and resume with the pause key or a tap decided by the user 2026-10-22, at first resume-only, a menu since 2026-10-23, details Proposed):** Escape or P (GDD-CTL-01), or the touch pause button, pauses. The pause button sits in the top-right corner, 6 px from both edges (of the safe area, where an inset reaches into the picture, GDD-CTL-03), 24 px, growing like the other buttons so it is never under 48 CSS px (`TOUCH.pauseSize` in `src/config/tuning.ts`); it shows only on touch devices, drawn as two upright bars. The level stands still under a dim layer with "Paused" and the pause menu below it. The HUD and the touch buttons stay on top.
- **Resume:** Escape or P again (each press counts once, on any keyboard layout), the pause button, a tap or click anywhere but on a menu row, or picking "Resume" from the pause menu. The game keys (jump, claw, Fuego, moving) do not resume by themselves, though Space and Z pick the chosen menu item and Up, W, Down and S choose one; on touch any tap off the menu rows does, on a game button too, but that tap is no game press. A button pressed while paused (the Jump button under the tap that resumed, a key pressed while the game stood still, a Left or Right key or button held through the resume, a finger on the slide stick, GDD-CTL-03) does nothing until it is released and pressed again, also on the *Siete vidas gastadas* screen (GDD-LIFE-05), wherever the finger is (the Resume row reaches into the stick's lower quarter, left or, mirrored, right); a key held at the pause and pressed again while paused is such a press too, and a quick tap of Left or Right while paused counts for nothing after the resume; a button held since before the pause stays held, so a run or a jump carries on.
- **Pause menu (built; a two-item menu decided by the user 2026-10-23, a third item, "Settings", 2026-10-25, details Proposed):** under "Paused", three items, "Resume", "Settings" and "Quit to title", in 12 px text, centred, one under the other. Each item is a row 160 px wide and 28 px tall, the first one's top 136 px from the screen's top, each next one directly below; on a small screen the rows grow so none is under 48 CSS px tall (`PAUSE_MENU` in `src/config/tuning.ts`). The chosen item is pale with a ">" in front of it, the others grey. The menu opens with "Resume" chosen, every time the game pauses. On a keyboard Up or W chooses the item above and Down or S the item below (no wrap-around); Enter, Space or Z picks the chosen item. Each press counts once, on any keyboard layout, and the presses of one frame count in order, so Down, Down and then Enter in one frame quits (they are read from the browser's own key events, like Escape and P). A tap or click on an item's row picks that item at once, whichever is chosen. Picking "Settings" opens the settings screen; leaving it comes back to the pause menu with "Settings" chosen. There is no confirmation for quitting (user, 2026-10-23). While the phone is upright the menu is hidden and nothing on it can be chosen or picked; turned sideways again, the item chosen before is still chosen (the menu did not open again).
- **Quit to title:** picking "Quit to title" brings back the title screen, waiting for a press as when the game opens. The level behind it is a fresh run of the level "Start" plays (see "Which level"), as when the game opens (user, 2026-10-23): the lives "Start" gives it (seven, or the lives World 1 keeps, see "Which level"; a triangle the run quit had filled and the fresh one has not shows no crack, and a last life pulses from the level's start, GDD-LIFE-04), every ofrenda unused and the respawn point at the level start, every hilo not saved yet back (GDD-WLD-04, GDD-PRG-02), Poncho in Base with no keys, and every piñata, door, fragile wall, taco, enemy, crumbling tile, moving platform and moving block back at its start. Poncho stands at the level start with no respawn flip and no invulnerability, and no sound plays. The press that quit does not start the level: the button that picked counts as already held on the title screen, and an Enter press, tap or click in the same frame does not count there.
- **Automatic pause:** the tab or window losing focus or being hidden pauses; it never resumes by itself. A touch device whose screen is upright pauses (the screen's orientation decides, or the window's shape where the browser has none), and the pause menu gives way to "Turn your device sideways", in 12 px grey text, larger than the 8 px prompts since the whole level is drawn small; no key, button or tap resumes until it is sideways again, and then it stays paused until the player resumes. A desktop window made taller than wide does not pause, nor does a touchscreen laptop's, whose screen stays landscape. `src/sim/pause.ts`.
- **Title screen (built; the level behind a veil with the placeholder Poncho decided by the user 2026-10-23, the prompt replaced by the title menu 2026-10-25, details Proposed):** the game opens on it. The level "Start" plays (see "Which level" below) is drawn standing still (no simulation step runs) under a dark veil like the *Siete vidas gastadas* screen's (GDD-LIFE-05), which covers the HUD too; the touch buttons are hidden. Over the veil, centred: "PONCHO" in large pale text (32 px), "Siete Vidas" under it (16 px), Poncho's standing frame (GDD-ART-04) at his size in the level, standing left of the two lines with his feet level with the bottom of "Siete Vidas" (104 px from the screen's top) and 8 px between him and the wider line's left edge (user, 2026-10-31: beside the title, so four rows fit; it stood above the menu before), and under them the title menu (user, 2026-10-25; the third row, 2026-10-28; "Continue", 2026-10-31): "Continue" while a saved run exists (GDD-PRG-02, "Saved run"), then "Start", "Tutorial" and "Settings", drawn like the pause menu (12 px, 160 x 28 px rows growing to 48 CSS px, the first one's top 112 px from the screen's top, so four fit on a landscape phone, where they grow to 34 px; `TITLE_MENU` in `src/config/tuning.ts`), with "Continue" chosen whenever the title screen appears with it, else "Start". "PONCHO" and "Siete Vidas" have their middles 68 and 96 px from the screen's top (Proposed).
- **Starting (the title menu, with the other buttons still starting, decided by the user 2026-10-25):** Up or W chooses the row above and Down or S the row below (no wrap-around); Enter, Space or Z picks the chosen item, and a tap or click on a row picks that row. Picking "Start" starts the level, picking "Tutorial" starts 0-1 (see "Which level" below) and picking "Continue" resumes the saved run (GDD-PRG-02); none of them drops the saved run. A tap or click anywhere off the rows (on the hidden buttons' places and the pause button's too) and a new press of claw, Fuego, Left or Right (GDD-CTL-01) start the level as "Start" does, whichever row is chosen. Picking "Settings" opens the settings screen; leaving it comes back to the title with "Settings" chosen. The jump keys start the level only by picking: Space and Z pick, Up and W choose. The touch buttons are hidden and press nothing on the title screen, nor on the settings screen opened from it: a finger on their places, the slide stick's lower quarter included (left, or right when mirrored), is only a tap (a finger on the hidden mode switch does not change the mode, GDD-CTL-03). Only a game button that was up on the frame before counts, so pressing a second button while holding one does; a tap shorter than one frame counts (the game buttons are latched, L-006; Enter, Space, Z and the menu keys are read from the browser's own key events like Escape and P). Escape and P do nothing on the title screen, and losing focus there does not pause the level behind it. The button that started the level counts as already held, as after a resume: a jump held from the title screen does not make Poncho jump until it is released and pressed again, and a Left or Right key, button or slide-stick finger down when the level starts does not move him until it is released. `src/sim/title.ts`.
- **Upright on the title screen:** a touch device whose screen is upright (the same test as the automatic pause) hides the title menu and shows "Turn your device sideways" in its place, in grey 12 px text, also when the game opens that way; nothing on the menu can be chosen or picked until it is sideways again (the presses of a frame count in order, like the pause menu's, so a pick of "Settings" just before a turn upright opens the settings screen, hidden until the phone is sideways), and the level starts only in a frame that ends sideways (so a tap and a turn upright together do not start it); sideways again, the title menu is back with the item chosen before still chosen. So the level never starts upright.
- **Settings screen (built; volume rows, Left and Right to change, "<" and ">" on touch, saved in the browser, decided by the user 2026-10-25, details Proposed):** opened from the pause menu or the title menu, it replaces that menu: the same dark layer, "Settings" in 16 px pale text, centred, its middle 44 px from the screen's top, and under it six rows, "Master volume", "Effects volume", "Language", "Button opacity", "Layout" and "Back", each 260 px wide and 28 px tall, centred, the first one's top 62 px from the screen's top, each next one directly below, growing like the pause menu's rows so none is under 48 CSS px tall (`SETTINGS_MENU` in `src/config/tuning.ts`; on a landscape phone they grow to 34 px, so the six end 266 px from the top). A volume row shows its name in 12 px text from 20 px inside the row's left edge, a "<" 8 px inside its left edge, a ">" 8 px inside its right edge, and its value as 10 small upright bars, 5 px wide and 10 px tall with 2 px between them, centred in the row's height, the last one ending 20 px inside the row's right edge: as many filled as the value, the rest outlined. The chosen row is pale and the others grey; "Back", when chosen, has a ">" in front of it like the pause menu. The screen opens with "Master volume" chosen, every time. On a keyboard Up or W chooses the row above and Down or S the row below (no wrap-around); Left or A lowers the chosen volume by 1 and Right or D raises it by 1, from 0 to 10 (no wrap-around; the default is 10, `VOLUME` in `src/config/tuning.ts`); Enter, Space or Z on "Back", Escape, P or the touch pause button leave the screen, back to the menu it was opened from; Enter, Space or Z on a volume row does nothing. Each press counts once, read like the pause menu's keys, in order within a frame. On touch, a tap or click on a volume row chooses it and, on the row's left half, lowers it by 1, on its right half raises it by 1; a tap on "Back" leaves; a tap anywhere else does nothing (it does not resume). A change that moves a volume plays the jump sound at the new volume, so the player hears it; at 0, or at 10 when raised, nothing changes and nothing plays. Every sound effect plays at Master times Effects, each as a share of 10 (GDD-AUD-01, GDD-AUD-02). Opened from the pause menu, it lies under the HUD and the touch buttons like the pause menu, and a button or finger pressed on it is held back after the resume like any press made while paused; opened from the title, it covers everything like the title screen. A phone turned upright hides it like the pause menu, showing "Turn your device sideways"; turned sideways again, the same row is still chosen. Losing focus changes nothing on it. `src/sim/settings.ts`, `src/ui/settingsMenu.ts`.
- **Language (built; a Language row with English, Español and Deutsch, user, 2026-10-25; every text on screen switches and a first visit takes the browser's language, user, 2026-10-29; details and the Spanish and German texts Proposed):** the "Language" row is drawn like a volume row (its name from 20 px inside its left edge, "<" and ">"), with the chosen language's own name ("English", "Español" or "Deutsch", 12 px, in the row's colour) centred where a volume row's bars are, so it reads the same in every language. The languages go in that order. Left or A, or a tap on the row's left half, chooses the language before; Right or D, or a tap on its right half, the one after; at either end nothing changes (no wrap-around), and Enter, Space or Z on it does nothing, like the volume rows. A change plays no sound, is saved at once (see "Saving the settings") and from that frame every text below is drawn in the new language, the settings screen's own included, so its title and rows change under the player's eyes while the same row stays chosen. The names "PONCHO", "Siete Vidas", "Siete vidas gastadas", "Hilos", "Don Zopilote" (GDD-BOS-02) and the language names are the same in every language, and so are the placeholder and dev lines of the test levels (GDD-UI-05). The texts (12 px rows and prompts, 16 px titles and 8 px prompts as in English):

  | English | Español | Deutsch |
  | --- | --- | --- |
  | Start | Jugar | Spielen |

  The tutorial's tips are listed in each language in GDD-UI-05.
- **Button opacity (built; user, 2026-11-01: three steps, "Low / Medium / High"; details Proposed):** the "Button opacity" row is drawn like the Language row, with the step's name ("Low", "Medium" or "High", in the language chosen, 12 px, in the row's colour) centred where a volume row's bars are. The steps go in that order. Left or A, or a tap on the row's left half, chooses the step before; Right or D, or a tap on its right half, the one after; at either end nothing changes (no wrap-around), and Enter, Space or Z on it does nothing. A change plays no sound and is saved at once (see "Saving the settings"). The default is Medium. The row is there on every device; it changes the touch buttons (GDD-CTL-03), which a device without touch does not show. Opened from the pause menu, the buttons drawn above the settings screen change as the step is picked.
- **Layout (built; user, 2026-11-01: a row "Layout: Standard / Mirrored", the pause button staying top-right; details Proposed):** the "Layout" row is drawn like the Button opacity row, with "Standard" or "Mirrored" (in the language chosen) where a volume row's bars are. Standard comes first. Left or A, or a tap on the row's left half, chooses the step before; Right or D, or a tap on its right half, the one after; at either end nothing changes, and Enter, Space or Z on it does nothing. A change plays no sound and is saved at once (see "Saving the settings"). The default is Standard. The row is there on every device; it moves the touch buttons (GDD-CTL-03, "Mirrored layout"), which a device without touch does not show. Opened from the pause menu, the buttons drawn above the settings screen move as the step is picked.
- **The first language:** a first visit, or a save with no language or one that is not `en`, `es` or `de`, uses the browser's languages (`navigator.languages`, else `navigator.language`), in the browser's order: the first one whose main part (before any `-`, in any case) is `en`, `es` or `de` decides, so `fr-FR, de-AT` gives Deutsch; none gives English. Until something is saved the browser's language is followed, so a new one counts at the next visit; once the game saves (a setting changed, or the tutorial started), the save holds the language in use, and that one is used from then on.
- **Saving the settings (built, details Proposed):** every change, of a volume, the language, the Button opacity or the Layout, is saved in the browser's local storage at once, and the game opens with the saved settings (GDD-PRG-02). Nothing saved, or something unreadable, opens with the defaults, and a saved value that is not a whole number from 0 to 10 falls back to its default alone (the language to the browser's, see "The first language"; a Button opacity that is not `low`, `medium` or `high` to Medium, a Layout that is not `standard` or `mirrored` to Standard). Where the browser refuses storage the game plays with the settings of this visit and saves nothing, without an error; a level started as a scene of its own (the next level, or a title or pick over another level) keeps them too.
- **Gamepad (built):** the D-pad or left stick works like the arrow keys, south like Enter and start like Escape on every menu, and start on the title screen starts the level (GDD-CTL-02).
- **Which level (built; user, 2026-10-25, 1-1 added 2026-10-27, the tutorial played once 2026-10-28, World 1's run followed and its lives saved 2026-11-02, details Proposed):** "Start" plays the tutorial 0-1 (GDD-WLD-06), with seven lives, until 0-1 has been started once; 0-1 counts as played the moment it starts, by "Start" or by "Tutorial", even if the player quits at once (user, 2026-10-28), and this is saved in the browser (GDD-PRG-02). From then on "Start" follows the World 1 run the save keeps (user, 2026-11-02: "Follow the run": "The save remembers where your World 1 run is: Start plays the next level in order of the current run (1-1 after a game over, then 1-2, ...). Clears still count for medals.", which replaced their first answer, "First level not cleared"; "Save the lives": "The save keeps the World 1 lives too: Start plays the first level not cleared with the lives you had, and after a game over the world restarts at 1-1 with 7, however you get there."): it plays the level the run has reached (GDD-PRG-02, "World 1's run") from its start, with the lives the save keeps for World 1, or seven where it keeps none; 1-1, World 1's start, always with seven, and 1-1 with seven where the run is kept with 0 lives (only a damaged save holds that: a game over moves the run back to 1-1). So it plays 1-1 until 1-1 is cleared, then 1-2 with the lives at 1-1's clear until 1-2 is cleared, then 1-3 with the lives at 1-2's clear until 1-3 is cleared, then the boss arena 1-4 (GDD-BOS-02) with the lives at 1-3's clear until 1-4 is cleared, then 1-1 with seven again, a new run, since 1-4 is World 1's last level (Proposed); after the *Siete vidas gastadas* screen in a World 1 level, 1-4 included, it plays 1-1 with seven, whatever is cleared (GDD-LIFE-05). A save that keeps no World 1 run (one from before the run was kept, or whose run names no real World 1 level) keeps the older rule: the first World 1 level, in World 1's order (1-1, 1-2, 1-3, then the arena 1-4), that the saved progress does not hold as cleared (GDD-PRG-02, "Progress"), with the lives World 1 keeps, or seven where it keeps none, but 1-1 always with seven, 1-1 once every World 1 level is cleared, and 1-1 where World 1 keeps 0 lives. The test levels are not among World 1's levels here. Where the browser refuses storage, the run, the lives and the levels cleared in this visit count. "Tutorial" always plays 0-1 with seven, and "Continue" resumes a run saved at an ofrenda inside a level, with its lives (GDD-PRG-02, "Saved run"). An address picks what "Start" plays instead, whatever was played, cleared or kept, with seven lives: `?level=1-1`, `?level=1-2`, `?level=1-3`, `?level=1-4` (the boss arena, GDD-BOS-02), `?level=0-1`, or a test level: the sandbox with `?level=sandbox`, the slopes level with `?level=slopes` (GDD-MOV-09); any other `level`, or none, gives the rule above. The title screen shows the start of the level "Start" plays behind the veil, with the lives it gives: when the game opens, after quitting to the title, from the pause menu or the results menu. So after the tutorial has started once, quitting it brings back the title over 1-1, or over 1-2, 1-3 or the arena 1-4 while the run is there. When the level picked is not the one behind the veil ("Tutorial" over a World 1 level, or "Start" over a level "Start" no longer plays), it starts at once from its own start with seven lives, as the next level starts (below), with the press held. The playtests use the sandbox, except those for 0-1, 1-1, 1-2, 1-3, the boss arena 1-4 (`e2e/boss.spec.ts`), the slopes level and World 1's run and lives (`e2e/world1.spec.ts`). `startLevel` in `src/levels/index.ts`.
- **The next level (built; user, 2026-10-27: a press on 0-1's level clear screen starts 1-1; since 2026-10-31 a pick of "Next level" on the results menu; details Proposed):** a level can name the level after it; 0-1 names 1-1, 1-1 names 1-2 (GDD-WLD-08), 1-2 names 1-3 (GDD-WLD-09) and 1-3 names the boss arena 1-4 (GDD-BOS-02); 1-4, World 1's last level until World 2 exists, and the test levels (the sandbox, the slopes level) name none. In a level that names one, picking "Next level" on the results menu (see "Results menu") starts that level at once, with no title screen: a fresh run of it, with the lives Poncho has at the clear when both levels are in one of the seven worlds, as 1-1, 1-2, 1-3 and 1-4 are, and with seven after the tutorial, which is outside them (GDD-LIFE-05), drawn from its start with the camera there (GDD-UI-04). The button that pressed counts as already held there, on any device, as after a resume: a key, finger or gamepad button still down does nothing until it is released and pressed again, and so does a key that the browser's auto-repeat presses again (a key held through the change shows there only as a repeat). The settings carry over (GDD-PRG-02). Quitting to the title from 1-1, 1-2, 1-3 or 1-4, by the pause menu or by the results menu, brings back the title screen over a fresh run of the level "Start" plays, with the lives it gives, as for any level: the level the World 1 run has reached (see "Which level"), so 1-1 with seven until 1-1 is cleared, 1-2 with the lives at 1-1's clear until 1-2 is cleared, 1-3 with the lives at 1-2's clear until 1-3 is cleared, 1-4 with the lives at 1-3's clear until 1-4 is cleared, and 1-1 with seven after that or after the *Siete vidas gastadas* screen in any of them; a new visit to the game opens on the same level with the same lives. "Continue" resumes a run saved at one of their ofrendas (1-4 has none, GDD-BOS-01), with its lives (GDD-PRG-02).
- **Level clear screen (built; a placeholder for the results screen, user, 2026-10-25; the time, the medal and the reveal, user, 2026-10-30; the menu, user, 2026-10-31; details Proposed):** when Poncho reaches the exit (GDD-WLD-03), or in a boss level 1.0 s (60 steps) after the hit that defeats the boss (GDD-BOS-01), the level stays in view, standing still, under the dark veil of the *Siete vidas gastadas* screen (GDD-LIFE-05), with, centred, one under the other: "Level clear!" in 16 px pale text (its middle 92 px from the screen's top), the hilos as "Hilos 31/50", collected and the level's total ("Hilos 0/0" in the boss arena 1-4, which has none, Open Question 21; 12 px, pale, its middle at 116 px), the clear time as "Time 2:14" (12 px, pale, at 132 px; see "Best time"), the medal (at 150 px) and, from its first 1.0 s (60 steps) on, the results menu (below). Poncho stays drawn where he reached it, or in a boss level where he is as the screen comes up. The HUD and the touch buttons stay on top. A new press of jump, claw, Fuego, left or right, on any device, counts only for the reveal (below); a tap shorter than a frame counts, and a button held as Poncho reaches the exit, or as a boss level's clear screen comes up, is no new press. No button leaves the screen: only a pick on the results menu does. Not built yet: the score (GDD-PRG-03), the world map and a sound for the screen itself.
- **Clear time (user, 2026-10-30, details Proposed):** the time from the level start to the exit (in a boss level, to the step its clear screen comes up), counted in simulation steps (60 a second), so the pause, the settings screen and the title screen do not count, and the waits after a lost life do. The restart after the last life (GDD-LIFE-05) starts it again from 0, as it gives back the seven lives, also when it plays 1-1 after the last life was lost in 1-2, 1-3 or 1-4. It is shown as minutes and whole seconds, rounded down: "Time 0:07", "Time 2:14", "Time 12:05", and "Time 99:59" for anything longer. `src/sim/results.ts`.
- **Medal line (user, 2026-10-30, details Proposed):** the medal of GDD-PRG-03 as "Bronze medal", "Silver medal" or "Gold medal" (in the chosen language, see "Language") in 12 px text in the medal's colour, with a filled disc 10 px across in the same colour 6 px left of the text, the pair centred together. Bronze is `#c7834a`, silver `#c9cfdc`, gold `#f2c14e` (placeholders until medal art exists). `src/sim/results.ts`.
- **Best time and new bests (user, 2026-10-31: the best time and the best medal are kept, and the screen says when a new best is reached; details Proposed):** the time line adds, after two spaces, the level's saved best time before this run, as "Best 1:58" in the format of the clear time, or "New best!" when this run's time is shorter than it. The medal line adds "New best!", after two spaces and in the medal's colour, when this run's medal is better than the saved one; the disc and text stay centred together. A level cleared for the first time has no best yet: its time line and medal line show nothing more. Both words are in the language chosen (see "Language"). The saved bests read are those from before this run; the save is written on the same step (GDD-PRG-02). `src/sim/results.ts`.
- **Reveal (user, 2026-10-30: lines one by one with a tick; details Proposed):** "Level clear!" shows as the screen comes up, then the hilo line at 0.25 s (15 steps), the time line at 0.5 s (30 steps) and the medal line at 0.75 s (45 steps), each playing the tick (GDD-AUD-02) on the step it appears. A new press of jump, claw, Fuego, left or right before the medal line shows shows every line at once and plays one tick; the results menu still appears at 1.0 s. A press after the medal line does nothing. `RESULTS` in `src/config/tuning.ts`.
- **Results menu (user, 2026-10-31: "Next level", "Retry" and "Quit to title", Retry a fresh run, "Next level" greyed out where there is none; details Proposed):** from the level clear screen's first 1.0 s on, three rows, "Next level", "Retry" and "Quit to title", drawn like the pause menu (12 px, centred, 160 x 28 px rows growing so none is under 48 CSS px, the first one's top 162 px from the screen's top, each next one directly below; `RESULTS_MENU` in `src/config/tuning.ts`). The chosen row is pale with a ">" in front of it, the others grey. It opens with "Next level" chosen, or "Retry" in a level that names no next level (see "The next level"; the boss arena 1-4), where "Next level" is drawn darker (`#5c6178`) and can be neither chosen nor picked: Up or W from "Retry" stays there and a tap on it does nothing. On a keyboard Up or W chooses the row above and Down or S the row below (no wrap-around); Enter, Space or Z picks the chosen row, and a tap or click on a row picks that row at once; a tap anywhere else does nothing. Each press counts once and the presses of one frame count in order, read from the browser's own key events like the pause menu's. The gamepad's D-pad or left stick and south work like the arrow keys and Enter (GDD-CTL-02). Claw, Fuego, Left, Right and the touch buttons pick nothing; the jump keys Space and Z pick only by their key events. Escape, P, the pause button, the gamepad's start, losing focus and a phone turned upright pause the game as anywhere in a level: the pause menu comes over the screen, the results menu hidden under it, and resuming brings the results menu back with the same row chosen. Picking "Next level" starts the next level (see "The next level"); picking "Retry" starts a fresh run of the same level at once, as the next level starts: from its start, with the lives Poncho has at the clear in a World 1 level (user, 2026-11-02: "Lives at the clear": "The lives you finished the level with; its ofrendas and veladora can win some back before Next level.") and with seven in the tutorial (GDD-LIFE-05), every ofrenda and the veladora unused (GDD-LIFE-03), every hilo not saved yet back (GDD-WLD-04, GDD-PRG-02), the clock at 0:00, the button that picked held; picking "Quit to title" brings back the title screen over a fresh run of the level "Start" plays (see "Which level"), as the pause menu's "Quit to title" does, the button that picked held there. `src/sim/results.ts`, `src/ui/pauseMenu.ts`.
- **World 2 (Proposed, not built yet; what "Start" plays changes the user's quoted "Follow the run" and needs their confirmation, Open Question 30):**
  - **Which level:** from then on "Start" follows the run of the newest open world: World 2 once the save holds 1-4 as cleared ("Progress"), else World 1. A world opens when the boss level of the world before it is cleared and the world is complete, its boss level built (GDD-WLD-01); until then its levels are opened by address only. Within that world "Start" plays the level its run has reached, with the lives the save keeps for it; the world's first level always gets seven, as does a run kept with 0 lives. So a save that has cleared 1-4 plays 2-1 with seven until 2-1 is cleared, then 2-2 with the lives at 2-1's clear, then 2-3, then the arena 2-4; after 2-4 is cleared, 2-1 with seven again, a new run, now with the glide (GDD-PRG-01); after the *Siete vidas gastadas* screen in any World 2 level, 2-1 with seven. A save that cleared 1-4 before World 2 existed opens on 2-1 with seven, its World 1 run kept at 1-1. The older rule for a world with no kept run applies per world, inside the newest open world. Addresses add `?level=2-1`, `?level=2-2`, `?level=2-3` and `?level=2-4`, each with seven. The title shows the start of the level "Start" plays, so after World 1 is cleared it shows 2-1 in the Mercado look. `startLevel` stops being World 1 only. World 1 is replayed by an address until the world map exists (GDD-WLD-01).
  - **The next level:** 2-1 names 2-2, 2-2 names 2-3 and 2-3 names the arena 2-4; once World 2 is complete, 1-4 names 2-1 (GDD-WLD-01), and 2-4, the game's last level until World 3 exists, names none. The lives carry when both levels are in the same world, so 1-4 to 2-1 gives seven (GDD-LIFE-05). Quitting to the title from a World 2 level brings back the title over the level "Start" plays.
  - **Results menu:** "Next level" is greyed in 2-4, and live in 1-4 once World 2 is complete, starting 2-1. "Retry" starts with the lives at the clear in a World 2 level as in a World 1 level.
  - **The unlock line:** on the clear of 2-4 that first unlocks the glide (GDD-PRG-01), the line "New power: Glide!" ("¡Nuevo poder: planeo!", "Neue Kraft: Gleiten!"; Spanish and German Proposed) appears above "Level clear!", 12 px, in the hilo counter's yellow, its middle 68 px from the screen's top (below the boss bar), at 1.0 s with the results menu, playing the tick; a press that skips the reveal shows it at once; later clears of 2-4 do not show it (Open Question 29). It joins the language table when built.
  - **Not in this pass (Open Question 31):** a level card with the level's id and name, names for World 1's levels, and a "Start" row that names the level it plays. World 2's level names (GDD-WLD-12 to GDD-WLD-14) live in the GDD and the code only for now.
- **Not built yet:** boot and world map screens.

### GDD-UI-03 Accessibility

Status: Proposed

- Remappable keys and adjustable touch layout (size, mirror, opacity; the mirror and the opacity are built, GDD-CTL-03).
- Assist mode: infinite lives, slower game speed (0.75x), hold-to-jump toggle.
- Reduced motion and screen-shake off. High-contrast outlines for hazards.
- Never rely on colour alone to signal danger. Subtitles for any spoken line.
- English, Spanish and German text, with a language switch (German added by the user, 2026-10-25): the Language row of the settings screen, GDD-UI-02.

### GDD-UI-04 Camera

Status: Proposed (user, 2026-10-25: it follows Poncho sideways only, with a small dead zone, and stops at the level's edges).

- The camera shows 480 x 270 game px of the level. It follows Poncho left and right: while the middle of his drawn body is inside the dead zone, 32 px wide in the middle of the screen (from 224 to 256 px from the screen's left edge), it stands still; when he goes past either side of it, it moves with him so that he stays on that side's edge. It never shows anything past the level's left or right edge, so a level one screen wide (the sandbox, the slopes level) never scrolls. It does not move up or down: levels are one screen tall for now.
- It follows the body where it is drawn, between steps, so it moves smoothly, and it stands at whole art pixels (a quarter of a game px, GDD-ART-01). While Poncho is hidden waiting to respawn it stands still; when he appears at the respawn point it moves there at once. On the title screen and in a fresh run he is at the level start, so it shows the level start.
- The HUD, the screens (title, pause, settings, *Siete vidas gastadas*, level clear) and the touch buttons do not move with it. The placeholder title, controls and dev lines at the top of a test level's start move with the level.
- `CAMERA` in `src/config/tuning.ts`, `src/ui/camera.ts`.


### GDD-UI-05 Tutorial tips

Status: Proposed (user, 2026-10-26: texts and highlights pop up while Poncho is in a stretch of the level and play goes on; they name the buttons of the device used last; the highlight is a pulsing outline). The wording, sizes and timings are Proposed.

- **When:** a level can have tips (only the tutorial 0-1 does, GDD-WLD-06; with World 2, Proposed and not built yet, also 2-1 and 2-2, below). Each tip has a stretch of tile columns. While Poncho is playing (not waiting to respawn, not on a screen) and the middle of his body is in a tip's stretch, that tip is shown; the stretches do not overlap. Play never stops for a tip. It fades in over 0.2 s (12 steps) and out over 0.2 s when he leaves the stretch, counted in steps so it stands still with the game; when he goes from one tip's stretch into the next, the old one goes at once and the new one fades in. A tip shows again whenever he comes back to its stretch. A tip about a thing that is done goes for good once it is done: the Alacrán tip once that Alacrán is defeated, the piñata tip once the piñata is open, the veladora tip once it is used, until the run starts afresh (a restart after the last life or quitting to the title).
- **Text:** in a box at the top middle of the screen, under the HUD: 8 px pale text (`#fff3dc`), at most two lines, on a dark box (`#0d1020` at 0.75) with 4 px of room around the text, wrapped at 280 px, its top 26 px from the screen's top. It does not move with the camera (GDD-UI-04). The placeholder title, controls and dev lines at the top of the level start are drawn in the test levels only (the sandbox and the slopes level, GDD-MOV-09): in the tutorial the tips take their place, and 1-1, 1-2, 1-3 and 1-4 have neither (the dev keys still work everywhere); of World 2's levels (Proposed, not built yet) 2-3 and 2-4 have neither, and 2-1 and 2-2 have one tip each.
- **Device:** the text names the buttons of the device used last: the keyboard (any key press), touch (a finger on the screen) or a gamepad (any button or stick push past its dead zone). Before any is used it is touch on a device that reports touch, otherwise the keyboard. A mouse click does not change it.
- **Highlight:** with each tip, its thing in the level gets a dashed pale outline (`#fff3dc`, 1 px, dashes of 4 px with 3 px between them) 2 px outside it, which grows by up to 2 px on each side and back, smoothly, every 0.8 s (48 steps), and a small pale triangle 6 px wide pointing down, its tip 6 px above it, bobbing 2 px with the same beat. The outline is a shape, not a colour (GDD-UI-03). It moves with the level and fades with its tip. While touch is the device used last, the tip about running, jumping or the claw also draws the same dashed outline round that touch button (the left and right buttons, or the slide stick's track, for running).
- **The tips of 0-1** (stretch in tile columns; highlighted thing; keyboard / touch / gamepad text):
  1. Columns 0 to 5; none, but on touch the move buttons: "Arrows or A/D to run" / "Hold left or right to run" / "Stick or D-pad to run".
  2. Columns 6 to 13; the 2-tile wall (columns 11 and 12, rows 12 and 13): "Space to jump. Hold it to jump higher" / "Tap the jump button. Hold it to jump higher" / "Bottom button to jump. Hold it to jump higher".
  3. Columns 14 to 20; the two gaps (columns 16 to 20, row 14): "Jump over the gaps" (every device).
  4. Columns 21 to 25; the first ofrenda: "Ofrendas are checkpoints. The first touch gives back a life" (every device).
  5. Columns 26 to 38; the first Alacrán's pen (columns 28 to 36, rows 12 and 13): "Jump on the Alacrán, or X to claw it" / "Jump on the Alacrán, or tap the claw button" / "Jump on the Alacrán, or left button to claw it". Gone once that Alacrán is defeated.
  6. Columns 39 to 41; the piñata: "Jump into the piñata from below" (every device). Gone once it is open.
  7. Columns 42 to 47; the spikes (columns 43 and 44, row 13): "Spikes hurt. With a taco, a hit costs only the taco" (every device).
  8. Columns 48 to 52; the second ofrenda: "Hilos are threads to collect. Find all 50!" (every device).
  9. Columns 53 to 57; the 3-tile gap (columns 53 to 55, row 14): "A running jump crosses 3 tiles" (every device).
  10. Columns 58 to 61; the 3-tile wall (columns 58 and 59, rows 11 to 13): "A full jump climbs a wall 3 tiles high" (every device).
  11. Columns 62 to 75; the veladora: "A veladora up there gives back a life" (every device). Gone once it is used.
  12. Columns 82 to 89; the exit: "Reach the arch to finish the level" (every device).
- **The tips of 0-1 in Spanish and German** (Proposed, 2026-10-29; the same stretches and highlights, in the language chosen, GDD-UI-02; keyboard / touch / gamepad where they differ):
  1. "Flechas o A/D para correr" / "Mantén izquierda o derecha para correr" / "Stick o cruceta para correr"; "Pfeiltasten oder A/D zum Laufen" / "Links oder rechts halten zum Laufen" / "Stick oder Steuerkreuz zum Laufen".
  2. "Espacio para saltar. Mantenlo para saltar más alto" / "Toca el botón de salto. Mantenlo para saltar más alto" / "Botón inferior para saltar. Mantenlo para saltar más alto"; "Leertaste zum Springen. Halten für höhere Sprünge" / "Sprungknopf antippen. Halten für höhere Sprünge" / "Untere Taste zum Springen. Halten für höhere Sprünge".
  3. "Salta los huecos"; "Spring über die Lücken".
  4. "Las ofrendas son puntos de control. El primer toque devuelve una vida"; "Ofrendas sind Kontrollpunkte. Die erste Berührung gibt ein Leben zurück".
  5. "Salta sobre el Alacrán, o presiona X para arañarlo" / "Salta sobre el Alacrán, o toca el botón de zarpazo" / "Salta sobre el Alacrán, o botón izquierdo para arañarlo"; "Spring auf den Alacrán oder kratz ihn mit X" / "Spring auf den Alacrán oder tipp auf den Krallenknopf" / "Spring auf den Alacrán oder kratz ihn mit der linken Taste".
  6. "Salta contra la piñata desde abajo"; "Spring von unten gegen die Piñata".
  7. "Los picos hacen daño. Con un taco, un golpe solo cuesta el taco"; "Stacheln tun weh. Mit einem Taco kostet ein Treffer nur den Taco".
  8. "Los hilos son para coleccionar. ¡Encuentra los 50!"; "Hilos sind Fäden zum Sammeln. Finde alle 50!".
  9. "Un salto con impulso cruza 3 casillas"; "Ein Sprung mit Anlauf überquert 3 Felder".
  10. "Un salto completo sube un muro de 3 casillas"; "Ein voller Sprung erklimmt eine 3 Felder hohe Wand".
  11. "Una veladora ahí arriba devuelve una vida"; "Eine Veladora dort oben gibt ein Leben zurück".
  12. "Llega al arco para terminar el nivel"; "Erreiche den Bogen, um das Level zu beenden".
  Every tip in every language fits the two lines of the box.
- **World 2's tips (Proposed, not built yet; Open Question 31):** one tip for each signature element, over its safe first introduction (GDD-WLD-03), each gone for good once done: the awning tip in 2-1 over the first awning (GDD-WLD-10, GDD-WLD-12), done after Poncho's first bounce on an awning, and the string tip in 2-2 over the first string (GDD-WLD-11, GDD-WLD-13), done after his first stand on a string. Their stretches are set when the levels are built. No glide tip in World 2: the glide's announcement is the unlock line on 2-4's clear screen (GDD-UI-02). In the Spanish text an awning is a "toldo" and a string a "cordel"; in German "Markise" and "Papel-picado-Schnur". Each text is to fit the box's two lines (checked when built).
  1. Awning (keyboard / touch / gamepad): "Hold Space as you land on an awning to bounce high" / "Hold the jump button as you land on an awning to bounce high" / "Hold the bottom button as you land on an awning to bounce high"; "Mantén Espacio al caer sobre un toldo para rebotar alto" / "Mantén el botón de salto al caer sobre un toldo para rebotar alto" / "Mantén el botón inferior al caer sobre un toldo para rebotar alto"; "Halte die Leertaste beim Landen auf einer Markise, um hoch zu federn" / "Halte den Sprungknopf beim Landen auf einer Markise, um hoch zu federn" / "Halte die untere Taste beim Landen auf einer Markise, um hoch zu federn".
  2. String (every device): "Papel picado strings hold you up. Jump up through them from below"; "Los cordeles de papel picado te sostienen. Salta a través de ellos desde abajo"; "Papel-picado-Schnüre tragen dich. Spring von unten hindurch".
- `TIPS` in `src/config/tuning.ts`, the tips in `src/levels/level0-1.ts`, `src/ui/tips.ts`.

## 12. Audio

### GDD-AUD-01 Music

Status: Proposed

Upbeat chiptune-and-acoustic mix: marimba, guitarrón, guitar, trumpets, played in the spirit of regional styles of each world. Each world has its own theme and a tense variation for Última Vida. All music is original or properly licensed. Audio is unlocked on first user gesture (browser requirement). Master, music and effects volumes are adjustable. Built: Master and Effects volume on the settings screen (GDD-UI-02); a Music volume row comes with the music.

- **World 2's theme (Proposed, not built yet; Open Question 33):** a bright mid-tempo street tune in the spirit of a Mexico City *organillero*, the barrel organ heard in markets: the organ carries the lilting melody over marimba, guitar and a light chiptune bass. The Última Vida variation drops the organ to a single wavering line over a faster guitar strum. Like World 1's, a generated placeholder until Open Question 9 is answered.

### GDD-AUD-02 Sound effects

Status: Proposed (the ofrenda sound being a marimba is Decided, user, 2026-10-20)

Short, punchy and readable: jump, land, claw, stomp, hilo pickup (rising pitch combo), life lost (unravelling thread sound), ofrenda activate (marimba), the results tick, Poncho meows as personality. Each SFX is under 1 s and compressed for the web.

- **Ofrenda activate:** two rosewood marimba bars struck in quick succession, E5 then B5, 0.95 s. Generated from code (`scripts/audio/ofrenda.mjs`, encoded by `scripts/audio/make-sfx.mjs`) as a placeholder until real audio exists (Open Question 9). Shipped as Ogg and MP3. It plays when an ofrenda is used for the first time, not on a repeat touch and not when a restart resets the ofrendas.
- **When the other sounds play (user, 2026-10-23: the meow plays on respawn; the rest Proposed).** Each is generated from code (`scripts/audio/effects.mjs`, encoded by `scripts/audio/make-sfx.mjs`) as a placeholder until real audio exists (Open Question 9), shipped as Ogg and MP3, and plays on the step it happens. All but the meow and the tick play only while Poncho is in play; the meow plays as he appears (and as Don Zopilote is defeated, a step in play, GDD-BOS-02) and the tick on the level clear screen, below. Numbers are in `SFX` in `src/config/tuning.ts`.
  - **Jump:** a short rising chirp, 0.15 s, on every jump takeoff (from the ground, in coyote time or from the jump buffer, GDD-MOV-03) and every wall kick (GDD-MOV-08). Not on a spring launch (GDD-WLD-05) or a stomp bounce (GDD-CMB-01), which are not jumps.
  - **Land:** a soft thump, 0.12 s, when Poncho touches down from the air while falling at 60 px/s or faster, so stepping onto a platform or a tiny drop stays quiet. Not when the landing is a stomp or a spring launch, which have their own sound or none. It also plays when Don Zopilote lands in the sand after a dive (GDD-BOS-02); a step in which he and Poncho both land plays it once.
  - **Claw:** a swish, 0.18 s, when a claw swing starts (GDD-CMB-02). A press the claw ignores (during a swing or its cooldown) is silent.
  - **Stomp:** a springy thud, 0.2 s, on every stomp (GDD-CMB-01), a Tlacuache's first stomp that makes it play dead included (GDD-ENM-02), and on every hit on Don Zopilote (GDD-BOS-02). One sound for a step, even if two enemies are stomped at once.
  - **Hilo pickup:** a plucked thread, 0.3 s, for each step in which hilos are collected (GDD-WLD-04). Its note climbs a major scale: the first hilo plays the root (G5), and each hilo collected within 0.75 s of the previous one plays the next note up, to the octave (G6, the eighth note), where it stays. A hilo after a longer pause starts again at the root. Two hilos in one step move the scale on by two and play the higher note once.
  - **Life lost:** an unravelling thread, a fast run of plucks falling in pitch, 0.85 s, when a life is lost (GDD-LIFE-02): a pit, a crush, or a hit in Base. A hit that Taco or Fuego absorbs (GDD-CMB-03) is not a lost life and does not play it.
  - **Tick (user, 2026-10-30, the sound itself Proposed):** a short wooden tick, 0.06 s, as each line of the level clear screen appears, and once when a press shows them all at once (GDD-UI-02). It plays on that screen, where Poncho is not in play.
  - **Meow (Decided, user, 2026-10-23):** a short meow, 0.5 s, when Poncho appears again after a lost life: on the first step of the respawn flip, or at the respawn itself when a skip cut the flip short, and after the restart from the *Siete vidas gastadas* screen (GDD-LIFE-05). It also plays when Don Zopilote is defeated (GDD-BOS-02, Proposed), on the step of the hit that defeats him, with that hit's stomp. The sound itself is Proposed.
- **World 2 (Proposed, not built yet):** for now an awning bounce plays the spring's sound an octave lower (GDD-WLD-10), a string plays none (GDD-WLD-11), a hit on El Tlacuachón plays the stomp and his landing the land an octave lower (GDD-BOS-03). Later, each generated in `scripts/audio/effects.mjs`, under 1 s, with its numbers in `SFX`: an awning's taut canvas *fwump* with a short rising twang (0.25 s), not the jump chirp, since a bounce is not a jump, and a soft paper rustle (0.2 s) when Poncho first touches a string. No crowd or voice ambience, matching the rule that no people are drawn (GDD-ART-06).
- Sounds start playing only after the first key press or touch, because browsers keep audio locked until then (GDD-AUD-01).

---

## 13. Art

### GDD-ART-01 Style and resolution

Status: Decided (pixel art, user, 2026-10-19; drawn in code, the 1920 x 1080 art resolution and a mood per world, user, 2026-10-25). The game units, outlines and palette rules are Proposed.

- Pixel art with a hand-made feel. **Drawn in code (Decided, user, 2026-10-25):** every sprite and tile is a pixel grid in a script that writes the image files, as the placeholder sounds are made (GDD-AUD-02), and the user approves each one from screenshots before it goes in.
- **Art resolution (Decided, user, 2026-10-25):** 1920 x 1080 (16:9), four times 480 x 270 on each side, so the sprites can hold the detail of the sprite references (GDD-ART-02). A tile is 64 x 64 art pixels. Poncho's frame is 160 x 160 art pixels, where each pixel of the user's drawing is 2 x 2 art pixels (Proposed, GDD-MOV-06).
- **Fitting the screen (Proposed, as before):** the picture is scaled to fit the screen by integer factors where possible and letterboxed on other aspect ratios.
- **Game units (Proposed):** the rules keep their numbers. Every number in this document is in game pixels unless it says art pixels, and a game pixel is 4 x 4 art pixels, so the screen stays 480 x 270 game pixels, a tile 16 and Poncho's body box 12 x 32 (GDD-MOV-06). No speed, jump, gap or level changes with the art; only the drawing works in art pixels, and things are drawn at positions rounded to whole art pixels.
- The illustration (`reference.jpg`) is smooth; the sprites follow the sprite references' shapes, proportions and colours (GDD-ART-02, GDD-ART-03) and the pixel rules (GDD-ART-05).
- A limited palette per world, drawn from real Mexican craft colours (GDD-ART-03), and a mood per world (GDD-ART-06).
- **Built (details Proposed):** the canvas is 1920 x 1080 and the camera draws the 480 x 270 game pixels 4 times larger, so the placeholder shapes and the text look as before and Poncho's sprite shows every art pixel. A tap is read in game pixels as before. Built so far: Poncho's sprite and World 1's backdrop and ground tiles (GDD-ART-06); everything else is placeholder.

### GDD-ART-02 Poncho (character)

Status: Decided (appearance from the user's reference image, 2026-10-19; the sprite references and the main pixel reference, user, 2026-10-25). Sprite-level details are Proposed.

References, all in `assets/characters/poncho/`:

- `reference.jpg`: the illustration (512 x 512, orange background), the source for Poncho's colours, face, taco and PB badge.
- **Main reference (Decided, user, 2026-10-25):** `sprite-pixel.jpg`, the user's pixel drawing of Poncho standing side-on, facing right (a 100 x 100 pixel grid, the figure 56 x 71 pixels). The sprite is this drawing, pixel for pixel, with a tail added behind him (user, 2026-10-25: "add a tail"); it wins over every other reference. It has a fringe along the poncho's hem, the furrowed brows of the illustration, a near-black outline, and the front paw held under the poncho.
- **Sprite references (Decided, user, 2026-10-25):** `sprite-side.jpg` (two side views), `sprite-back-front.jpg` (back and front), `sprite-walk-a.jpg` and `sprite-walk-b.jpg` (walk cycles of four frames), and `sprite-run.jpg` (the user's run cycle, four frames of legs, the source of the run frames, GDD-ART-04). Poncho is drawn side-on, as in these. Where they and the illustration differ, the sprite references win for the sprite (user, 2026-10-25: "side view as drawn"); the main reference wins over both, and these stay the guide for poses it does not show (the walk, the back, the front).

The look:

- **Build:** chubby chibi cat standing upright on two legs, large round head, big pointed ears with pink insides. Below the poncho the ginger legs show, with cream paws.
- **Fur:** warm tan and ginger, cream muzzle, chest and paws, darker ginger tabby marks on the forehead (illustration).
- **Face:** large dark brown eyes with white highlights, pink cheek blush, small pink nose, small smirk, slightly furrowed brows (confident, cheeky; illustration). Side-on (sprite references): one big dark eye with a white highlight, a pink blush below it and a small smile on the cream muzzle. The front view shows the eyes shut in a happy smile.
- **Sombrero:** wide brim, blue on top with a red rim and a zigzag trim; a tall, rounded red crown with a blue band. In the sprite references the ear nearer the viewer pokes up above the brim in front of the crown, and the brim tilts with the way Poncho faces.
- **Poncho:** blue, with a red V-shaped chevron at the neck and a red border at the hem, both dotted white, and small white triangles in the blue field. The illustration also has a cream fringe and a darker navy layer behind (cape or back panel); the sprite references show neither, so the sprite leaves them out (Proposed reading of "side view as drawn").
- **Tail (from the sprite references):** long and ginger, curving up behind the body, with a white tip.
- **Back (from the sprite references):** the crown and brim cover the head and the ears poke up behind the crown; the back of the poncho has the same chevron and triangles.
- **Held item:** a taco with lettuce, tomato and meat in one hand (illustration). This becomes the Taco state (GDD-PWR-01); the sprite references, in Base, hold nothing.
- **Glow:** the illustration has a golden rim light. It is reserved as the invulnerability effect (GDD-CMB-03, GDD-PWR-04).
- **PB mark (Decided, user, 2026-10-19):** the small gold "PB" badge on the chest and the "PB" mark in the bottom-right corner of the reference stand for **Poncho on Base** (GDD-VIS-05). Keep the badge on the chest, as the front view shows it, and on the side view where the frame allows. The corner mark is a brand watermark and is not part of the character.
- **Personality (Decided):** loves tacos and spicy food.
- **Pronouns (Decided, user, 2026-10-25):** he.

- Each material gets three shades: base, shadow and highlight (GDD-ART-05). Poncho's palette is fixed across all worlds, at most 16 colours, the Taco and Fuego states included.
