Poncho must stay readable against every world background: the outline and the poncho's blue and red do that against the warm desert.

### GDD-ART-04 Poncho animation set

Status: Proposed

Idle (blink, glances at the taco in Taco state), run, skid, jump up, apex, fall, land, claw swipe, fireball spit (Fuego), hurt (sombrero tips off, taco bitten), respawn flip, eating a taco or chile, victory (taco in the air), wall cling (post-MVP), glide (post-MVP). Exact frame counts decided with the final sprite sheet.

The run cycle is the user's run drawing (`sprite-run.jpg`, GDD-ART-02), side-on, the tail swinging behind; the four-frame walks of the sprite references (`sprite-walk-a.jpg`, `sprite-walk-b.jpg`) stay the guide for a walk.

- **Built: the standing frame and the run cycle** (user, 2026-10-25), in `assets/sprites/poncho.png`, five frames of 160 x 160 art pixels made by `scripts/art/make-sprites.mjs` from the user's drawing and run cycle (`sprite-pixel.jpg`, `sprite-run.jpg`; GDD-ART-02). The four run frames are the user's run legs under the standing drawing's body, with the tail's tip swaying.
- **When they show (Proposed):** on the ground and moving at 10 px/s or more, the run frames play in order, one frame for every 8 px he travels, so the feet keep pace with the ground; the cycle starts at its first frame each time he starts to run. Otherwise, standing still, in the air, gliding, clinging to a wall and in the respawn flip, he shows the standing frame until those frames are drawn. He faces the way he last moved (the drawing faces right; facing left is its mirror image). `ANIM` in `src/config/tuning.ts`, `src/ui/ponchoAnim.ts`.
- **Where (Proposed):** the frame's bottom row is level with his feet and the frame is centred on his body box; the taco and chile he holds, the glide bar and the claw marks keep their placeholder places by the body box.

### GDD-ART-05 Pixel rules

Status: Proposed

- **Outline:** every character, enemy, item and pickup has a near-black outline (#070509, as in the main reference, GDD-ART-02, which replaces the dark plum first proposed), 2 art pixels thick on the outside (one pixel of the user's drawing). Tiles and scenery have no outline, so what can be touched stands out.
- **Light** comes from the upper left. Each material has three shades: base, a shadow on the lower right and a highlight on the upper left. No soft gradients, no blurred or blended edges, no anti-aliasing.
- **Dither** (a checker of two colours) only in skies and far scenery, never on characters or pickups.
- **Three layers:** far scenery (sky, mountains, clouds; low contrast, scrolls slower than the level), the play area (tiles, mid contrast) and characters and pickups (highest contrast, outlined).
- **Hazards** have a pale edge or tip (cream on a spike's points, a scorpion's sting): this is how the high-contrast hazard outlines of GDD-UI-03 are drawn, so danger never rests on colour alone. Hazard tiles get this edge although other tiles have no outline.
- **Silhouette first:** Poncho must be told apart by shape alone, the wide brim and tall crown above a poncho and a tail.
- Sprites are drawn facing right and flipped for left.

### GDD-ART-06 World moods

Status: Decided (a mood per world, darker towards Mictlán, user, 2026-10-25; World 1's blend, user, 2026-10-25). The other worlds' moods are Proposed.

| # | World | Mood |
| --- | --- | --- |
| 1 | Desierto | **Decided (user, 2026-10-25): a blend.** The scenery of the user's desert scene (`assets/worlds/desierto/reference-scene.jpg`): blue and purple mountains far away, clouds, the cacti and plants of the Chihuahuan desert in place of the scene's saguaros (user, 2026-10-25: keep the Chihuahuan desert and swap in cacti that grow there): prickly pears (nopal), cholla, barrel cactus (biznaga), with palm yucca and ocotillo as the tall shapes (the species are Proposed), agave (lechuguilla) and red and orange flowers in front, a strip of grass along the sandy path, red earth below. Under a warmer golden sky than in that scene, with a bright sun. |
| 2 | Mercado | Early afternoon, still bright: pale blue sky, pink and terracotta walls in the first long shadows, papel picado strings in pink, gold, teal and blue. |
| 3 | Sierra | Cool afternoon: grey-blue sky, green-grey peaks, ochre rock. |
| 4 | Selva | Golden hour in the jungle: amber sky, deep greens, mossy ruins. |
| 5 | Cenote | Underground: dark teal, shafts of light from above, turquoise water. |
| 6 | Volcán | Dusk: red and black sky, a glowing orange crater. |
| 7 | Mictlán | Night: deep purple, a pale moon, marigold paths and candle light. |

Each world is a little darker than the one before, so the trip from World 1 to 7 goes from midday through the afternoon and dusk to night (the Cenote, underground, sits between golden hour and dusk). Proposed: the HUD and Poncho's palette stay the same in every world.

- **World 1's backdrop and ground (built, details Proposed; user, 2026-10-26: the backdrop and the ground first, from the desert scene alone; the look approved from screenshots the same day).** The levels of World 0 and World 1 (0-1, GDD-WLD-06, 1-1, GDD-WLD-07, 1-2, GDD-WLD-08, 1-3, GDD-WLD-09, the boss arena 1-4, GDD-BOS-02, and the test levels, the sandbox and the slopes level, which count as World 1) are drawn in the desert look; the art of hazards, enemies, pickups and moving things comes later and keeps its placeholder look until then (Don Zopilote, his cactus fruit and the arena's rocks too, GDD-BOS-02; the plants are below). Drawn in code like Poncho's sprite (GDD-ART-01) by `scripts/art/make-desert.mjs` into `assets/sprites/desierto/`, at 4 x 4 art pixels per game pixel, with no outline on any of it (GDD-ART-05).
  - **Four far layers behind the level**, back to front (the fourth, the far desert strip in front of the mountains, is described under the plants below), each filling the screen's width and repeating sideways without a seam, never moving up or down:
    - **Sky:** fixed to the screen. Four horizontal bands of gold, from a deep amber at the top to a pale gold at the horizon, with a two-colour checker dither, 2 art pixels tall, between each pair of bands (GDD-ART-05: dither only in skies and far scenery). The **sun** is a round pale disc 24 game px across with a gold ring and short pixel rays, its centre at x = 104, y = 36 on the screen, fixed with the sky.
    - **Clouds:** a few flat-bottomed clouds, cream on top and a warm shade underneath, between y = 16 and y = 88, scrolling at 0.1 of the camera's speed.
    - **Mountains:** two ranges of peaks between y = 92 and the screen's bottom, the far one purple, the near one blue with rust-red light on the slopes facing the upper left (GDD-ART-05) and a darker blue in shadow; below the peaks the blue fills down to the screen's bottom, so a pit shows mountains and not a hole. They scroll at 0.3 of the camera's speed.
    - A layer that scrolls at a share of the camera's speed is drawn at that share of the camera's scroll, rounded to a whole art pixel (GDD-UI-04).
  - **Ground tiles:** every solid tile of the level is a ground tile 64 x 64 art pixels. A tile with no solid tile and no sand slope directly above it (or at the level's top row) is a **top tile**: a strip of green grass along its top with a ragged lower edge and a few tufts, then pale sand, then red earth. Any other solid tile, but one under a sand slope (see Slope tiles), is an **earth tile**: red earth with darker specks. Each kind has two versions, so the ground does not look stamped: the version is (7 x column + 3 x row) mod 2, which alternates like a checkerboard. Light from the upper left: each grass tuft has its light green on the upper left and its shade on the right and below, and each sand speck has a pale cell on its left. The crumbling tiles, doors, fragile walls, piñatas, springs and the rest keep their own placeholder look.
  - **Slope tiles (built, details Proposed; user, 2026-11-02, "Extend the desert tiles": "The same approved sand and rock, cut along the slope by the same script, shown to you in screenshots before it lands."; the look approved from screenshots the same day: "Approved"):** a sand slope (GDD-MOV-09) and the tile under it are drawn as one 16 x 32 game px strip: the top tile over the earth tile of the same version, each one game px column moved down until its top cell is the cell the slope's surface passes through at the column's middle, then cut back into the two tiles. Above the surface the slope tile is empty, so the backdrop shows. So the grass strip, the sand and the red earth run along the slope at the top tile's thickness, in whole game pixels; at a slope's high end they meet the flat top tile beside it without a seam (checked by `scripts/art/desert.test.mjs`), and at its foot the surface starts one game px above the flat ground's. The tile under a slope takes the slope's version, so its part of the strip lines up. The ground sheet has 28 frames: the four tiles as before (frames 0 to 3, unchanged), then the six slopes in the order `/ \ r R F f`, two versions each (frame 4 + 2 x the slope's place in that order + the version), then the six tiles under them in the same order (frame 16 + 2 x place + the slope's version). Drawn by `scripts/art/make-desert.mjs` like the rest; `src/ui/groundTiles.ts`.
  - **Palette (20 colours, within GDD-ART-03's 24 for a world):** sky #E39A48, #EDB15A, #F4C66E, #F9DA8E; sun #FFF6D0, #FFD25A; clouds #FFF4DC, #E9B98A; mountains #8E6A96 (far purple), #A65B5B (rust light), #4D5A9C (blue), #3A4580 (blue shadow); grass #9CC24E, #6FA03A, #4E7A2C; sand #F4D29C, #E8B87A, #D19A5E; earth #A0472F, #7A3326. The four left are the plants' (below). `BACKDROP` in `src/config/tuning.ts`, `src/ui/backdrop.ts`, `src/ui/groundTiles.ts`.
- **World 1's plants (built, details Proposed; user, 2026-10-26: drawn from the desert scene, the species it lacks from how they look, with a far plant layer too; the look approved from screenshots, 2026-10-27).** Scenery only: nothing touches them, they never hide a thing Poncho can touch, and they keep the backdrop's rules (drawn in code by `scripts/art/make-desert.mjs`, no outline, blocky at whole game pixels, light from the upper left).
  - **Seven plants** (sizes in game px, width x height): nopal (prickly pear, round pads with a few red fruit; 16 x 16), cholla (a short branching cactus with pale spines; 12 x 20), biznaga (a barrel cactus, ribbed, with a crown of orange flowers; 10 x 10), palm yucca (a brown trunk with a spiky ball of leaves; 14 x 36), ocotillo (thin brown stems fanning up from one point, red tips; 16 x 40), agave (a blue-green rosette of pointed leaves; 16 x 12) and a flower clump (red and orange flowers on green stems; 12 x 8). No saguaros (GDD-WLD-02), and none has the cactus buddy's look of a short column with two raised arms (GDD-ENM-02), so scenery is never mistaken for an enemy.
  - **Where they grow:** on top tiles of the ground (GDD-ART-06 above), rooted at the tile's top in the middle of the tile, behind the ground tiles' front edge and behind everything else in the level (in front of the backdrop). A top tile (a level tile with no level tile and no sand slope directly above it, GDD-MOV-09) at (column, row) gets a plant when (37 x column + 11 x row) mod 9 is 0 or 1 (two top tiles in nine); its kind is the seven in the order above, chosen by (3 x column + 5 x row) mod 7, so neighbouring plants differ. 0-1 gets 12 plants of all seven kinds, the sandbox 4, the slopes level 1, a nopal at column 0, 1-2 11 of five kinds (no biznaga and no flower clump), none on a sand slope or on the tile under one, 1-3 16 of six kinds (no palm yucca), none on a sand slope or on the tile under one and none in a moving block's column, and the boss arena 1-4 gets 4, on the floor at columns 8, 9, 17 and 18 (a palm yucca, a flower clump, a biznaga and an agave), none on a pillar top, where the rocks rest (checked by `src/ui/plants.test.ts`). A top tile gets none when the tiles above it, up to the plant's height, are not all empty: a level tile, a fragile wall, a crumbling tile, a piñata or a door there stops it (so a plant never pokes into a wall or a platform, even one that breaks later), or when any of these stands within 2 columns of it on the same row or the row above: the spawn, an ofrenda, the exit, a spike, a spring, a door, a key, a piñata, a taco, the veladora, or an enemy's start. That keeps the spots that teach or warn clear, and keeps cacti away from the spikes and the cactus buddy. `src/ui/plants.ts`.
  - **Far plants:** a strip of far desert between the mountains and the level, from y = 180 to y = 224 on the screen, scrolling at 0.5 of the camera's speed like the other far layers and repeating sideways without a seam. It is sand in its two darker shades with a ragged top, dotted with small plants 4 to 10 px tall in the plants' shapes and colours, drawn flat (two shades each). Below y = 224, the floor's top in every level so far, the mountains' blue foot still shows, so a pit still reads as a hole.
  - **The palette's last four colours:** #D8473A (red: flowers, fruit, ocotillo tips), #F08A3C (orange: flowers), #6F9C8E (agave and yucca blue-green), #2F5A3A (deep green: the cacti's shade). The plants and the far strip use only the 24 colours of this palette. `BACKDROP.desertFactor` (0.5), `BACKDROP.desertTop` (180) in `src/config/tuning.ts`.
- **World 2's backdrop and ground (Proposed, not built yet; user, 2026-11-04: "Draft from GDD mood": there is no reference image, so it is drawn in code from the mood line above and approved from screenshots; Open Question 32).** The levels of World 2 (2-1, GDD-WLD-12, 2-2, GDD-WLD-13, 2-3, GDD-WLD-14, and the boss arena 2-4, GDD-BOS-03) are drawn in the market look; a level's world picks its backdrop, ground sheet and scenery. Hazards, enemies, pickups and moving things keep their placeholder look until their own art comes, as in World 1. Drawn by `scripts/art/make-mercado.mjs` into `assets/sprites/mercado/` from the drawing functions in `scripts/art/mercado.mjs` (reusing `png.mjs` and `raster.mjs`), at 4 x 4 art pixels per game pixel, with no outline (GDD-ART-05) and light from the upper left; `scripts/art/mercado.test.mjs` checks that the committed images match the drawing and use only the 24 colours below, and `assets/worlds/mercado/README.md` records that the look comes from the mood text, with no reference image.
  - **Four far layers behind the level**, back to front, each filling the screen's width, repeating sideways without a seam, never moving up or down, drawn at its share of the camera's scroll rounded to a whole art pixel (GDD-UI-04), with World 1's scroll factors:
    - **Sky:** fixed to the screen. Four horizontal bands of pale blue, from a clear mid blue at the top to an almost white blue at the horizon, with a two-colour checker dither 2 art pixels tall between each pair. No sun disc: the early afternoon sun is off the screen to the upper left, where the light comes from.
    - **Clouds:** three or four small flat-bottomed fair-weather clouds between y = 16 and y = 72, cream on top and blue-grey underneath, scrolling at 0.1 of the camera's speed; fewer and smaller than the desert's, so the sky reads clear.
    - **Far town:** a hazy hill-town skyline in two flat shades of lilac blue, with flat roofs, water tanks and one generic church with two bell towers and a tiled dome and no large religious symbols; its tops between y = 100 and y = 150, the haze filling down to the screen's bottom, so a pit shows haze and not a hole. It scrolls at 0.3 of the camera's speed; dither is allowed here.
    - **Near houses:** a row of two-storey house and shop fronts from y = 150 to y = 224, scrolling at 0.5: pink and terracotta plaster walls, arched doorways, small iron balconies with hanging clay pots, and painted sign bands in the style of hand-painted *rótulos*, blocks of colour suggesting lettering with no readable words. The first long shadows: each front's right-hand return is in shadow, and under every eave and balcony a shadow band 3 game px deep slants down to the right, 1 px down for each 1 px across. Decorative papel picado hangs between the roofs, a cord 1 game px thick with flags 4 x 5 game px, solid, with no cut-outs, in pink, gold, teal and blue in turn, static. Star piñatas for sale (five or seven points) hang from a few balconies. Below y = 224 the far town's haze still shows.
  - **Rules that keep the signature readable:** papel picado in the level layer is always something Poncho can use, a string (GDD-WLD-11) or a fragile wall (GDD-WLD-05); decorative papel picado appears only in the near-houses layer, smaller, without cut-outs, moving with the parallax. Awnings appear only as bouncy awnings (GDD-WLD-10): no backdrop layer and no scenery piece has an awning or canopy, so any awning a child sees bounces. Piñatas for sale appear only in the backdrop; at level depth a piñata is always a piñata block (GDD-PWR-05). No people are drawn: the goods, the strings and the signs show the market is busy, which avoids caricature (GDD-VIS-04). No stall or backdrop shows pigeons, dogs, wasps, opossums or lucha masks (GDD-ENM-01, World 2).
  - **Ground tiles:** every solid tile is a ground tile 64 x 64 art pixels, top tile and body tile by World 1's rule. A top tile is a roof edge, from top to bottom: a row of curved terracotta roof tiles (*tejas*) 5 game px tall, with a highlight on the upper left of each curve and a shadow on its right; a 1 game px shadow line; a talavera trim 3 game px tall, four cream squares across the tile with a cobalt motif; warm terracotta plaster to the tile's bottom. A body tile is that plaster with a few hairline cracks. Two versions each, by World 1's rule ((7 x column + 3 x row) mod 2): the top tile's motif a cross or a four-petal flower; the body tile plain with cracks, or with a patch where the plaster has fallen away showing three courses of terracotta brick with dark mortar. The sheet `ground.png` has 4 frames (top v0, top v1, body v0, body v1). World 2 has no sand slopes. The pink walls appear only in the near-houses layer, never in the ground tiles, so the pink fragile walls never blend into the ground.
  - **Scenery (World 1's plant rules: nothing touches it, it never hides a thing Poncho can touch, no outline, blocky at whole game pixels, light from the upper left):** six pieces (game px, width x height): fruit crates (*huacales*) heaped with oranges and limes, 16 x 12; a tin bucket of pink and gold cut flowers, 10 x 14; stacked clay pots (*ollas de barro*), 14 x 12; a stack of woven baskets, 14 x 10; a painted clay pot of pink geraniums, 10 x 12; a wooden chair with a woven rush seat (*silla de tule*), 10 x 16. None has a flat top wider than 6 px, so none reads as a platform, and none shares a silhouette with a World 2 enemy (GDD-ENM-03). Marigolds (*cempasúchil*) are kept for Mictlán. They stand on top tiles by World 1's placement rule: a top tile gets one when (37 x column + 11 x row) mod 9 is 0 or 1, its kind (3 x column + 5 x row) mod 6 in the order above, with World 1's keep-outs, and also none within 2 columns of a bouncy awning (on, under or beside it) and none in the column under either end of a string, so the new signature reads alone. The Avispas' sweets stall is part of their own drawing, not scenery (GDD-ENM-03). `src/ui/scenery.ts` (World 1's `plants.ts` generalised by world) and `src/ui/scenery.test.ts`, which gives each level's count and kinds once the levels exist.
  - **The signature pieces' final look (their rules and placeholders are GDD-WLD-10 and GDD-WLD-11):** an awning is canvas in stripes 4 game px wide of teal and cream with a scalloped front edge and its underside in shadow terracotta, with no poles, squashing 2 px for 3 steps on each bounce; every awning has the same colours. A string is a dark cord with flags 8 x 10 game px in pink, gold, teal and blue, each with a cut-out pattern (holes 1 game px wide showing the backdrop) and a zig-zag lower edge.
  - **Palette (24 colours, GDD-ART-03's limit for a world):** sky #6FA8D8, #8DBDE3, #AED3EE, #CDE5F5; cloud underside #D6E2EE; far town haze #B9C3E0, #96A3CF; pink plaster light, base and long shadow #F4B9B4, #E0919A, #A86A86; terracotta light, base and shadow (roofs, plaster, brick, pots, oranges) #E8A070, #C9643E, #8C3F33; talavera cobalt #23428F; cream (talavera, cloud tops, awning stripes) #F5EFE2; papel picado pink, gold, teal and blue (gold also the fruit and flowers, teal also the awnings) #EE5FA0, #F2BE2E, #22A99B, #5C9EE6; wood and wood shade #A8703F, #6B4330; green and deep green (limes, leaves, stems) #5E9E4C, #2E6A44; deep shade (doorways, window holes, gaps in crates, the string's cord) #4A2E3A. The papel picado blue is lighter than Poncho's poncho blue (#3F66B1) and the cobalt darker, so his blue and red still stand out against the pale sky. `BACKDROP_ART.mercado` in `src/config/tuning.ts`, beside World 1's, the scroll factors shared, when built.

### GDD-ART-07 UI look

Status: Proposed

- **Font:** a blocky pixel font drawn in code like the art (GDD-ART-01), cream text (#F1F3E3) with a dark plum outline, so it reads on any background. Gameplay text stays readable at the sizes GDD-UI-01 and GDD-UI-02 give.
- **Serape band:** menus, the title screen and the pause screen are framed by a woven band in Poncho's blue, red and cream triangles.
- **Lives triangles** (GDD-UI-01): filled in Poncho's red with a cream edge; a lost one is only the cream edge.
- **Title:** "PONCHO" in large cream letters with a red shadow, "Siete Vidas" in gold, Poncho beside it. The layout, sizes and menus stay those of GDD-UI-01 and GDD-UI-02; only the look changes.
- **Not built yet:** the HUD, menus and title still use the placeholder look described in GDD-UI-01 and GDD-UI-02 (plain text, outlined triangles), until the art switch (GDD-ART-01).

---

## 14. Technical

### GDD-TEC-01 Stack

Status: Decided (user, 2026-10-19: state of the art, latest releases). See `docs/decisions/0002-state-of-the-art-stack.md`.

Phaser 4, TypeScript 7 (native compiler, strict), Vite 8, Vitest 5 for unit tests, Playwright for end-to-end playtests, Oxlint with type-aware rules for linting, Node 24 LTS in CI. Exact versions are in the ADR and `package-lock.json`. The first decision (Phaser 3) is superseded.

### GDD-TEC-02 Deterministic simulation

Status: Proposed

- Fixed timestep of 60 Hz for gameplay logic; rendering interpolates.
- Simulation code (movement, lives, damage, collision) has no Phaser imports and no `Math.random()` without a seeded source, so it is unit-testable and replayable.
- Scene code only wires input, rendering and audio to the simulation.

### GDD-TEC-03 Performance and mobile targets

Status: Proposed

- 60 fps on a mid-range phone from the last 4 years, 30 fps floor on low end with automatic quality fallback.
- Initial download under 5 MB for the first level; later assets lazy-loaded. The 1920 x 1080 art (GDD-ART-01) is drawn in code and saved as images: the first world's images must fit this budget too.
- Works offline once loaded (service worker, PWA installable).
- Handles device pixel ratio, safe areas, orientation change and tab suspension.

### GDD-TEC-04 Level authoring

Status: Proposed

Levels are authored in Tiled and exported as JSON (tilemap and object layers: spawn, ofrendas, hilos, enemies, hazards, exit). A validator test loads every level and checks: exactly one spawn, 3 ofrendas, an exit (a boss level: neither, GDD-BOS-01), no gap wider than the world's limit (GDD-WLD-03).

- The validator is built (`src/levels/validate.ts`) and a test runs it over every level in `src/levels/index.ts`: 0-1 (GDD-WLD-06), 1-1 (GDD-WLD-07), 1-2 (GDD-WLD-08), 1-3 (GDD-WLD-09), the boss arena 1-4 (GDD-BOS-02), the sandbox and the slopes level (GDD-MOV-09). The Tiled JSON loader is not built yet: levels are still written in code, and the loader will feed the same validator (user decision, 2026-10-23). One spawn is guaranteed by the level data, which has exactly one; the loader will count spawn objects.
- It checks 3 ofrendas, an exit, a length of 3 to 6 screens (GDD-WLD-03), no gap wider than the world's limit, and no enemy starting within 3 tiles of the spawn or an ofrenda (GDD-ENM-01). It also checks that every sand slope tile has a level tile directly under it, and holds no spike, spring, crumbling tile, door, fragile wall or piñata (GDD-MOV-09). A world with no gap limit in GDD-WLD-03 is itself a problem; only World 1 and the tutorial (World 0, with World 1's 3 tiles) have one so far (World 2's 4 tiles are Proposed, below).
- A gap is a run of adjacent pit columns, and a pit column is one whose bottom row is empty, where Poncho falls out of the level. A slope tile never sits in the bottom row (it needs a level tile under it), so a slope beside a gap does not change its width. A floating tile higher up does not fill a pit (user decision, 2026-10-23), and a column whose bottom row alone is empty counts, on the safe side.
- A level marked as a test level (the sandbox, the slopes level) skips the exit and length checks and gets the others (user decision, 2026-10-23), the slope checks included (GDD-MOV-09). The sandbox's pit, columns 14 to 16, is a gap of 3 tiles.
- A level with a boss (the arena 1-4, GDD-BOS-02) is a boss level, held to a rule of its own (built, Proposed): instead of 3 ofrendas, an exit and 3 to 6 screens, it must have no ofrendas and no exit and be exactly one screen (30 tiles) wide, and the boss's starting point must be at least 3 tiles from the spawn, the arena's only respawn (GDD-BOS-01, GDD-ENM-01). It keeps the other checks every level gets: no gap wider than the world's limit and no enemy starting within 3 tiles of the spawn (checked by `src/levels/validate.test.ts` with a boss level that breaks each), and the sand slope checks (GDD-MOV-09).
- **World 2 (Proposed, not built yet):** World 2's levels are written in code like the others, as level arrays: `awnings` as `{ col, row, width }` with width 2 to 4, and `strings` as `{ col, row, width }` with width 2 to 8, beside the spawn, ofrendas, hilos, enemies, hazards and exit. `LEVEL_RULES.maxGapTiles` gains World 2's 4 tiles (GDD-WLD-03), so World 0, World 1 and World 2 have a limit. The validator runs over 2-1 to 2-4, the boss-level rule on 2-4, and adds:
  - an awning's or a string's tiles never overlap a level tile or another thing, and the tiles of its row under it are empty and hold nothing;
  - no awning column is a pit column (its bottom row holds a level tile), and nothing solid lies within an awning's high-bounce height above it (target: that height, measured);
  - each string's two anchor tiles, (col − 1, row) and (col + width, row), are level tiles, and a string spans no gap wider than the world's limit;
  - no spawn, ofrenda, exit or enemy start stands on an awning or a string; the 3-tile respawn check uses the Paloma's perch and the Avispas' home point (GDD-ENM-03);
  - no real level (0-1 or a world's level) sets `abilities`: unlocks come from the save (GDD-PRG-01), and only test levels turn an ability on themselves;
  - each level's id begins with its world's number, and every real level but the game's last names as `next` the level after it in `LEVELS` order. The rule that a world has exactly three levels and one boss level, its boss level last, and that a world's last level names the next world's first, applies only to a complete world, so World 2 can be built one level at a time with the gate green (1-4's `next` changes in the PR that completes World 2, GDD-WLD-01);
  - El Tlacuachón's arena: every free stretch of floor fits his box plus `awningClear` (GDD-BOS-03).

### GDD-TEC-05 Tuning in one place

Status: Proposed

All gameplay numbers in this document live in a single typed file, `src/config/tuning.ts`, each constant commented with its GDD ID. A unit test simulates a full jump and asserts the derived values stated in section 4: peak 56 px within 1.5 px, and horizontal distance 66 px at full run within one step of travel (2 px).

### GDD-TEC-07 Dependency policy

Status: Decided (use the latest stable releases, user, 2026-10-19). The review cadence and the holdback rule are Proposed.

- Track the latest stable release of every dependency. No pre-release versions in `main`.
- At every GDD minor version bump, run `npm outdated`, upgrade, run the full gate and the playtests, and record the result in the changelog or an ADR.
- Anything that must stay behind (a blocker like a peer-dependency conflict) is written down in an ADR with the condition for upgrading.
- Phaser APIs marked deprecated (the Canvas renderer, custom pipelines) are not used.

### GDD-TEC-06 Testing strategy

Status: Proposed

- Unit tests (Vitest) for simulation: movement numbers, forgiveness windows, lives rules, save migration.
- Level validation tests (GDD-TEC-04): every level in `src/levels/index.ts` passes the validator.
- Playwright playtests per the `harness-playtest` skill: boot, jump, life loss, touch input, console free of errors.
- `scripts/check.mjs` runs all of these through npm scripts once they exist.

---

## 15. Scope

### GDD-MVP-01 Vertical slice

Status: Proposed

The first playable milestone:

- Poncho with run, jump (GDD-MOV-01 to GDD-MOV-06), stomp and claw (GDD-CMB-01 to GDD-CMB-03).
- Seven-lives system complete (GDD-LIFE-01 to GDD-LIFE-05) and HUD (GDD-UI-01).
- Power-ups: Taco, Chile and Fuego, piñatas (GDD-PWR-01, GDD-PWR-02, GDD-PWR-03, GDD-PWR-05, GDD-PWR-06).
- World 1 with 3 levels and the boss, bestiary of GDD-ENM-02.
- Keyboard and touch controls (GDD-CTL-01, GDD-CTL-03), including the Fuego button; the gamepad (GDD-CTL-02) is built ahead of it.
- Autosave (GDD-PRG-02), pause, settings with volume and language.
- Placeholder music and SFX are acceptable.

Done means: the `harness-playtest` skill passes on desktop and a phone viewport, and the user has played it.

### GDD-MVP-02 Later scope

Status: Proposed

Worlds 2 to 7, Chile Fantasma (GDD-PWR-04), glide, wall cling, further powers, score, cosmetics shop, full soundtrack, localisation beyond English, Spanish and German, native packaging (new ADR).

The glide and the wall cling are built early but stay off in real levels; only the sandbox turns them on (GDD-MOV-07, GDD-MOV-08). Proposed with World 2, not built yet: the glide is on in real levels once 2-4 is cleared (GDD-PRG-01), and the wall cling stays off until World 3. World 2 itself (GDD-WLD-10 to GDD-WLD-14, GDD-ENM-03, GDD-BOS-03) is designed, not built.

## Open Questions

Answer these to move items from Proposed to Decided. Until then the Proposed text stands as the working assumption.

1. **Taco as shield (GDD-PWR-01, GDD-CMB-03):** Taco and Fuego absorb hits like Mario's mushroom and flower. This makes lives less scarce. Right balance, or should power-ups be rarer or not absorb hits?
2. **Power-up set (section 6):** Taco, Chile (Fuego fireballs), Chile Fantasma (star), piñata blocks. Anything you want added, such as a different fireball style, a reserve slot, or other foods?
3. **Seven worlds (GDD-WLD-02):** are the world themes and names right? Anything you want, or want avoided? (World 1 is answered: the Chihuahuan desert, user, 2026-10-25.)
4. **Story (GDD-VIS-01):** is the curse-and-seven-lives story seed what you want, or a different premise?
5. **Pronouns (GDD-ART-02):** answered: he (user, 2026-10-25).
6. **Brand and chain scope (GDD-VIS-05):** the draft keeps the game free of wallets, tokens and on-chain features. Is that right? Should the game mention the Poncho on Base token or community anywhere (credits, links), and who owns the final brand rules? Note that app stores restrict crypto features, which affects distribution (question 11).
7. **Combat (GDD-CMB-02):** keep both claw swipe and stomp, with fireballs on top? Partly answered (user, 2026-10-21): the claw swipe is a standard mechanic that Poncho always has, not a power-up. Stomp and fireballs alongside it are still open.
8. **Difficulty (GDD-VIS-02):** is assist mode and a kid-friendly tone right, or do you want a harder game?
9. **Audio (GDD-AUD-01):** will you provide music, or should placeholders be generated and licensed assets chosen?
10. **Score (GDD-PRG-03):** keep or cut? Medals are kept (user, 2026-10-30).
11. **Distribution:** where will it be hosted (itch.io, own site, stores)? This drives PWA versus native packaging.
12. **Taco pickups in Taco (GDD-PWR-02, GDD-PWR-06):** a taco lying on the floor is left where it is when Poncho is already Taco, but a walking taco released from a piñata is used up when he touches it. Should both behave the same?
13. **Gap limits after World 1 (GDD-WLD-03, GDD-TEC-04):** only World 1 has one (3 tiles). The level validator fails a level in any other world until that world has a limit. Should the limit grow by world, up to the 5 tiles a running jump can cross? Proposed for World 2: 4 tiles (GDD-WLD-03; Open Question 28); later worlds stay open.
14. **Detail on phones (GDD-ART-01):** at 1920 x 1080, one art pixel is less than one screen pixel on most phones, so the finest detail blurs there. Fine as it is, or should the sprites keep their key details (eye, triangles) at least 2 art pixels wide?
15. **Secrets (GDD-WLD-03, GDD-WLD-06 to GDD-WLD-09):** tiles cannot hide anything yet, so the veladoras of 0-1, 1-1, 1-2 and 1-3 are off the path but in plain view (as 2-1's and 2-2's are proposed to be; 2-3 proposes a room behind a fragile wall, Open Question 33). Fine for now, or should a secret be truly hidden (a false wall, a room off the screen's top)?
16. **Short phone windows (GDD-UI-02, GDD-CTL-03):** on a landscape phone whose browser window is under 360 CSS px tall (a browser bar showing), the menu rows grow so far that the last row of the title menu and of the results menu runs off the bottom of the screen (found in the review of PR #62). Since the title menu moved up to 112 px (0.8.7) its four rows fit down to about 340 CSS px; the results menu's do not. The settings screen's six rows (0.8.10, from 62 px) fit down to about 381 CSS px; five rows from 80 px fitted down to about 341, four from 96 px to about 302. Shrink the rows there, move the menus up (into the medal line on the results screen), or accept it?
17. **What 1-2 brings back (GDD-WLD-08):** besides its new things it has one cactus buddy and two piñatas from 1-1, and no Alacrán or Zopilote; its secret veladora is reached by the spring's timed launch, which no level teaches (only the sandbox has it), or by a full jump left from the top of the 4-tile wall the plain launch throws Poncho onto. Right, or should 1-2 bring back more of 1-1's enemies, or move the veladora so that it asks for the timed launch alone?
18. **The details of World 1's run and lives (GDD-LIFE-05, GDD-UI-02, GDD-PRG-02, GDD-PRG-03):** built as decided (user, 2026-11-02: "Build it as decided", "Save the lives", "Lives at the clear", "Keep them", "Lives lost in the level", "Follow the run"), with these Proposed details. (a) The save writes World 1's run only at a clear (the lives at the exit and the next level) and when the *Siete vidas gastadas* screen comes up (0 lives and 1-1), so quitting a level, or closing the game, and picking "Start" plays it again from its start with the lives Poncho had at the last clear, the lives lost in it given back (another way: write the lives at every life lost or won, which keeps the losses but lets a player who quits after an ofrenda win its life again on every new start). (b) An address (`?level=…`) always starts with seven. (c) "Start" plays 1-1, World 1's start, always with seven, and once the run has cleared World 1's last level a new run starts there (another way: the last level cleared, the boss arena 1-4, again); with World 2 built, World 1's saved run still starts again at 1-1, but "Start" is proposed to go on to World 2 (Open Question 30). (d) A save from before the run was kept goes by the levels cleared: one with 1-1 cleared opens on 1-2 with seven. (e) Gold counts the lives lost as the lives the level started with less those at its exit, so a life won back at an ofrenda or the veladora counts against one lost (that keeps "4 lives at the exit" for a level started with seven); another way: count every life lost, won-back lives not helping. After the restart that follows the last life the count starts again from seven. (f) "Retry" on the tutorial 0-1's results menu starts it with seven, since its lives carry nowhere (another way: the lives at its clear, as "Lives at the clear" gives a World 1 level). (g) Out of lives in 1-1 the restart stays in the same scene, where a Left or Right key or a finger held through the press moves Poncho as soon as the flip ends, as before; out of lives in 1-2, 1-3 or 1-4 the restart in 1-1 starts as the next level does, where a button held through the press does nothing until it is lifted (GDD-UI-02). Right?
19. **The pillar tops in Don Zopilote's arena (GDD-BOS-02):** in 1-4 nothing of his reaches Poncho on a pillar top in any phase, except on a lip of about 5 px at each pillar's inner edge, where the dive or the drop beside that pillar clips him. Fine for World 1's boss (the lip a warning not to crowd the edge), or should the whole top be safe (his lap's ends a few px further in), or should a dive or a fruit reach the tops?
20. **His fruit's telegraph (GDD-ENM-01, GDD-BOS-02):** a cactus fruit has no 0.5 s telegraph of its own; it falls in view for 0.7 to 0.9 s before it reaches Poncho on the floor. Is the fall its telegraph, or should Don Zopilote flash or call out before each drop? (El Tlacuachón's ripples raise the same question, Open Question 27.)
21. **Hilos in the boss arena (GDD-WLD-04, GDD-UI-01, GDD-UI-02):** 1-4 has none, so the HUD counter reads "0/0" and the level clear screen "Hilos 0/0". Hide both in a boss level, or give the arena some hilos? The same holds for World 2's arena 2-4 (GDD-BOS-03).
22. **How awnings work (GDD-WLD-10):** recommended: a high bounce at 520 px/s while jump is held or buffered and a low one at 340 otherwise, cut short by releasing jump; Poncho never rests on an awning; flat awnings only. Another way: (a) one fixed bounce of about 480 that ignores input; (b) the bounce dies away over 3 bounces, after which he stands on it like a floor.
23. **What papel picado strings are (GDD-WLD-11, GDD-ENM-03):** recommended: one-way cords that sag; no launch when leaving one; no enemy perches on a cord; the fragile walls keep their pink paper look. Another way: (a) the cord gives a small "twang" boost (up to about 60 px/s) when he jumps off it; (b) Palomas may perch on a cord, which does not sag for them; (c) World 2's fragile walls are redrawn as crates; (d) grab-and-swing strings, or bridges the claw cuts.
24. **World 2's enemy roster (GDD-ENM-03, GDD-WLD-05):** recommended: the Paloma, the Chihuahua and the Avispas (wasps at a sweets stall); the Tlacuache returns in 2-3 only; no heavy enemy, no alebrije enemy and no new minor hazard; defeated World 2 enemies pop off with a puff and later run or fly home unhurt, while World 1's stay "gone". Another way: (a) bees at a flower stall instead of wasps; (b) the Guajolote, a market turkey, as a heavy enemy, which brings in the claw's stun; (c) the Alacrán back as a simple patroller in 2-1, or no World 1 enemy at all; (d) a minor hazard such as rolling fruit or crates that fall after a shake.
25. **The Chihuahua and awnings (GDD-WLD-10, GDD-ENM-03):** recommended: it never steps or hops onto an awning, so awnings bounce only Poncho and lift him out of its chase (its hop peaks about 16 px, level with 2-3's row-13 awning and below every other one; found in the review of PR #76). Another way: awnings set at the street's own height that throw it straight up, once per hop.
26. **World 2's boss (GDD-BOS-03):** recommended: El Tlacuachón as drafted: only a stomp hurts him, in any state but hurt or defeated; no strings in the arena; he plays dead at the end, mask on; phases of 1, 2 and 3 leaps with poses of 1.5, 1.0 and 0.75 s; the name the same in every language. Another way: (a) a stomp hurts him only while he poses, or a claw too while he poses; (b) a giant alebrije, or a luchador Chihuahua, as the boss (a rooster is not offered, for its cockfighting association); (c) the name "Don Tlacuache"; (d) one string over B as a high refuge in phase 3.
27. **A telegraph for El Tlacuachón's ripples (GDD-BOS-03, GDD-ENM-01; like Open Question 20):** recommended: the 0.5 s telegraph before his landing counts for its ripples. Another way: a 0.25 s dust puff where he will land, before he touches down.
28. **World 2's gap limit (GDD-WLD-03, GDD-TEC-04; Open Question 13):** recommended: 4 tiles; none in 2-1, two in 2-2, four in 2-3. Another way: (a) keep 3 tiles for all of World 2 and let the awnings carry the difficulty; (b) 4-tile pits already in 2-1.
29. **The glide unlock (GDD-PRG-01, GDD-UI-02):** recommended: on in every real level but the tutorial once `levels["2-4"].cleared` is true, with no new save field, announced by a "New power: Glide!" line on 2-4's first clear screen. Another way: (a) only in worlds after World 2; (b) a Settings toggle to switch it off in older worlds; (c) an explicit `abilities` list in the save; (d) also a one-time glide tip at the start of the next level played.
30. **What "Start" plays, the world map and old saves (GDD-UI-02, GDD-LIFE-05, GDD-PRG-02, GDD-WLD-01; Open Question 18):** this changes the user's quoted "Follow the run". Recommended: "Start" plays the run of the newest open world: 2-1 with seven once 1-4 is cleared, and 2-1 again after 2-4; a save that cleared 1-4 before World 2 existed also opens on 2-1; 2-1 starts with seven whatever 1-4 left (the Decided "7 at world start"); the world map waits for World 3. Another way: (a) Left and Right on the title's "Start" row pick the world; (b) a minimal two-node map now; (c) after 2-4, the arena again or World 1's run; (d) an old save keeps playing World 1's run until 1-4 is cleared again.
31. **Tips, a level card and names (GDD-UI-05, GDD-UI-02, GDD-WLD-12 to GDD-WLD-14):** recommended: the awning tip in 2-1 and the string tip in 2-2, each gone once done; the names "Los toldos", "Los cordeles" and "Las azoteas" kept in the GDD and the code; no level card and no "Start 2-1" row label yet. Another way: (a) a level card (the id and name, 2.0 s, with "World 2: Mercado" on a world's first level, the word for "World" translated and the names not), on every real level, World 1's needing names from the user; (b) no tips at all; (c) "El tianguis" as 2-2's name.
32. **World 2's look in the playfield (GDD-ART-06, GDD-WLD-10, GDD-WLD-05):** recommended: terracotta roof and plaster ground, pink only in the backdrop; awnings in teal and cream stripes with a scalloped edge and no poles, told apart from the teal moving platform by the stripes and scallops; no sand slopes; no sun disc; one generic church in the far town; static backdrop strings; the six scenery pieces as drafted. Another way: (a) pink plaster ground, with the fragile walls recoloured; (b) gold and cream awnings; (c) terracotta roof slopes on GDD-MOV-09's rules; (d) a pale sun in the upper left (two palette colours); (e) no church, or one modelled on a town the user names; (f) a two-frame sway for the backdrop strings; (g) a sweets tray or bougainvillea in place of one scenery piece.
33. **How hard World 2's levels are, and its music (GDD-WLD-12 to GDD-WLD-14, GDD-AUD-01):** recommended: a street floor that catches most falls, with a way back up on the same screen; 5 screens for 2-1 and 6 for 2-2 and 2-3; 2-3's secret a room behind a fragile wall, visible from the cord; the placeholder spike in the market; an *organillero* street-organ theme with marimba and guitar. Another way: (a) roofs over open pits, harder and more like World 1, and dead-end wells that cost a life; (b) 6 screens for all three; (c) 2-3's veladora in plain view like earlier levels, or a fully hidden room; (d) spikes drawn as broken clay-pot shards or nopal paddles; (e) a son jarocho or a banda theme.
